# Impeccable CLI contract

The observable behavior of every `impeccable` verb: arguments, environment,
inputs, stdout and stderr byte for byte, exit codes, files written, network.
This is the specification an alternate implementation of the scripts has to
meet; `tests/oracle/` records goldens against it and replays them.

Verb names are the binary's subcommands. Each verb was a script under
`skill/scripts/` or a `cli/bin` subcommand when this contract was recorded;
the mapping is `JS_VERBS` in `tests/oracle/lib.mjs`, and file references below
name those scripts as the source the contract was read from. Since the
launcher swap the skill invokes every verb as `{{scripts_path}}/impeccable
<verb>` and the scripts are gone from the tree; the contract stands as
written.

Quoted strings, regexes, and JSON in this document are verbatim from the
source, including any em dashes inside user-facing messages; do not "fix" them.

Sections:

1. Detect CLI and misc verbs (`detect`, `ignores`, `skills`, `concept-seed`, `serve-question`, `generate-image`)
2. Context and utility verbs (`context`, `doctor`, `pin`, `surface-brief`, `critique-storage`, `palette`, `embed-prompt`, `signals`, `detect-csp`)
3. Design hook (`hook`, `hook-before-edit`, `hooks`)
4. Live mode (`live*`)

---

## 1. Detect CLI and misc verbs

Source snapshot: repo `impeccable-second` @ main (f88b2837), 2026-08-17. Node `>=22.18.0`, package `impeccable` v3.6.0, `"type": "module"`, `bin: { impeccable: cli/bin/cli.js }`, `main`/`exports["."]`: `cli/engine/detect-antipatterns.mjs`, `exports["./browser"]`: `cli/engine/detect-antipatterns-browser.js`.

Notation: `stdout>` / `stderr>` are literal writes; `exit N`; template strings use `${}` as in the source. All regexes are quoted verbatim.

---

### PART 1 — the `impeccable` binary and the detect engine

#### `cli/bin/cli.js` -> `impeccable <command>` (root dispatcher)

- **Invoked from**: README.md "CLI" section (`npx impeccable detect src/`, `npx impeccable ignores ...`), README.npm.md Quick Start, `skill/reference/hooks.md:15` ("Manual `npx impeccable detect` scans use the same project filter config..."), `hooks.md:65` ("Run `npx impeccable detect <path>` first to see what actually fires there").
- **Args**: `args = process.argv.slice(2)`, `command = args[0]`.
- **Dispatch order (exact)**:
  1. `!command || command === '--help' || command === '-h'` → print (via `console.log`, stdout) then `exit 0`:
     ```
     Usage: impeccable <command> [options]

     Commands:
       detect [file-or-dir-or-url...]   Scan for UI anti-patterns and design quality issues
       ignores                          Manage detector ignore rules, files, and values
       help                             List all available skills and commands
       install                          Install impeccable skills into your project or global harness
       link                             Symlink skills from a local checkout or submodule
       update                           Update skills to the latest version
       check                            Check if skill updates are available

     Options:
       --help       Show this help message
       --version    Show version number

     Compatibility:
       impeccable skills <command>       Legacy namespace; still supported.
     ```
  2. `--version` / `-v` → `console.log(pkg.version)` read from `<cli/bin>/../../package.json`; `exit 0`. The binary run directly (the skill launcher's path) prints the installed skill's `SKILL.md` frontmatter version instead (skill dir from `IMPECCABLE_SKILL_DIR`, else the binary's path), else `<engine version>+engine`.
  3. `detect` → `process.argv = [argv0, argv1, ...args.slice(1)]`; dynamic-import `../engine/detect-antipatterns.mjs`, `await detectCli()`.
  4. `ignores` or `ignore` → `./commands/ignores.mjs` `run(args.slice(1))`.
  5. `skills` → `./commands/skills.mjs` `run(args.slice(1))` (legacy namespace).
  6. `command ∈ SKILL_COMMANDS = {'help','install','link','update','check'}` → `skills.mjs run(args)` (note: whole `args`, so `run` sees the verb as `args[0]`).
  7. `looksLikeDetectTarget(command)` → detect shorthand: `process.argv = [argv0, argv1, ...args]` then `detectCli()`. Predicate: `arg.startsWith('-') || /^https?:\/\//i.test(arg) || arg.includes('/') || arg.includes('\\') || arg.includes('.') || existsSync(resolve(arg))`.
  8. `init` → `console.error('"init" is not a CLI command. Type /impeccable init in your AI coding agent\'s chat (Claude Code, Cursor, Codex, ...), not in this terminal.')`, `exit 1`. (Note: a real path literally named `init` hits rule 7 first — tested: "a real path named init still routes to detect".)
  9. otherwise → `console.error(\`Unknown command: "${command}"\n\nTo see a list of supported commands, run:\n  impeccable --help\`)`, `exit 1`.
- **Top-level catch**: `main().catch(error => { if (error?.code === 'IMPECCABLE_PROMPT_ABORT') { console.log('\nAborted.'); exit 130 } console.error(error?.message || error); exit 1 })`.
- **No `live` subcommand exists in the CLI** (README/CLAUDE.md mentions of `npx impeccable live` are aspirational; live mode is driven by `skill/scripts/live*.mjs`, out of scope here).
- **Tests**: `tests/skills-cli.test.js` ("root help advertises top-level skills commands", "top-level install aliases the legacy skills install command", "#472" init tests, "a real path named init still routes to detect").

---

#### `cli/engine/detect-antipatterns.mjs` (facade) and `skill/scripts/detect.mjs` (wrapper) -> `impeccable detect`

- **Facade**: re-exports registry (`ANTIPATTERNS`, `RULE_ENGINE_SUPPORT`, `getAntipattern`, `getRulesForCategory`, `getRuleEngineSupport`), constants (`SAFE_TAGS`, `BORDER_SAFE_TAGS`, `OVERUSED_FONTS`, `GENERIC_FONTS`, `KNOWN_SERIF_FONTS`), color helpers, `isFullPage`, check fns, `createDetectorProfile`, `summarizeDetectorProfile`, design-system fns (`parseFrontmatter as parseDesignFrontmatter`, `normalizeDesignSystem`, `loadDesignSystemForCwd`, `checkSourceDesignSystem`, `collectStaticDesignSystemFindings`), `detectHtml`, `detectUrl`, `createBrowserDetector`, `detectText`, `extractStyleBlocks`, `extractCSSinJS`, fs helpers (`walkDir`, `hasScannableExtension`, `SCANNABLE_EXTENSIONS`, `SKIP_DIRS`, `buildImportGraph`, `resolveImport`, `detectFrameworkConfig`, `isPortListening`, `FRAMEWORK_CONFIGS`), `formatFindings`, `detectCli`. Main-module guard: `process.argv[1]?.endsWith('detect-antipatterns.mjs') || endsWith('detect-antipatterns.mjs/')` → `detectCli()`.
- **`skill/scripts/detect.mjs`** (what the skill invokes as `node {{scripts_path}}/detect.mjs ...`): candidates in order `<scripts>/detector/detect-antipatterns.mjs` (bundled install layout), then `<scripts>/../../cli/engine/detect-antipatterns.mjs` (repo layout). If neither exists: `stderr> Error: bundled detector not found.\n`, `exit 1`. Else dynamic-imports via `pathToFileURL` and `await detectCli()`. `detectCli` strips a leading `detect` arg itself, so both `detect.mjs --json x` and `detect.mjs detect --json x` work.
- **Skill call sites (quoted)**:
  - `reference/routing.md:16`: "**If `scan.targets` is non-empty and `setup.platform` is not `ios`/`android`/`adaptive`, run `node {{scripts_path}}/detect.mjs --json <scan.targets joined by spaces>` once** (the bundled detector over local files: no network, no npx; it reads HTML/CSS, so skip it for native projects)."
  - `reference/critique.md:73`: `node {{scripts_path}}/detect.mjs --json [target]` with "Pass markup files/directories as `[target]`; do not pass CSS-only files. For URLs, skip CLI scan and use browser visualization. ... Exit code 0 = clean; 2 = findings. If the detector entrypoint is missing or fails to load, report deterministic scan unavailable and continue".
  - `reference/layout.md:28`: `node {{scripts_path}}/detect.mjs --json --scope layout [target files or dirs]`; `reference/typeset.md:27`: `... --scope type ...`.
  - `reference/audit.native.md:3`: "no browser tooling or `detect.mjs` applies" for native.

#### `detectCli()` — `cli/engine/cli/main.mjs`

**Arg normalization**: `args = process.argv.slice(2)` with `-json`→`--json`, `-fast`→`--fast`; if `args[0] === 'detect'` drop it.

**Flags** (all detected with `args.includes` unless stated):

| Flag | Effect |
|---|---|
| `--json` | JSON output to **stdout** |
| `--quiet` | text mode prints only summary line(s) to stderr |
| `--github` | GitHub Actions workflow commands to **stdout**, one per finding (`::warning` / `::notice` / `::error file=…,line=…,title=…::[id] snippet%0Adescription`). Paths are relative to `GITHUB_WORKSPACE` when set, else cwd. `line` is omitted when the finding has no line (`0` / NaN). Like `--json` it skips the text-mode notes (purple, scan and regex-only notes), the directory framework note and its port probe, and the >50-files prompt; an attached (`--cdp`) scan's capture notes go to stderr, as under `--json`. Combined with `--json` → `stderr> Error: --github cannot be used with --json\n`, `exit 1`. Combined with `--list-rules` (with or without `--json`) → `stderr> Error: --github annotates scan findings and cannot be used with --list-rules\n`, `exit 1`. Evaluated after `--help` (so `--github --help` prints usage). Exit codes are unchanged |
| `--quiet` | text mode prints only summary line(s) to stderr; under `--list-rules` it instead prints one rule id per line to **stdout** |
| `--list-rules` | print every registered rule (`all_antipatterns()`: the built-ins plus any installed rule pack) and **exit 0** without scanning. Catalog goes to **stdout**. `--json` pretty-prints an array of `{id,name,category,severity,scopes,description}` (plus `advisory: true` on advisory rules). `--quiet` prints one rule id per line. `--scope` and `--no-advisory` filter the catalog the same way they filter a scan; config ignores (`ignoreRules`) are not applied, so the catalog lists rules a project has switched off. `--github` with it is rejected (see `--github`). A positional target or `--cdp` with it → `stderr> Error: --list-rules prints the rule catalog and takes no file, URL, or --cdp scan\n`, `exit 1`. Evaluated after `--help` (so `--list-rules --help` prints usage) |
| `--help` | print usage (stdout) and `exit 0` — evaluated *after* scope/viewport parsing (so a bad `--scope` still errors first) |
| `--no-advisory` | drop findings with `advisory === true` before output/exit-code |
| `--no-consent-hiding` | URL scans only (Rust engine): leave known consent managers' banners, and a site's own cookie notices, on the page instead of hiding them before the scan (see **Consent banners** under URL scans). No effect on file scans |
| `--no-overlay-hiding` | URL scans only (Rust engine): leave product tours, site-builder badges and preloaders on the page, and skip the preloader wait (see **Tours and preloaders** under URL scans). No effect on file scans |
| `--fast` | deprecated, ignored; `stderr> Note: --fast is deprecated and ignored. The full scan is fast now and runs every rule.\n` |
| `--gpt`, `--gemini` | deprecated, ignored; `stderr> Note: --gpt and --gemini are deprecated and ignored. Generated-UI tells now run by default.\n` |
| `--no-config` | `configEnabled=false`: detectionConfig = `{ignoreRules:[],ignoreFiles:[],ignoreValues:[]}`; also disables design system and inline ignores |
| `--no-inline-ignores` | `inlineIgnoresEnabled = configEnabled && !flag` |
| `--no-design-system` | `designSystemEnabled = configEnabled && !flag && detectionConfig.designSystem?.enabled !== false` |
| `--scope <v>` / `--scope=<v>` | comma-split, trimmed, filtered; repeated occurrences accumulate; the flag+value are spliced out of `args`. Value missing or starting with `--` → `stderr> Error: --scope requires a value. Valid scopes: ${[...RULE_SCOPES].join(', ')}\n`, `exit 1`. Unknown → `stderr> Error: unknown --scope value(s): ${unknown.join(', ')}. Valid scopes: ...\n`, `exit 1`. `RULE_SCOPES` = union of rules' `scopes` = `type`, `layout` (in insertion order: `type` first from `overused-font`, then `layout` from `nested-cards`). |
| `--viewport <WxH>` / `--viewport=WxH` | regex `/^(\d{2,5})x(\d{2,5})$/i`; failure → `stderr> Error: --viewport requires a WxH value, e.g. --viewport 390x844\n`, `exit 1`. Sets `baseScanOptions.viewport = {width,height}` (browser scans only). Spliced out. |
| `--cdp <v>` / `--cdp=<v>` | Rust engine only. Scan a page of a running Chromium-based app through its DevTools endpoint instead of any target (see **Attached pages** below). Spliced out with its value (the last one given wins). Value missing (end of args, or the next arg starts with `--`) → `stderr> Error: --cdp requires a DevTools port or URL, e.g. --cdp 9222 or --cdp ws://127.0.0.1:9222/devtools/browser/<id>\n`, `exit 1`. Accepted values: a port (`9222`, host `127.0.0.1`), `host:port`, `[v6]:port`, `http://host:port[/]`, `ws://host:port[/]`, `ws://host:port/devtools/browser/<id>`, `ws://host:port/devtools/page/<id>` (that page is the target). A host that is not `localhost` or a loopback address → `stderr> Error: --cdp only attaches to a DevTools port on this machine (127.0.0.1, localhost or [::1]), not <host>\n`; a port outside 1 to 65535 → `stderr> Error: --cdp port must be between 1 and 65535; got <v>\n`; another scheme → `stderr> Error: --cdp takes a port, host:port, http:// or ws:// URL; got <v>\n`; another path → `stderr> Error: --cdp expects a DevTools port or a ws://.../devtools/browser/<id> or .../devtools/page/<id> URL; got <v>\n`; each `exit 1`. With `--viewport` → `stderr> Error: --viewport does not apply with --cdp: the attached window keeps its own size\n`, `exit 1`. With positional targets (checked after `--help`) → `stderr> Error: --cdp scans the page of a running app and takes no file or URL targets\n`, `exit 1`. Stdin is never read under `--cdp`. |
| `--target <s>` / `--target=<s>` | With `--cdp`: picks the page (below). Spliced out with its value. Missing or blank value → `stderr> Error: --target requires a substring of the page's URL or title, e.g. --target localhost:5173\n`, `exit 1`. Without `--cdp` → `stderr> Error: --target picks a page of the app --cdp attaches to; pass --cdp <port> with it\n`, `exit 1`. |
| positional | `targets = args.filter(a => !a.startsWith('--'))` (so a bare `-x` single-dash arg is a *target*, except `-json`/`-fast` which were rewritten) |

**Usage text** (`printUsage`, stdout, verbatim):
```
Usage: impeccable detect [options] [file-or-dir-or-url...]

Scan files or URLs for UI anti-patterns and design quality issues.

Options:
  --json              Output results as JSON
  --quiet             In text mode, only print the final findings count
  --github            Write GitHub Actions annotations for each
                      finding. Not with --json or --list-rules
  --list-rules        Print the detector rule catalog and exit. --json
                      for JSON, --quiet for ids only. Takes no targets
  --scope <name>      Only report rules in the given design domain
                      (type, layout). Comma-separated.
  --viewport <WxH>    Browser viewport for URL scans (default 1280x800),
                      e.g. --viewport 390x844 for a mobile-width pass
  --no-config         Do not apply project config, detector ignores, inline
                      ignore comments, or DESIGN.md
  --no-inline-ignores Do not honor in-file impeccable-disable* ignore comments
  --no-design-system  Do not load local DESIGN.md / .impeccable/design.json context
  --no-advisory       Suppress advisory findings entirely (e.g. em-dash overuse)
  --no-consent-hiding URL scans: keep known consent managers' banners
                      (OneTrust, Cookiebot, ...) on the page instead of hiding
                      them before the scan
  --no-overlay-hiding URL scans: keep product tours (driver.js) and
                      preloaders still covering the page instead of hiding
                      them before the scan
  --cdp <port|url>    Scan a page of a running Chromium-based desktop app
                      (Electron, NW.js, CEF, WebView2) through its local
                      DevTools port instead of loading a URL. The page is
                      scanned as shown and left as found: never navigated,
                      resized or closed. Takes no file or URL targets
  --target <text>     With --cdp: pick the page whose URL or title contains
                      this text when the app has more than one
  --help              Show this help message

Advisory findings:
  Some rules are advisory: detected and listed in a separate section, but never
  counted as failures and never changing the exit code. They stay out of the
  failure count so they never block automation. --no-advisory hides them.

Output streams:
  Human-readable findings go to stderr so stdout stays available for structured
  output. Use --json for JSON on stdout, or redirect text with 2> findings.txt.
  --github writes Actions annotations to stdout (no human findings).
  --list-rules writes the catalog to stdout (no scan).

Exit status:
  0  Scan completed with no primary findings (advisories may still be listed)
  1  At least one requested target could not be scanned
  2  Scan completed with primary findings
  Operational failure takes precedence when a multi-target scan is partial.

Project config:
  Respects .impeccable/config.json and .impeccable/config.local.json detector
  settings: detector.ignoreRules, detector.ignoreFiles, detector.ignoreValues,
  and detector.designSystem.enabled.

Inline ignores:
  In-file comments waive a finding where it lives and travel with the file:
    <!-- impeccable-disable overused-font -- exported brand doc -->
    .brand { font-family: Inter } /* impeccable-disable-line overused-font */
    // impeccable-disable-next-line bounce-easing: intentional bounce
  impeccable-disable applies to the whole file; -line / -next-line are scoped.
  List one or more rule ids (comma-separated), or omit them / use * for all.

Detection modes:
  HTML files     Static HTML/CSS analysis (default, catches linked CSS)
  Non-HTML files Regex pattern matching (CSS, JSX, TSX, etc.)
  URLs           Puppeteer full browser rendering (auto-detected;
                 http(s):// and file:// URLs; accessible linked CSS included)
  Running apps   --cdp: a page of a Chromium-based app, attached through its
                 DevTools port and scanned like a URL

Examples:
  impeccable detect src/
  impeccable detect index.html
  impeccable detect https://example.com
  impeccable detect --cdp 9222 --target "My App" --json
  impeccable detect --json .
  impeccable detect --github src/
  impeccable detect --list-rules
  impeccable detect --no-config src/
```

**Config**: `readDetectionConfig(process.cwd())` (see "Config file" below). Note config is resolved from **cwd**, not from the target.

**Design system per target**: `scanOptionsFor(localPath)`: if design system enabled and `localPath` given, `loadDesignSystemForTarget(localPath, {cache})` walks up from the target's own dir (never cwd): a dir with `DESIGN.md`/`Design.md`/`design.md` (directly, or under fallback dirs `.agents/context`, `docs`) is the design root; a dir with a project marker (`.git`, `package.json`, `.impeccable`) but no DESIGN.md is a boundary → no design system; reaching `os.homedir()` or fs root → none. Sidecar candidates: `<root>/.impeccable/design.json`, `<root>/DESIGN.json`, `<contextDir>/DESIGN.json`. Cache key `root:<dir>` or `\0none`. Options become `{...baseScanOptions, designSystem}` when found. `design-system.mjs` has **no CLI surface** (no main guard, no argv parsing); it only exports functions.

**A DESIGN.md purple switches the purple AI-palette check off** (Rust engine, corpus decision r3-23-ai-color-palette-brand-hue). When a target's design system declares a purple or violet colour (a DESIGN.md frontmatter `colors` entry or a sidecar colour, chroma at least 30 and hue 250 to 320 degrees: `is_declared_purple` in `crates/core/src/checks/rules.rs`), every engine drops the purple/violet forms of `ai-color-palette` for that target: the static HTML engine (`Purple/violet text (#hex) on heading`, `text-purple-* on heading`, `Purple/violet gradient (Tailwind)`, `Purple/violet accent colors detected`), the regex engine (`text-(purple|violet|indigo)-* on heading`, `from-(purple|violet|indigo)-* gradient`) and the URL engine on a `file:` target (the same forms plus `Purple/violet gradient background` and `Purple/violet neon text on dark background`). A form is purple when its snippet names purple, violet or indigo (`is_purple_palette_finding`); the cyan forms (`Cyan gradient background`, `Cyan neon text on dark background`) keep reporting, and so does every other rule. The drop runs after the built-in passes and before a rule pack and inline ignores, so the design hook, which scans through the same engines with the project's design system, and the wasm `detect` exports get it too. `http(s)` targets never load a design system (above) and are unaffected. In text mode (not `--json`, `--quiet` or `--github`) the CLI then prints, once per design system that applied to a scanned target and after the findings or alone when there are none: `stderr> \nNote: ai-color-palette's purple/violet check is off: ${source_path} declares ${label} (${hex}).\n` (no leading blank line when there are no findings; `${label}` is the first purple's declared name, `colors.primary` or `sidecar.<name>`). JSON output has no place for notes and is unchanged apart from the dropped findings.

**Input resolution**:
1. If `!process.stdin.isTTY && targets.length === 0` → **stdin mode** (`handleStdin`): read all stdin; try `JSON.parse`; if `parsed.tool_input.file_path` exists on disk → `detectLocalFile(fp, scanOptionsFor(fp))` (hook payload dispatch); else `detectText(input, '<stdin>', scanOptionsFor(null))`.
2. Else `paths = targets.length ? targets : [process.cwd()]`. `urlRe = /^(?:https?|file):\/\//i`. If more than one URL target, a shared browser is created once via `createBrowserDetector()` (defaults `waitUntil:'load'`, `settleMs:100`), closed in `finally`; a single URL uses `detectUrl` directly (`waitUntil:'networkidle0'`, `settleMs:0`).
3. For each target, in order:
   - URL: `file:` URLs get `scanOptionsFor(fileURLToPath(url) or null)`; http(s) get `baseScanOptions` (never cwd's design system). Errors: `stderr> Error: ${e.message}\n`, continue.
   - Else `resolved = path.resolve(target)`; `fs.statSync` failure → `stderr> Warning: cannot access ${target}\n`, continue.
   - **Directory**: unless `--json`/`--quiet`/`--github`, `detectFrameworkConfig(resolved)` (see below) and if found probes the port, writing one of three stderr notices:
     - listening & matched: `\n${name} dev server detected on localhost:${port}.\nFor more accurate results, scan the running site:\n  npx impeccable detect http://localhost:${port}\n\n`
     - listening & !matched: `\n${name} project detected (${basename(configPath)}).\nPort ${port} is in use by another service. Start the ${name} dev server and scan via URL for best results.\n\n`
     - not listening: `\n${name} project detected (${basename(configPath)}).\nStart the dev server and scan via URL for best results:\n  npx impeccable detect http://localhost:${port}\n\n`
     Then `files = walkDir(resolved, config.extensions).filter(f => !shouldIgnoreDetectionFile(f, cwd, config))`. The walk collects built-in scannable extensions plus any `detector.extensions` from cwd config (empty under `--no-config`). If `files.length > 50 && stdin.isTTY && !json && !quiet && !github`: `stderr> \nFound ${n} files (${htmlCount} HTML) in ${target}.\nScanning may take a while${htmlCount > 10 ? ' (static HTML/CSS processes each HTML file individually)' : ''}.\nTarget a specific subdirectory to narrow scope.\n` then readline prompt `Continue? [Y/n] ` on stderr; empty or `/^y(es)?$/i` continues; otherwise `stderr> Aborted.\n`, `exit 0`. Then `buildImportGraph(files)` → reverse map; each file scanned with its own options; findings from a file that is imported get `f.importedBy = [basename(importer), ...]` (Set iteration order). 
   - **File**: skipped if `shouldIgnoreDetectionFile`; else `detectLocalFile`.
   - `detectLocalFile(fp, opts)`: a configured `detector.extensions` entry matching the file (longest suffix) picks the engine (`html` → `detectHtml(fp, opts)`, `text` → `detectText`); otherwise extension (lowercased) in `HTML_EXTENSIONS = {'.html','.htm'}` → `detectHtml(fp, opts)`; else `detectText(readFileSync(fp,'utf-8'), fp, opts)`. The design hook uses the same rule.
   - **Linked stylesheets (#1087)**: the static HTML engine reads every local `<link rel=stylesheet>` for the cascade, but a linked sheet that `detector.ignoreFiles` waives is left out of the page-level pattern scan, so a pattern found only in that sheet is no longer reported under the page at line 0. Element rules still apply its styles through the cascade, and sheets that are not waived join the pattern scan as before. The design hook waives linked sheets the same way, matching each one the way it matches the edited file.
   - **Regex-only note (#884)**: after every target is scanned (never in stdin mode), unless `--json`/`--quiet`/`--github`, if `n > 0` local files went to `detectText` (by the `detectLocalFile` routing above, so a configured `html`-engine extension is not counted): `stderr> \n${n} file${n === 1 ? '' : 's'} scanned with regex matching only${exts.length ? ` (${exts.join(', ')})` : ''}.\nRules that need a parsed page (tiny-text, low-contrast and other element rules) did not run on them; findings are an undercount, not a clean bill of health.\n` where `exts` is the sorted set of those files' lowercased extensions (`extFromFilePath`, empty ones dropped). Then the full scan: `ports` = the sorted, deduplicated `port` of every scanned directory that matched `FRAMEWORK_CONFIGS` (whether or not the dev-server nudge found it listening); when non-empty, `For the full check, scan ${ports.length === 1 ? 'the running app' : 'each running app'}:\n` followed by `  npx impeccable detect http://localhost:${port}\n` per port, else `For the full check, scan the running dev server (npx impeccable detect http://localhost:PORT) or a static site's built .html.\n`. Then, only when `exts` intersects `HTML_LIKE_TEMPLATE_EXTS = ['.astro', '.svelte', '.vue']` (in that order, `t` = the intersection): `${t.join('/')} files can take the HTML engine instead: add { "ext": "${t[0]}", "engine": "html" } under detector.extensions in .impeccable/config.json (trades the regex rules and line numbers for the element rules).\n`. JSX/TSX get no opt-in line because the HTML parser reads nothing useful from them. The note precedes the findings output and changes neither stdout nor the exit code.
4. Post-filter: `filterDetectionFindings(all, config)` (ignoreRules/ignoreValues), then `filterByScopes(all, scopes)` (keeps findings whose rule declares any requested scope; empty scopes = no filter), then `--no-advisory` drop.
5. Partition `{primary, advisory}` by `f.advisory === true || f.severity === 'advisory'`.

Any target that cannot be scanned sets `hadOperationalFailure` (#711): a URL
whose browser setup or scan throws, a path `statSync` cannot reach
(`stderr> Warning: cannot access <target>`), an unreadable directory or file
in a dir walk, and a per-file scan that throws
(`stderr> Error: cannot scan <target>: <message>`). A multi-URL scan whose
shared browser fails to launch prints its `Error:` once and skips every URL
target. A URL whose loaded page is a bot challenge or an HTTP error page is
also a failure to scan (see **Page validity gate** under URL scans): it
reports no findings and prints `stderr> Error: the page is ...`.

**Output and exit codes**:
- `allFindings.length > 0`:
  - json: `stdout> JSON.stringify(allFindings, null, 2) + '\n'` (all findings, advisory ones flagged).
  - quiet: `stderr> ${primary.length} anti-pattern${n===1?'':'s'} found.\n`; if advisory: `stderr> dim(`${adv} advisory note${adv===1?'':'s'} (not counted).`) + '\n'`.
  - text: `stderr> formatFindings(all,false) + '\n'`.
  - `exit(hadOperationalFailure ? 1 : (primary.length > 0 ? 2 : 0))`.
- no findings: json → `stdout> []\n`; text/quiet → nothing. `exit(hadOperationalFailure ? 1 : 0)`.
- Text mode (not `--json`, `--quiet` or `--github`) prints scan notes after the findings, or alone when there are none, one line each in first-seen order: the DESIGN.md purple note; for a URL scan that hid consent banners `Hid the <names> consent banner(s) on <url> before scanning. Pass --no-consent-hiding to scan it.` (`<names>` joined `A, B and C`); for one that hid tours or preloaders `Hid <items> on <url> before scanning. Pass --no-overlay-hiding to scan it.`, each item `the <library> tour`, `the <builder> badge`, `the preloader <name>`, `the intro <name>` or `the splash <name>`, joined the same way (a site's own cookie notice is named `cookie notice` among the consent banners); and for a URL scan whose capture held sliders that never started, the capture note under `content-hidden-at-rest` below.
- Exit 1 takes precedence over exit 2: findings from the targets that did scan
  do not turn a partial scan into a complete one (#711).
- Any other exit: `1` for arg errors above; uncaught exceptions propagate to `cli.js` catch (`exit 1`).
- `dim(text)` = `process.stderr.isTTY ? '\x1b[2m' + text + '\x1b[0m' : text`. This is the **only** ANSI styling in detect output.

**Text format** (`formatFindings(findings, false)`):
```
formatFindingsBody(primary):
  group by f.file preserving first-seen order;
  for each file:  "\n${file}${importNote}"  where importNote = items[0].importedBy?.length ? ` (imported by ${items[0].importedBy.join(', ')})` : ''
     for each item: "  ${item.line ? `line ${item.line}: ` : ''}[${item.antipattern}] ${item.snippet}"
                    "    → ${item.description}"          (U+2192 arrow, 4-space indent)
then "\n${primary.length} anti-pattern${1?'':'s'} found."
then, if advisory non-empty:
  "\n" + dim('── Advisory (not counted as failures) ──')      (U+2500 box-drawing dashes)
  each body line individually dim()-wrapped
  dim(`\n${advisory.length} advisory note${1?'':'s'}. Suppress with --no-advisory.`)
all joined with '\n'.
```
Example (non-TTY):
```

/abs/a.css
  line 3: [side-tab] .card — border-left: 4px solid #6366f1
    → Thick colored border on one side of a card — ...

1 anti-pattern found.

── Advisory (not counted as failures) ──

/abs/a.html
  [em-dash-overuse] 9 em-dashes in body text
    → Em-dash saturation ...

1 advisory note. Suppress with --no-advisory.
```

**Finding object** (`cli/engine/findings.mjs` `finding(id, filePath, snippet, line = 0)`), key order exactly:
```js
{ antipattern: id, name: ap.name, description: ap.description, severity: ap.severity || 'warning', category: ap.category || null, file: filePath, line, snippet }
// plus, only when the effective severity is 'advisory':  advisory: true
```
Optional keys added later by engines (appended after the above): `ignoreValue` (design-system rules; browser findings with a value), `importedBy` (dir scans), `selector`, `thirdParty`, `consentHidden` and `overlaysHidden` (URL scans, below), `severity` may be overwritten by per-finding promotion (browser & html-patterns, e.g. pulsing dot in a header). Design-system findings are `{...finding(...), ...extras}` where extras = `{ ignoreValue }`. Static-HTML and browser findings have `line: 0`; regex findings have 1-based lines. `severity` values in registry: `'warning'` (default), `'advisory'` (many generated-UI tells and design-system-color/radius/font-size, numbered-section-labels, blinking-cursor, shape-assembled-illustration, bounce-easing, straggler, unbalanced-heading, hard-offset-shadow), `'error'` (`script-error`, `content-hidden-at-rest`). Per-finding demotions to `'advisory'` in the Rust engine: `flat-type-hierarchy` when weight separates the roles (below), a `script-error` an ad-tech script threw, and a `script-error` that is one of React's recoverable hydration errors (URL scans). `severity` is the canonical advisory field (#709): `deriveAdvisoryFlag` stamps `advisory: true` when and only when the effective severity is `'advisory'`, so a per-finding promotion or demotion carries the flag with it, and every `severity:'advisory'` rule is partitioned out of the failure count and the exit code. `isAdvisory` accepts either `finding.advisory === true` or `finding.severity === 'advisory'`.

**hard-offset-shadow** (advisory, `quality`, the static HTML engine and the URL engine; the regex engine that scans CSS, JSX, TSX and other source files does not run it, so a quiet source scan has not checked shadows): reads the element's resolved `box-shadow` with its `color` (for `currentColor`) and effective opacity, and reports once per element when every layer of the list parses and one outer layer has zero blur, a non-negative spread, an x or y offset of at least 4px and a painted alpha (layer alpha times opacity) above 0.1. A zero length counts as zero in any length unit (`0em` blur is zero blur); a non-zero length in a unit other than `px` is unresolved. An unparseable layer anywhere in the list, an `inset` layer, or an unresolved length or colour reports nothing. The static engine skips an element that is `hidden`, `display: none` (on it or an ancestor) or `visibility: hidden`/`collapse`. An empty or `none` shadow returns before the list is tokenized or any ancestor is walked. Waived with `data-impeccable-ignore="hard-offset-shadow"`.

**Categories**: `category` is `'slop'` (AI tells) or `'quality'`. Category has **no effect on output**, ordering, or exit codes; it is only carried in the finding and used by `getRulesForCategory`. Registry (62 ids, in order): side-tab, border-accent-on-rounded, overused-font, flat-type-hierarchy, gradient-text, ai-color-palette, cream-palette, nested-cards, monotonous-spacing, bounce-easing, pulsing-dot, blinking-cursor, shape-assembled-illustration, organic-clip-path, buried-raster, hard-offset-shadow, dark-glow, radial-halo, radial-spotlight-glow, marquee, icon-tile-stack, italic-serif-display, hero-eyebrow-chip, kicker-above-heading, numbered-section-labels, em-dash-overuse, marketing-buzzword, aphoristic-cadence, oversized-h1, extreme-negative-tracking, broken-image, script-error, content-hidden-at-rest, edge-flush-cards, text-occlusion, first-viewport-column-overflow, gray-on-color, low-contrast, line-length, cramped-padding, body-text-viewport-edge, tight-leading, skipped-heading, heading-rhythm, justified-text, straggler, unbalanced-heading, tiny-text, undersized-ui-text, all-caps-body, wide-tracking, text-overflow, repeated-container-text, clipped-overflow-container, design-system-font, design-system-color, design-system-radius, design-system-font-size, gpt-thin-border-wide-shadow, repeating-stripes-gradient, codex-grid-background, theater-slop-phrase. Retired ids (`image-hover-transform`, `layout-transition`) are in no list and no engine reports them; an `ignoreRules` entry or an inline `impeccable-disable` naming one still parses and suppresses nothing, and `doctor` reports it as `detector-ignore-rules-unknown`. Scopes: `type` = overused-font, flat-type-hierarchy, italic-serif-display, hero-eyebrow-chip, kicker-above-heading, numbered-section-labels, oversized-h1, extreme-negative-tracking, line-length, tight-leading, skipped-heading, heading-rhythm, justified-text, straggler, unbalanced-heading, tiny-text, undersized-ui-text, all-caps-body, wide-tracking, design-system-font, design-system-font-size; `layout` = nested-cards, monotonous-spacing, icon-tile-stack, content-hidden-at-rest, edge-flush-cards, text-occlusion, first-viewport-column-overflow, line-length, cramped-padding, body-text-viewport-edge, heading-rhythm, text-overflow, clipped-overflow-container. `RULE_ENGINE_SUPPORT = { regex: Set['source','page-analyzer'], 'static-html': Set['element','page'], browser: Set['element','page','layout'], visual: Set['visual-contrast'] }`.

**Tests**: `tests/detect-antipatterns-fixtures.test.mjs` (CLI block: `--help exits 0` and contains `Usage:`/`--quiet`, not `--gpt`; `--gpt` prints deprecation; `detect` prefix accepted; should-pass exits 0; should-flag exits 2 with `side-tab` in stderr; `--json` parses; `--quiet` stdout empty and stderr matches `/^[1-9]\d* anti-patterns? found\.$/`; `formatFindings — advisory partitioning`), `tests/detect-cli-stdin-dispatch.test.mjs`, `tests/detect-cli-design-contamination.test.mjs`, `tests/inline-ignores.test.mjs` ("detect CLI end-to-end"), `tests/detect-url-launch.test.mjs`.

#### `cli/engine/node/file-system.mjs`

- `SKIP_DIRS = {'node_modules','dist','build','__pycache__'}`; any directory whose name starts with `.` is skipped **except** `HIDDEN_SOURCE_DIRS = {'.vitepress','.vuepress','.storybook'}`. The root passed to `walkDir` is never name-checked (an explicit hidden dir scans).
- `SCANNABLE_EXTENSIONS = {'.html','.htm','.css','.scss','.sass','.less','.jsx','.tsx','.js','.ts','.vue','.svelte','.astro','.blade.php'}`; `hasScannableExtension` lowercases and also matches multi-dot exts by `endsWith` (`.blade.php`). CLI directory walks pass configured `detector.extensions` as a second suffix match: `name` lowercased; `name.length > ext.length && name.endsWith(ext)`.
- `walkDir` returns files in `readdirSync` order, recursive, unreadable dirs → `[]`.
- **There is no generated-file detection in the CLI** (`skill/scripts/lib/is-generated.mjs` is hook-side only and not imported by `cli/`).
- Import graph: `IMPORT_SPECIFIER_PATTERNS = [/import\s+(?:[\s\S]*?from\s+)?['"]([^'"]+)['"]/g, /@import\s+(?:url\(\s*)?['"]?([^'");\s]+)['"]?\s*\)?/g, /@(?:use|forward)\s+['"]([^'"]+)['"]/g]`; `resolveImport` only for specifiers matching `/^[./]/`: exact, `base+ext` for each scannable ext, then `base/index+ext`.
- `FRAMEWORK_CONFIGS` (first match wins, in this order): Next.js (`next.config.js|mjs|ts`, 3000, `/port\s*[:=]\s*(\d+)/`, header `x-powered-by` ~ `/next/i`); SvelteKit (`svelte.config.js|ts`, 5173, header `x-sveltekit-page` any); Nuxt (`nuxt.config.js|ts`, 3000, `x-powered-by` ~ `/nuxt/i`); Vite (`vite.config.js|ts|mjs`, 5173, body `/@vite\/client/`); Astro (`astro.config.js|ts|mjs`, 4321, body `/astro/i`); Angular (`angular.json`, 4200, `/"port"\s*:\s*(\d+)/`, body `/ng-version/i`); Remix (`remix.config.js|ts`, 3000, `x-powered-by` ~ `/remix/i`). Port overridden by first regex match in the config file.
- `isPortListening(port, fingerprint)`: with fingerprint → `fetch('http://localhost:${port}/')` 2s abort, header check then body check → `{listening:true, matched:bool}`; error → `{listening:false}`. Without fingerprint → TCP connect to 127.0.0.1 with 500ms timeout.

#### `cli/engine/shared/inline-ignores.mjs` — inline ignore comments

- `DIRECTIVE_RE = /impeccable-(disable-next-line|disable-line|disable)\b[ \t]*([^\n\r]*)/gi` (matched anywhere on a line, any comment syntax; case-insensitive).
- `TRAILING_CLOSER_RE = /\s*(?:\*\/\}?|--+>|\*\}|#\}|%>|\}\})\s*$/` stripped from the remainder; then reason cut at first `/\s*(?:--+|:)\s*/`; tokens split on `/[\s,]+/`, lowercased; empty or containing `*` → `['*']`.
- Lines split on `'\n'` only (CRLF-safe since `\r` excluded from capture). `disable` → file set; `disable-line` on line i (1-based) → `line.get(i)`; `disable-next-line` on line i → `nextLine.get(i+1)`.
- `isInlineIgnored(finding)`: rule = lowercased `finding.antipattern`; file set match (`*` or rule) → true; if `line > 0`, `line`/`nextLine` set match → true. Line-less findings (static HTML, browser) match **only** whole-file directives.
- Applied inside `detectText` and `detectHtml` at the end unless `options.inlineIgnores === false` (set by `--no-config` or `--no-inline-ignores`). Not applied to URL scans.
- Fast path: skip unless `/impeccable-disable/i` occurs.
- **DOM-scoped ignore** (`rules/checks.mjs scopedIgnoreActive`): attribute `data-impeccable-ignore="rule-a rule-b"` (split on `/[\s,]+/`, lowercased; empty value or `*` = all) on an element waives matching findings for it and its subtree in browser, extension, and static engines. In `detectHtml`'s html-patterns pass, selector-backed findings are dropped when every element matched by the (pseudo-stripped) selector is under a waiver; unmatched selectors keep the finding.
- Tests: `tests/inline-ignores.test.mjs`; fixture `scoped-ignore.html`.

#### Config file (`cli/lib/impeccable-config.mjs`) — `.impeccable/config.json` + `.impeccable/config.local.json`

- Paths: `<root>/.impeccable/config.json` (shared) and `<root>/.impeccable/config.local.json` (per-dev; `writeDetectionConfig(...,{local:true})` also appends a block to `.git/info/exclude`: `# impeccable-config-ignore-start\n.impeccable/config.local.json\n# impeccable-config-ignore-end`, idempotent by marker regex, following `gitdir:` files, and a linked worktree's `commondir`, to the exclude file git reads).
- Shape:
  ```json
  { "detector": { "ignoreRules": ["side-tab"], "ignoreFiles": ["src/legacy/**"],
                  "ignoreValues": [{ "rule": "overused-font", "value": "inter", "files": ["src/a.css"], "createdAt": "ISO", "reason": "..." }],
                  "designSystem": { "enabled": true }, "advisoryRules": "include"|"exclude" },
    "hook": { "consent": "accepted"|"declined", ... }, "updateCheck": true }
  ```
- `readDetectionConfig(root)`: start `{ignoreRules:[],ignoreFiles:[],ignoreValues:[],designSystem:{enabled:true},extensions:[]}`; for shared then local: apply legacy `raw.hook.*` section then `raw.detector.*`. Arrays are unioned (`uniqueStrings`, String-coerced); ignoreValues merged by key `rule\0value\0sortedFiles.join('\x1f')` (later wins); `designSystem.enabled` false only when literally `false`; `advisoryRules` copied only if `'include'|'exclude'`; `extensions` merges normalized `detector.extensions` entries by ext, later source wins (string → engine `html`; `{ext, engine}` → `text` only when engine is literally `"text"`). Invalid/non-array `extensions` skipped. Invalid JSON / non-object files are ignored silently. **No validation errors are ever raised by the CLI**; the only validation of ignore lists lives in `skill/scripts/lib/staleness-deep.mjs checkDetectorIgnores` (doctor): unknown `ignoreRules` ids vs live `ANTIPATTERNS` → finding `detector-ignore-rules-unknown` (severity `mention`); non-glob `ignoreFiles` entries that don't exist → `detector-ignore-files-missing`.
- `normalizeIgnoreValue(v)`: trim, strip one leading/trailing quote, `+`→space, collapse whitespace, lowercase. Rules lowercased/trimmed.
- `normalizeIgnoreValueEntries`: keeps `{rule, value, [files], [createdAt], [reason]}` in **that key order**; `file` (string) and `files` merged, trimmed, deduped.
- Glob → regex: `**/` → `(?:.*/)?` (zero or more whole directories, so `src/**/a.html` matches `src/a.html` and `src/pages/a.html` but not `src/xa.html`), any other `**` → `.*`, `*` → `[^/]*`, `?` → `[^/]`, `{a,b}` → `(?:a|b)`, regex specials escaped; anchored `^...$`. `matchesAnyGlob` tests the `/`-normalized path and its basename.
- `shouldIgnoreDetectionFile(filePath, root, config)`: raw path, absolute path, and root-relative path (if inside root) tested against `ignoreFiles`.
- `filterDetectionFindings`: drop when `ignoreRules` has the rule, or an `ignoreValues` entry matches: same rule; entry.value `*` (wildcard) OR extracted value equals (with color-key equality for `design-system-color`: rgb/hex/hsl parsed to `r,g,b,round(a*255)`); if entry has `files`, `finding.file` (or any `/`-suffix of it) must glob-match; a wildcard with no files never matches (unscoped `*` disallowed).
- `extractFindingIgnoreValue`: only for `overused-font, bounce-easing, design-system-font, design-system-color, design-system-radius, design-system-font-size`; source `finding.ignoreValue || finding.value`, else parse `detail`/`snippet`: bounce → `animate-bounce`, `cubic-bezier(...)`, or animation token matching `/bounce|elastic|wobble|jiggle|spring/i`; fonts → `Primary font:`, `Google Fonts:`, `font-family:` value, or `family=` URL param (decoded).

#### `impeccable ignores` (`cli/bin/commands/ignores.mjs`)

- Actions/aliases: `status|ls|list`→list (default when no action), `add-rule|ignore-rule`, `add-file|ignore-file`, `add-value|ignore-value|update-value`, `remove-rule|rm-rule`, `remove-file|rm-file`, `remove-value|rm-value`, `clear`. `--help`/`-h` prints usage (stdout). Unknown → throws `Unknown ignores action: ${a}. Run "impeccable ignores --help".` (exit 1 via cli.js).
- Scope flags: `--shared` (default), `--local`, `--all` (remove/clear only); more than one → error `Pass only one scope flag: --shared, --local, or --all` (or `--shared or --local`).
- `add-rule <rule> [--all-values] [--reason ...]`: `overused-font` without `--all-values` → error "overused-font is value-specific by default. Use add-value overused-font <font>, or add-rule overused-font --all-values for broad suppression." Output: `Added ${rule} to ${local?'local':'shared'} detector ignoreRules (${relpath}).`
- `add-file <glob>` → `Added ${glob} to ... detector ignoreFiles (...)`.
- `add-value <rule> <value...> [--file <glob>]... [--reason <text...>]`: value = normalized join of positionals after rule; `--file`/`--files`/`--file=`/`--files=` (empty or flag-like → error); unknown `--x` → `Unknown add-value flag: --x`; `*` value requires `--file`; existing entry (same key) updates reason/files, else pushes `{rule,value,[files],createdAt: ISO now,[reason]}`. Output `Added ${rule}=${value} to ... detector ignoreValues (...)`.
- `remove-*` → `Removed ${n} from shared (path), ${n} from local (path).` or `No matching detector ignore found.` `clear` → `Cleared detector ignores in ${'shared and local config'|'local config'|'shared config'}.`
- `list` output:
  ```
  Impeccable detector ignores
    shared file: .impeccable/config.json
    local file:  .impeccable/config.local.json

  Merged:
    ignoreRules:  (none)
    ignoreFiles:  ...
    ignoreValues: rule=value [glob1, glob2] - reason, ...
    designSystem: enabled|disabled

  Shared:
  ...

  Local:
  ...
  ```
- Tests: `tests/cli-ignores.test.js`.

#### `cli/engine/engines/browser/detect-url.mjs` — URL scans

- `detectUrl(url, options)`; options: `profile`, `waitUntil` (default `'networkidle0'`), `settleMs` (default 0), `viewport` (default `{width:1280,height:800}`), `browser` (external), `headless` (default true), `designSystem`, `scriptErrors` (default on), `contentHidden` (default on), `visualContrast`/`visualContrastBrowser`/`visualContrastPixel` (default on), `visualContrastMaxCandidates` (12), `visualContrastScrollOffscreen` (true).
- Puppeteer imported dynamically; missing → throw `puppeteer is required for URL scanning. Install: npm install puppeteer`. Browser script read from `<engine>/detect-antipatterns-browser.js`; missing → `Browser script not found at ${path}`.
- Launch: `launchArgs = process.env.CI ? ['--no-sandbox','--disable-setuid-sandbox'] : []`. On `win32` first `puppeteer.launch({channel:'chrome', headless, args})`, on failure fall back to bundled `puppeteer.launch({headless, args})` with `err.cause = channelError`. Non-Windows: bundled only.
- Flow: `newPage` → attach `pageerror` listener (message first line, trimmed, sliced to 160 chars, deduped) → `setViewport(viewport)` → `page.goto(url, {waitUntil, timeout: 30000})` → optional settle → `page.evaluate` sets `window.__IMPECCABLE_CONFIG__ = {...existing, autoScan:false, ...(designSystem ? {designSystem} : {})}` where designSystem serialized as `{present:true, hasFonts, allowedFonts:[...], hasColors, allowedColors:[{r,g,b}], hasRadii, allowedRadii:[px], hasPillRadius}` → `page.evaluate(browserScript)` (injects the bundle; defines `window.impeccableDetect/impeccableDetectAsync/impeccableScan/impeccableScanAsync/impeccableMeasureHiddenText/impeccableCollectVisualContrastCandidates/impeccableAnalyzeVisualContrast/impeccableGetLastVisualContrastAnalyses`) → `window.impeccableDetect({decorate:false, serialize:true})` returns groups `[{selector, tagName, rect, isPageLevel, isHidden, findings:[{type, category, severity, advisory, detail, ignoreValue, name, description}]}]`; flattened to `{id:type, snippet:detail, ignoreValue, severity}` → content-hidden sweep (`measureContentHiddenAfterReveal`: scroll in steps `max(200, floor(innerHeight*0.7))` with `behavior:'instant'`, 40ms rAF pauses, back to top, wait 700ms, then `impeccableMeasureHiddenText()`; `checkContentHiddenAtRest` fires when `totalChars>=200 && hiddenChars>=150 && share>0.3`, snippet `${pct}% of page text (${hidden} of ${total} chars) stays at opacity 0 / visibility hidden after reveal handlers ran (e.g. "sample")`) → up to 3 `script-error` findings `{id:'script-error', snippet:message}` → visual contrast fallback → `finally` close page (and browser if owned).
- Visual contrast fallback (`runVisualContrastFallback`): browser-side `impeccableAnalyzeVisualContrast({maxCandidates, scrollOffscreen})` findings not already low-contrast on that selector; then candidates (from analyses or `impeccableCollectVisualContrastCandidates`) not resolved pass/fail → per candidate `captureVisualContrastCandidate(page, candidate, viewport)`.
- **Screenshot flow** (`screenshot-contrast.mjs`, as the JS ran it; the Rust engine's verdict and refusals replace the p10 rule below, see **What the visual pass refuses to read** under the Rust engine additions): clip sanitized (`x,y` floored ≥0; `width` ≤ viewport width (default 1600), `height` ≤ 320, both ≥1); `page.screenshot({encoding:'base64', clip, captureBeyondViewport:true})` before; inject `<style id="impeccable-visual-contrast-hide-style">[data-impeccable-visual-contrast-target]{color:transparent!important;-webkit-text-fill-color:transparent!important;text-shadow:none!important}[data-impeccable-visual-contrast-target][data-impeccable-bgclip-text="true"]{background-image:none!important}` and set attribute `data-impeccable-visual-contrast-target=impeccable-contrast-${Date.now()}-${rand}` (+ `data-impeccable-bgclip-text="true"` if `candidate.backgroundClipText`); screenshot after; remove attributes; in-page canvas diff: pixels with channel delta sum ≥10 are glyph pixels; ratio uses WCAG luminance of CSS text color (unless `preferRenderedForeground`) vs after-pixel; needs ≥8 ratios; p10 = `ratios[floor(0.10*n)]`; finding when `p10 < candidate.threshold`: `{id:'low-contrast', snippet:`pixel contrast ${p10.toFixed(1)}:1 median ${median.toFixed(1)}:1 (need ${threshold}:1) on ${reasons.slice(0,3).join(', ') || 'visual background'} "${text}"`}`. No screenshot files are written.
- Results mapped via `finding(id, url, snippet)` (file = the URL, line 0); `ignoreValue` set if present; `severity` overridden if the browser supplied one.
- **Rust engine additions (not in the JS):**
  - **Scan order.** The reveal sweep runs *before* anything is measured, and one post-reveal capture serves every pass: the rule pass, `content-hidden-at-rest`, and the visual-contrast pass all read that capture, so a hit test any of them answers is answered for all three. The JS ran the rule pass over a capture taken right after load, when a scroll-reveal section is still at opacity 0 and the element checks skip it; the rules now measure the page as a visitor who scrolls it sees it, which reports the faults inside revealed sections and no longer reports a fade-in as `buried-raster`. `content-hidden-at-rest` still measures what stays hidden after the reveal handlers ran. Phase names, profile events, and the order findings are emitted in are unchanged.
  - **Covered text and unread surfaces.** The element pass asks `elementsFromPoint` over a failing `low-contrast` run, at the points the `text-occlusion` grid asks for the same box, in the same hit-test rounds. Where every deciding point has an image, video, canvas, or opaque fill at full opacity above the text (not the element, a descendant or an ancestor), the text is covered at capture: no verdict, and the visual-contrast collector skips it too. Where every deciding point has paint beneath the text that is nobody's ancestor, lies above the surface the background walk used, and could change what the text sits on, no element-pass verdict is printed; a visual-contrast candidate is still measured. That paint is a picture (an image, video, canvas or SVG `image`, or a `url()` background drawn `no-repeat`, `cover`, `contain`, at a percentage or larger than 256px), a gradient or fill that moves the named colour by more than 24 summed over the channels once composited over it, or, where the walk named no single colour or only reached the page ground (`html`, `body`, the canvas), a gradient, fill or SVG shape it cannot compare. Texture is read past and the verdict stands: a gradient drawn in cells of at most 64px on both axes, a `repeating-*` gradient, a gradient whose stops are mostly transparent or that pairs a transparent stop with a stop within 3px, a `url()` repeated at a drawn size of at most 256px, anything with a `mask-image`, and a picture, gradient or shape at an effective opacity of 0.3 or less. Paint at alpha times opacity of 0.1 or less is a wash and is read past too. A layer that cannot be placed (a remote image tiled at its own size, an `image-set`) keeps the verdict. The snapshot records `maskImage` and `webkitMaskImage` for this. The run must lie wholly inside the viewport, every point must be answered, and at least half must find the text; otherwise, and for a run only partly under a layer, the verdict stands. The SAFE_TAGS path asks before the page claims a colour pair. `text-occlusion` counts a decorated box only where its captured rect holds the probe point, so an answer from a page that moved after the capture names no occluder.
  - **What is not painted at capture reports nothing.** The URL engine's element pass drops findings on an element the painted-at-capture predicate (`crates/core/src/browser/painted.rs`) says a visitor cannot see: `display: none` or `content-visibility: hidden` above it, `visibility: hidden` or `checkVisibility()` false, an effective opacity of 0.02 or less, a clipping ancestor with no area or no overlap, a box wholly outside the document, a 1px visually hidden box. The text measurements, `italic-serif-display` and `gradient-text` also need text and area; `bounce-easing`, `dark-glow`, `hard-offset-shadow` and `ai-color-palette` need area. `blinking-cursor` needs area, and its own opacity and a `visibility: hidden` its parent does not share do not hide it, so a cursor caught between blinks still reports. `kicker-above-heading` asks the same about the heading and the label above it. The page-level forms of `bounce-easing`, `dark-glow` and `pulsing-dot` report only when their selector matches at least one painted element (no area test, since the selector may name a pseudo-element); a form without a selector is unchanged. `text-occlusion` asks it about the covered text, every hit-test answer that covers it, each card a headline overhangs and each inline leak host, in addition to its own ancestor test; it also skips text under a `filter: blur()` of 4px or more on the element or an ancestor and text with fewer than two characters on screen (a lone emoji), and names what covers the text: `overlapping text`, `an SVG graphic` (an SVG element other than `text`, `tspan` or `textPath`), `an opaque element` (a box with an opaque fill), or `a bordered element` (a box counted through its borders alone). `content-hidden-at-rest` is not gated. The static HTML engine measures no boxes and is unchanged.
  - **The box the text sits in** (observations-42 rows 5, 6, 12 and 13). `cramped-padding`'s own-text form measures only the recorded lines that cross the element's box (a line that wraps out below a fixed-height box is not held by it; a box none of whose lines cross it is not measured), skips `display: inline` boxes (a highlight hugs its glyphs by design), and does not count the sides of a background-only box that reaches both edges of the viewport. Its flush form reads no edge where an ancestor's `background-image` is gradients alone whose every shown colour nearly matches the box's fill (with transparent stops, only `linear-gradient(to <side>, transparent <len>, <fill> <len>)` with the box wholly in the filled part), or where an earlier sibling layer under the box holds an `img` or `video` (not a `canvas`) that paints (shown, not faded to 0.05 or less on the way) and whose own rect covers it, and inside a chip under 27.5px a label lands on an edge within a quarter of its font size (at most 4px), scaled as before. `text-occlusion` probes only the points on the victim's recorded lines that cross its own text when there are lines, no rotation or skew on it or above it, and at least one grid point on a line, and the share covered is taken over those points; it skips an answer that is a button or link (or within two levels of one) positioned `absolute` or `fixed` that reaches within a tenth of its height of the top and the bottom of a sideways scroller that overflows, holds the victim and not the control, and is at least four times as wide as the control, at one of the scroller's ends; the inline leak form reports only when the fill overlaps a sibling with text, and prints the box height less one line height per recorded line; and where the box or text that answered is not itself filled, the snippet adds ` on an opaque layer (<selector>)`, the nearest box above it (not holding the victim) whose opaque fill covers at least half of the victim's box. The surface climb looks inside a `display: inline` box with element children whatever its own rect (an `<img>` in an inline `<picture>`). The pixel walk answers `unresolved` (`text clipped at point`) at a point where the text is not in the `elementsFromPoint` stack, does not ignore pointer events, and lies outside the rect of an ancestor that clips its overflow. `low-contrast` is not reported on a disabled control (`[disabled]`, `aria-disabled="true"`, itself or an ancestor) in either engine. `first-viewport-column-overflow` does not measure the content of a closed `<details>` (outside its `<summary>`), and leaves out a column of three or more painted links that hold nine tenths of its text (hidden links and their text left out of both counts) at 40 characters or fewer on average, as it leaves out a navigation rail. A generic block at 24px or larger (`LEADING_DISPLAY_TYPE_PX`) is not prose for `line-length` and `body-text-viewport-edge`.
  - **What paints** (observations-47 rows 2, 13 and 22). `text-overflow` reports no spill from a box that generates a box, clips on y (`overflow-y`, else the shorthand's second or only value, at `hidden`, or at `clip` with an `overflow-clip-margin` of 0 or unrecorded), is drawn at its layout size (its rect's height within 1px of `clientHeight` plus its top and bottom borders) and whose own text rect and every painted descendant rect lie wholly above or below its padding box (top border plus `clientHeight`), when no descendant carries unmeasured generated content and each of its own unmeasured `::before` and `::after` generates one quoted string holding a single character that is not a letter, digit, space or control character, optionally followed by a variation selector (an icon glyph), at a recorded `font-size` no larger than the box's `clientWidth` and with no `transform` or `scale` of its own. The snapshot records `overflowClipMargin`, and `fontSize` for pseudo-elements, from this capture on; an older capture's `clip` box counts as clipping at 0, and its unmeasured `::before` or `::after` keeps the spill. `cramped-padding`'s flush form skips a text candidate whose computed `font-size` parses under 1px, and text exactly the land threshold off an edge lands only where an edge is drawn into the box on that side: a border (a width over 0 in a colour that is not transparent), a visible inset `box-shadow` layer, drawn on each side to the depth of its spread moved by its offset (spread plus y on top, minus x on the right, minus y on the bottom, plus x on the left) where that is over 0, or a visible outline with a negative `outline-offset`; a side with no recorded border width or colour, or a box with no recorded `box-shadow` or `outline-offset`, lands as before. `em-dash-overuse` counts `--` only when the run of hyphens is exactly two long and a non-space follows, and `---` only between two letters or digits, in both engines and the source-file matcher. Two U+2014 marks side by side count as one dash (round 14).
  - **Nothing a visitor can see** (observations-47 branch 1). A face under `backface-visibility: hidden` is turned away when the normal of the product of its own and every ancestor's linear transform (the third row of its inverse, Chromium's `cofactor33 * det` sign) points away by more than 0.1 of its length (0.3 under an ancestor that sets `perspective`); a second matrix in the chain with `m33` below 0, a chain deeper than 256, an unparsed transform, a perspective term or an individual `rotate` or `scale` above keeps it. `flat-type-hierarchy` skips headings in a 1px box `clip` or `clip-path: inset()` removes. `text-occlusion`'s covered text needs the Text gate's visible share (25% on the x axis), and an occluder that sets the victim's leading words from the same text origin (within 1px) with every one of `fontFamily`, `fontSize`, `fontWeight`, `fontStyle`, `fontStretch`, `fontVariant`, `fontVariantCaps`, `fontFeatureSettings`, `letterSpacing`, `wordSpacing` and `textTransform` recorded and equal (and, when the occluder's text wraps, `lineHeight`, `textAlign`, `whiteSpace`, `wordBreak` and `hyphens` too, since they move its later lines), and with no opaque fill or border of its own, is an overprint, not a collision; the snapshot records `fontStretch`, `fontFeatureSettings` and `wordSpacing` from this capture on, and older captures never overprint. `side-tab`'s page form is paint-gated like `bounce-easing`'s. `cream-palette` (browser) does not report a cream body when opaque, image-free, in-flow boxes spanning at least 95% of the body's width over the body (looked through transparent boxes to level 3) cover 95% of the page height, the colour covering the most height is neither cream nor near-cream (each channel 209 or more, red over green over blue, red at least 2 over blue), and no full-width box to level 8 paints a strict cream fill. `radial-spotlight-glow` scales the glow's opacity by `1 - opacity` of each later sibling `<video poster>` the capture saw at a `readyState` of 2 or more, or complete `<img>`, that covers it (the poster or image a JPEG, by a path ending in `.jpg`, `.jpeg`, `.jpe`, `.jfif`, `.pjpeg` or `.pjp` before any query or fragment, or a `data:image/jpeg` URI; any other format, or a URL naming none, may be transparent); the snapshot records a video's `readyState` from this capture on, and older captures never count a video, paints above it and fits by `cover` or `fill`. `content-hidden-at-rest` leaves out a `position: fixed` box at `visibility: hidden` covering the viewport that is not and holds no `main`, `[role="main"]` or `h1` and less than half of the body's text. The Text gate cuts a fixed layer's text at the viewport's sides. Below the fold, text whose parent is an avatar-shaped box (at most 160px each way, or text of at most three characters) is covered when a later sibling holds a complete JPEG `<img>` (as above) matching that box within 3px whose box, cut to the overflow clip of every box between it and the sibling, covers the text, with the picture and every box between it and the sibling at an opacity of 0.95 or more and none of them setting a `z-index`. Both of these covers follow CSS `order` in a flex or grid parent: a positioned sibling over an unpositioned box paints above it, an absolutely positioned child counts as `order: 0`, any other child needs its `order` recorded, and a sibling ordered before the box covers nothing; the snapshot records `order` from this capture on. `body-text-viewport-edge` skips text in a box (or ancestor) where the capture saw a running animation or transition move it on the x axis (`left`, `right`, `margin-left`, `margin-right`, `inset-inline*`, or a `transform` or `translate` with an x offset of 1px or more) and either a transition naming that property or CSS animations whose keyframes set it, every one finite (a positive iteration count), on the document timeline and not paused, trace the move; an animation whose keyframes the capture could not read keeps the box where it was measured. `buried-raster` takes a raster for a state layer when an ancestor is below full opacity, displaced, declares a transition naming `opacity` (not `all`), and either runs an `opacity` animation the capture saw or sits at an opacity off the 0.01 grid.
  - **Text a visitor cannot see at capture** (observations-1004 branch 1). `text-occlusion` skips a point over a victim whose nearest `sticky` or `fixed` ancestor (or itself) sets a numeric `z-index` of 0 or below and is anchored to the window's bottom (computed `top: auto`, `bottom` a length; a fixed box's `top` always resolves to pixels, so in practice only sticky layers qualify) when the recorded hit-test stack places a box outside that layer, painted at capture at an effective opacity of 0.95 or more, with a `background-color` alpha of 0.95 or more between the answer and the victim, but only when no point the probe asks over any non-screen-reader text in that layer answers the layer or anything in it, and every such point is answered (a curtain footer the page covers); `z-index: auto` or unrecorded keeps the finding, and the headline-overhang pass skips text in such a buried layer too. Over text that computes `pointer-events: none`, a graphic answer inside an SVG no longer counts as covering it (an SVG text element still does), as an opaque box already did not. A box that hides its vertical overflow is a script scroll frame only when, besides the earlier tests, a descendant in its flow (reached without passing an `absolute` or `fixed` box) ends more than 1px below it, or an absolutely placed box at least half its height starts inside it and runs past its bottom; such a placed box that does not run past (a wrapper at `inset: 0`) is read like the box's own flow; a descendant the capture did not measure, text of the box's own, or a walk past 2,000 descendants keeps the frame. `body-text-viewport-edge` skips text under a `position: fixed` ancestor shorter than half the viewport's height (a consent bar) when the text keeps at least 4px from both screen edges; a window-tall fixed layer, an unmeasured one and a sticky ancestor keep it. The Text gate treats a computed `font-size` of 1px or less (was: under 1px) as unpainted. `numbered-section-labels` skips a label or heading that is, or sits under, `display: none` or `content-visibility: hidden`; a label at opacity 0 still counts. Below the fold, `low-contrast` text is covered when a later sibling of it or of one of its ancestors (16 levels at most, every one at `z-index: auto`) holds a box with a `background-color` alpha of 0.95 or more, clipped to its border or padding box, in a positioned layer with no `z-index`, mask, clip-path, `filter`, `mix-blend-mode` other than `normal`, or opacity under 0.95 up to that sibling, whose box cut to every overflow clip and inset by 0.3 of its largest pixel corner radius covers the text within 1px; a percentage radius, anything unmeasured or a search past 3,000 elements covers nothing. `skipped-heading` quotes a heading without the text of its `script`, `style`, `template` and `noscript` descendants.
  - **Text not presented at capture** (observations-1006 branch 1; URL engine). The paint walk behind every gated rule and the occlusion victim filter treats an element, or an ancestor, as not rendered when it is a dialog (`<dialog>`, `role="dialog"` or `"alertdialog"`, or `aria-modal="true"`) carrying `inert` on the same box and a positioned sibling of it or of one of its ancestors buries it: a `background-color` alpha of 0.95 or more at an effective opacity of 0.95 or more, a box holding the dialog's box within 1px, stacked above the dialog's branch (a higher `z-index`, or the same one later in tree order). An inert dialog nothing buries, `inert` on anything that is not a dialog, and an unrecorded `z-index` keep their text. A box of 1px or less on each axis, with area, whose own `overflow-x` and `overflow-y` are `hidden` or `clip` is visually hidden, like the `clip`/`clip-path` utility. `text-occlusion` skips a victim whose computed `font-size` is 1px or less, or whose text fill (`-webkit-text-fill-color`, else `color`) is fully transparent with a `-webkit-text-stroke-width` of 0 and `text-shadow: none`, unless a box on its chain sets `background-clip: text` or its `::before` or `::after` draws content; an unrecorded ink property keeps it. Inside a box that hides its x overflow, when a track between the text and that box is moved by a running, endlessly repeating transform animation and runs past the box, the Text gate scores only a copy wholly inside the box (within 1px) instead of one with a quarter of its width showing, provided the text is no wider than the box's `clientWidth`. The hit-test cover test also takes frosted glass: a layer above the text whose fill alpha times its effective opacity is 0.75 or more over a `backdrop-filter` blur of 8px or more that also reaches a third of the text's font size (a translucent layer without blur still needs the 0.95 floors; an unrecorded font size takes no glass). A run whose grid is split, with a deciding point covered and one covering layer that holds 85% or more of its direct text box, is covered; a cover with a `clip-path`, a mask or a transform other than a translation is not measured that way, and a run with no direct text box keeps the verdict. `line-length`, `tight-leading`, `tiny-text` and `undersized-ui-text` drop a finding whose text the hit-test stacks show covered (the verdict contrast already reads, including a later opaque card below the fold) by layers that stay over it as the page scrolls: a covering layer counts only when neither it nor an ancestor is `fixed` or `sticky`, or both the first such box and the layer itself start within 1px of the viewport's top with the page scrolled to its top (a nav pinned over breadcrumbs). A bar fixed lower in the window, one at the bottom of a full-window fixed wrapper, and points the capture never asked keep the finding. In a live scan the stacks for these rules are asked as extra hit tests in the existing rounds.
  - **Surfaces off the ancestor chain.** Every `low-contrast` verdict, not only the SAFE_TAGS path's, also asks the structural layer climb (`impeccable_core::browser::visual::layer_under_text`), which answers below the fold and through layers with `pointer-events: none`, which `elementsFromPoint` never lists. Besides pictures and fills, the climb reads a gradient that paints a surface on a box that is nobody's ancestor (not a dot grid, hairline, small cell, mask, a wash at alpha times opacity of 0.1 or less, or a box at opacity 0.3 or less) and an SVG shape drawn around the text (at most three times the text box plus 16px on each axis) as paint the walk never read; an ancestor painting an opaque gradient ends the climb as its fill does. A positioned `::before` or `::after` is the surface only where its box, placed from its containing block, pixel offsets, size and transform (a rotation, scale or skew as the bounds of the transformed box about its centre), covers the text; one it cannot place keeps the size test. A pseudo-element or box at a negative `z-index` is skipped where an opaque fill or gradient lies between it and its stacking context (the host's own fill included, unless the host opens that context): an offset shadow behind a white card, a `-z-10` layer inside a white section that is not `isolate`. Paint the capture cannot place for certain (an unplaced pseudo, a pseudo with no recorded `z-index` or a negative layer with no recorded `isolation` over such a fill) is read as before but decides no verdict of its own: the verdict stands outside the SAFE_TAGS path, nothing is reprinted against it, and `dark-glow` ignores it. The snapshot records `isolation`, and `zIndex` and `translate` for pseudo-elements. Outside the SAFE_TAGS path the climb's own finding counts where the stacks cannot answer (below the fold, a replay that never asked), already see unread paint, or confirm the walk only because the layer ignores pointer events or is an SVG shape. That paint is read for the surfaces it can make over the ground the walk read (`unread_verdict`): the climb is asked again past each translucent layer, up to four, until it reaches paint that hides what is beneath or the walk's ground; the walk's own translucent layers are placed over or under that paint by the box each hangs from. A verdict that fails on every surface in that span stands, also where the stacks see unread paint, and prints `N:1 (need T:1) — text #ink on #surface (layer on <box>)` against the surface it reads best on when the walk named a flat colour more than 24 away from the span and the span's colours are known (fills and gradients); a verdict that passes on every one is dropped; one that depends on what the paint shows (a photo, unknown SVG fill) stands outside the SAFE_TAGS path, and the SAFE_TAGS path waives it as before. Outlined text (a `-webkit-text-stroke` wider than 0 in a colour of its own, or two or more opaque text-shadow layers blurred at most 2px and offset in opposing directions) keeps its element verdict. The snapshot records `webkitTextStrokeColor` and `webkitTextStrokeWidth`. `dark-glow`'s "on dark background" form reads an opaque detached fill or gradient under the element's box as its surface; a picture or translucent layer leaves the claim as the walk made it.
  - **Text the element pass hands over.** `collect_visual_contrast_candidates` takes `maxRoutedCandidates` (0 unless given; the URL engine passes 12, after its 12 `maxCandidates`, whose candidates are unchanged): text over unread paint whose verdict depends on the paint, ink in exactly the walk's surface colour over unread paint, links and spans with no fill among them, and outlined text, in document order. They carry `routed` (`unread layer` or `text outline`) and those reasons, which the sampled pass refuses; a first-budget candidate the element pass hands over carries `routed` only (and `preferRenderedForeground: true` for an outline). The URL engine takes a routed candidate to the screenshot pass even where the element pass reported on its selector, and where the pixels give a verdict, pass or fail, drops the element pass's `low-contrast` finding on that selector. Those extra reads stop after 4 seconds or 4 slow reads in a row with no verdict. Because the pixels can drop it, every element-pass `low-contrast` verdict on text `routed_reason` hands over (decided in the rule pass's recorded hit-test rounds) carries the `visual-contrast` origin in the evidence, where it stands when the pixels give none, and `replay_url_scan` leaves it out: the `scan` origin stays what a replay of the recorded capture reproduces. The CLI's findings and their order are unchanged. Where the pixels gave none (the routed reads were spent, the read was refused for a blocked reason such as `backdrop-filter`, the candidate never reached a slot, or the two shots differed), a verdict that measured nothing is dropped: the structural climb found a picture under the run, or paint it cannot model that the ratio is not measured through, the text is not outlined, and the verdict is in the element pass's plain form, not already printed against a layer (`handed_over_unmeasured`; round 13 decision 2, narrow form r13-t7: only a measured failing ratio reports). The unread paint is read from the pseudo-elements over the run that are not placed off it, hidden, at an `opacity` under 0.1, or beneath the box's own opaque fill, else from the box's own gradient (a raster or colours the capture does not give measure nothing). A layer with a `z-index` above 0 and above every one a positioned box sets on the text's way up to it is a cover over the text and its ground alike (a fixed full-window backdrop): the failing ratio of the text's own colours is measured through it at any opacity. Any other layer is a wash under the text, and washes stack where they overlap: within each wash its fill and background layers, bottom to top, are each absent or paint one of their colours, the group is faded once by the wash's `opacity`, and every combination of the washes is composited over the verdict's ground; the ratio is measured only where the ink fails on the ground and on every combination (more than 4096 combinations measure nothing). Any other verdict that stands reports with `severity: 'advisory'` and its text unchanged (corpus decision r6-t5-unread-pixel-verdicts; `settle_handed_over` in `crates/browser/src/lib.rs`). The `not measured` text is no longer printed. The second budget reads each design once before its copies: a routed candidate with the same tag, `color`, `font-size`, `font-weight` and class list as one already taken waits until the document is read and takes a slot only where distinct text left one free. The screenshot flow's hide style also clears `-webkit-text-stroke-color`, and `fill` and `stroke` on SVG `text` and `tspan`. In the stack walk a picture faded by its own box or a wrapper (below the nearest box that also holds the text) is composited at that opacity, and a picture that cannot be read (a tainted image, a refused video frame) ends the walk. `overlaps_page_width` measures against the root's scroll width when the document is wider than the window.
  - **Element identity.** A finding from the rule pass or the visual-contrast pass carries `selector` (after `ignoreValue`): the flagged group's generated selector (`body` / `html` for page-level groups), or the visual-contrast candidate's selector. `content-hidden-at-rest` and `script-error` findings have none.
  - **Where a script error was thrown.** A `script-error` snippet is the message followed by ` (at <source>)`: `<function>, <url>:<line>:<column>` from the top stack frame, or `<url>:<line>:<column>` for an anonymous frame or a script with no stack (a syntax error carries only the script position). A frame in a script with no URL (code from `eval` or `new Function`, a script element given text rather than a `src`) prints V8's name for it, `<anonymous>`, in the URL's place: `(at eval, <anonymous>:3:1)`. Line and column are 1-based (CDP's are 0-based); a URL past 160 characters is cut to 160 plus `...`; a throw with neither a named script nor a frame gets no suffix. The suffix is added after deduping, from the first occurrence. The JSON shape is unchanged: the source travels in `snippet`.
  - **One message shape.** Every script-error message reads the way the console prints it, `Uncaught <Type>: <message>`, whatever path the throw took, where the JS printed puppeteer's `err.message` with the type stripped on one path and CDP's typed text on another. CDP's marker comes first: `Uncaught`, or `Uncaught (in promise)` for an unhandled rejection. An `Error` reads the first line of its description, which names its type (`TypeError: ...`, or the name a library gave it, `ChunkLoadError: ...`); a description that names no type gets the class in front, and an empty message is the type alone (`Uncaught AbortError`). A thrown value that is not an `Error` (CDP `type: object` with no `subtype`: a plain object, a class instance, a rejected object) reads its class and V8's property preview, `Uncaught Object {code: "E_CONFIG", message: "config missing"}` (strings JSON-quoted, other values as previewed, `...` when the preview overflowed). A primitive reads as `String(value)`, `Uncaught boom`.
