A throw that arrives with no exception object (a cross-origin script) keeps CDP's text, which is typed already (`Uncaught TypeError: ...`, or `Uncaught [object Object]`). The body after the marker is its first line, trimmed, and the message after its type (`TypeError: `) is cut at 160 characters, the cut the JS applied to the untyped message, so naming the type costs the message nothing it showed before; a throw whose body is empty reports nothing.
  - **React invariants read as their message.** A production React build throws `Minified React error #<n>; visit <url> for the full message or use the non-minified dev environment for full errors and additional helpful warnings.` For a common invariant number the boilerplate from `; visit ` on is replaced by the message React's error decoder prints, the number kept: `Uncaught Error: Minified React error #418: Hydration failed because the initial UI does not match what was rendered on the server.` The table (`REACT_INVARIANTS` in `crates/browser/src/cdp.rs`) covers 31, 130, 185, 300, 301, 310, 321, 418, 419, 421, 422, 423, 425 and 426. The message is added after the 160-character cut, so it is never cut. A number the table does not know, text after the number other than `; visit `, and any message that is not an invariant stay as they were. Deduping compares the decoded messages, so both reports of one invariant still share a key.
  - **One throw, one report.** Deduping compares messages with the `(in promise)` marker read as a plain `Uncaught`, so a throw that surfaces both synchronously and as an unhandled rejection (a consent manager's patched `document.createElement` called from a script and from a promise) reports once, with the first report's text and source. Messages that differ in anything else stay separate. The three-error cap applies after deduping, and after classing (next item): counted errors and advisory ad-tech errors are capped at three each, in arrival order, so ad-tech errors never take a slot a counted error needs. Three failing ad scripts ahead of the site's own broken bundle still leave the bundle's error reported as `error`; a scan reports at most six script errors.
  - **Ad-tech script errors are advisory and name the vendor** (corpus decision r3-31-script-error-ad-tech). A script error thrown from a known ad-tech script (its source URL contains `anymind360.com` (AnyMind), a script file name beginning `prebid`, wherever it is hosted (Prebid), `connect.facebook.net` (Meta Pixel), `googletagmanager.com` (Google Tag Manager), `cookielaw.org` (OneTrust, the consent layer that holds ad tags back), or `googlesyndication.com` / `doubleclick.net` / `googleadservices.com` (Google Ads), `nagich.co.il` (Nagich, an accessibility overlay), or `asset.chase.com/web/library/digddsautomation/reportingjs/` (Chase Reporting, a shared reporting tag)), or whose message names an ad API (`browsingTopics`, `joinAdInterestGroup`, `runAdAuction` (vendor `ad tech`), `adsbygoogle` (Google Ads), `_prebidjs` (Prebid)), reports with `severity: 'advisory'`, its snippet ends ` (third-party: <vendor>)`, and it carries `thirdParty: "<vendor>"` after `ignoreValue`. The source is checked first. Product analytics and a site's own bundles and chunks (ad code served from the site's own host included) are not on the list and stay `error`. The list lives in `crates/core/src/third_party.rs` and holds only vendors the corpus has evidence for.
  - **React's recoverable hydration errors are advisory** (corpus decision r5-p13-script-error-hydration). A script error whose message is `Minified React error #<n>` with `<n>` one of 418, 419, 422, 423 or 425, decoded or in the build's own `; visit <url>` form, reports with `severity: 'advisory'`: React renders the page again on the client and the page shows complete. Its snippet is unchanged and it carries no `thirdParty`. Every other error stays `error`, other React invariants included (#185, #421, any number outside the five). The three caps are separate: at most 3 counted errors, 3 ad-tech errors and 3 hydration errors per scan, so a hydration chain never takes the slot a first-party error needs. The ad-tech check runs first. The list lives in `crates/core/src/script_errors.rs`.
  - **Closed interface is left out of `content-hidden-at-rest`** (corpus decision r5-p5-content-hidden-closed-navigation). The measure already skipped `display: none`, the `hidden` attribute, `aria-hidden="true"`, `content-visibility: hidden` and `dialog`. It now also skips, from both the hidden count and the page total, every subtree that starts at a box which is itself at opacity 0.02 or less or `visibility: hidden | collapse` and is closed interface by any one of: (1) the box or an ancestor is a `nav` or has `role` `navigation`, `menu` or `menubar`; (2) the box or an ancestor has the `inert` attribute; (3) the box or an ancestor has an `id` that an `[aria-controls]` element names while saying `aria-expanded="false"` or `aria-selected="false"`, and no element naming that id says `true`; (4) the box itself has `role` `dialog`, `alertdialog` or `tabpanel`; (5) the box itself has a class token whose words (split at non-alphanumerics and lower-to-upper steps) include `offcanvas`, `drawer`, `megamenu`, `submenu`, `dropdown`, `flyout`, `modal`, `tabpanel` or `tabpane`, the pairs `off canvas`, `mega menu` or `sub menu`, or one of `tab` / `tabs` / `accordion` together with one of `content` / `panel` / `pane` / `body`; (6) the element just before the box says `aria-expanded="false"`, or that element is a heading (`h1` to `h6`, `role="heading"`) or has a single element child and its first element child says it (an accordion heading wrapping its button; a site header that opens with a closed menu button says nothing about the box after it); (7) the box itself is `position: fixed` with area and wholly left or right of the viewport. A box none of these describe counts as before: an in-flow section a scroll reveal never showed, a hover overlay, a carousel slide, an unmarked panel. A bare `menu`, `tab`, `panel` or `popup` class is not enough. The thresholds and the snippet are unchanged. The predicate is `closed_container` in `crates/core/src/browser/page_checks.rs`.
  - **Scroll-linked reveals and sliders that never started** (corpus decision r6-t6-hidden-scroll-linked). The share is measured with the page back at the top after the reveal sweep, so a reveal that follows the scroll position is at 0 again when it is read. When the counts would report, the URL engine asks the hidden boxes that hold the most text (at most 8, `SCROLL_PROBE_MAX`) whether they show once the page is scrolled to them: it scrolls each box's centre to the viewport's centre (for a box inside a `position: sticky` stage, through the stage's parent in half-viewport steps, at most 12), waits up to four frames at each stop for its opacity to pass 0.02 with `visibility` not `hidden` / `collapse`, then scrolls back to the top and waits 700ms, all within 5 seconds. A box that showed is a scroll-linked reveal: its text stays in the page total and leaves the hidden count. The answers are recorded in the capture's facts (`shownOnScroll: [{ el, shown }]`, omitted when empty), so `replay_url_scan` measures what the live scan measured; a recording without them, and any DOM with no page behind it (the extension, the static engine), counts every such box as hidden, as before. Separately, a hidden box in a slider that never started leaves both counts: the box or one of its six nearest ancestors has a class token whose words include `slider`, `carousel`, `swiper`, `slick`, `splide`, `glide`, `flickity`, `slideshow` or `revslider` and none of `slide`, `item`, `cell`, `card` or `pane` (so `swiper-wrapper` is a slider and `swiper-slide` is one of its slides), and no element in that slider shows text (every one is `visibility: hidden | collapse`, or sits under a `display: none`, `aria-hidden="true"` or opacity 0.02 or less box within the slider). A slider showing its current slide is not this, and its waiting slides count as before. When such sliders hold 150 characters or more, text mode prints the note `Capture note: <n> chars of text on <url> sit in sliders that never started (every slide hidden, e.g. "<first 40 chars>"), so content-hidden-at-rest left them out. The page may not have finished loading; scan it again to check that text.` It is not a finding, does not change the exit code and blocks nothing; the evidence API records the same lines in `Evidence.capture_notes` and a replay in `ReplayOutcome.capture_notes`. `measureHiddenTextDOM()`'s JSON gains `unstartedSliderChars` and `unstartedSliderSamples` only when they are non-zero.
  - **Findings on third-party widget markup name the vendor** (corpus decision r4-p24-third-party-widget-markup). A finding whose element is a known widget vendor's markup keeps its severity; its snippet ends ` (third-party: <vendor>)` and it carries `thirdParty: "<vendor>"` (after `selector`). Taboola owns its whole feed: an element with an id starting `taboola-` or `internal_trc_`, a class starting `trc_rbox`, or the class `trc_related_container`, and everything under it. Swiper owns only its structural elements: the element itself has the class `swiper`, `swiper-container`, `swiper-wrapper` or `swiper-slide`, or an id starting `swiper-wrapper-`; the site's content inside a slide is the site's. Slick owns its structural elements the same way (the class `slick-slider`, `slick-list`, `slick-track`, `slick-slide` or `slick-arrow`) and its whole dot list (the class `slick-dots` and everything under it); SuperSlide owns its `tempWrap`; react-fast-marquee owns everything under an element with a class starting `rfm-`; Kaltura owns everything under a class starting `playkit-` or the class `kaltura-player` or `kaltura-player-container`. The rule pass tags in `serialize_findings` (so the in-page bundle and the extension see it too), the visual-contrast pass by its selector's first match. Page-level findings are never tagged. The static engine does not tag.
  - **Purple headings in the brand hue** (corpus decision r3-23-ai-color-palette-brand-hue). The heading forms of `ai-color-palette` (a snippet ending ` on heading`) do not report when the heading's computed `color` is within 12 degrees of hue of a brand surface on the same page: the solid `background-color` (alpha at least 0.9, chroma at least 50, no gradient painted over it) of the logo (an element whose `id`, `class`, `alt` or `aria-label` contains `logo`; its text colour too, read from the first element in it with text of its own, so a type logo set in a child `span` counts), the nav bar (`nav`, `header`, `role=navigation|banner`, at least half the viewport wide), neither inside `main` or an `article` (an article's own header, a pagination nav and a row of partner logos are local chrome), or a band across the page (at least 90% of the viewport's width) covering a fifth of the viewport's area or more. Headings are never brand surfaces, and the gradient and neon forms keep reporting: purple that shows up only in headings and gradients is the tell. URL engine only (the static engine has no rendered page to read surfaces from).
  - **Category colours** (corpus decision r5-p28-ai-color-palette-category-colours). An element-level `ai-color-palette` finding does not report when its element wears the colour as one of a category system's hues: the elements in the same role on the page carry six or more distinct hues between them, at least half of them outside the violet (260 to 310 degrees) and cyan (170 to 197 degrees) bands, read by hue alone. For the ink forms (` on heading`, ` neon text on dark background`) the role is the tag plus the computed `font-size` and `font-weight`, and an element's hue is its computed `color` (alpha at least 0.5, chroma at least 50). For the fill forms (` gradient background`, `Purple/violet gradient (Tailwind)`) the role is the tag plus the rounded box size, and the hue is the first chromatic stop of the computed `background-image` gradient. Two hues less than 5 degrees apart count once. Such an element does not vote in the two-hue ink gate either. The page-level `Purple/violet accent colors detected` is unaffected. URL engine only (the static engine reads no rendered roles and reports as before).
  - **The cyan band** (corpus decision r6-t7-cyan-band). The element-level `ai-color-palette` forms (` gradient background`, ` neon text on dark background`) read a colour as cyan when its hue is 170 to 197 degrees and its HSL saturation is at least 0.4; the violet band (260 to 310 degrees) has no saturation floor. Emerald (160 to 164), sky (198 to 200) and a grayed teal no longer count as cyan, for the finding or for the two-hue ink gate. URL engine only (the static engine has no cyan form).
  - **Footer column titles** (corpus decision r5-p29-skipped-heading-footer-titles; static HTML engine and URL engine alike). `skipped-heading` does not report a skip into the footer: the skipped heading is inside `footer`, `[role="contentinfo"]` or `#footer`, and the heading it follows is either outside that footer (the last heading before it) or that footer's first heading when that heading did not itself skip into the footer (a closing call to action inside the footer; a first heading that skipped in is a column title). A skip between two later footer headings reports, and so does every skip outside a footer. A footer built from unmarked `div`s is not recognised and reports as before.
  - **Contrast ratios in snippets.** Every `low-contrast` producer (the element text path shared by both engines, which covers links, spans and placeholders, the static engine's `:hover state`, and the URL engine's `browser contrast` sampling and `pixel contrast` passes, verdict and median) prints a ratio against its threshold: one decimal; two when one decimal would round a failing ratio up to the threshold; and those two cut, not rounded, when rounding them would reach it too. A ratio under the bar never prints as the bar: `4.49:1 (need 4.5:1)`, never `4.5:1` or `4.50:1`. A ratio at or above the threshold, such as a median over it, prints one decimal.
  - **Page validity gate.** After `goto` (and settle), before injecting anything, the engine classifies the page (`crates/browser/src/validity.rs`) from the main document's final CDP response and an in-page probe (title, visible text length, final URL, challenge-widget markers). Challenge: an `x-amzn-waf-action` header, `cf-mitigated: challenge`, a final URL on a challenge-only host (`perfdrive.com`, `captcha-delivery.com`), or, on a page with at most 3000 characters of visible text, a challenge title prefix ("Just a moment", "Attention Required! | Cloudflare", "Human Verification", "Access Denied", "Radware Bot Manager", "Captcha", ...) or a full-page widget marker (Cloudflare challenge form, PerimeterX, DataDome, Imperva, Akamai, AWS WAF, slider captchas). On a page of any size, a bot manager's challenge sheet laid over the rendered page is a challenge too: HUMAN Security's (PerimeterX's) "Press & hold" check, `#px-captcha-modal` or `#px-captcha-wrapper`, while it is showing (not `display: none`, not `visibility: hidden`, opacity over 0.02) and covers at least half the viewport; the signal reads `overlay human-press-and-hold`, and the probe records `overlays` only when one shows. HTTP error: a final status >= 400 with no challenge signal. A blocked page throws `the page is a bot challenge, not the site (<signals>). Findings would describe the challenge page, so none are reported.` or `the page returned an error (HTTP <status>). Findings would describe the error page, so none are reported.`, which the CLI prints as `Error: ...` and counts as an operational failure (exit 1). A `file://` load has no response, so only the probe signals apply.
    Consent wall: a page where a known consent manager (next item) is showing (its text counted with the text of open shadow roots under its roots, where Usercentrics renders), hiding it would leave next to nothing (fewer than 20 visible characters outside its roots, and no visible form control (`input` other than hidden, `select`, `textarea`, `button`) and no `img`, `svg`, `video`, `canvas`, `iframe`, `object` or `embed` of 10,000 square pixels or more outside them), and the manager holds at least 100 characters or a frame of 10,000 square pixels or more (a message drawn in an iframe, whose text the page cannot read). For this test, text of the manager's that `body.innerText` does not see (rendered in a shadow root, or in a root appended outside `<body>`) is added to the page's visible-text count too (`consentShadowChars`), so the two counts describe the same text; the challenge checks keep the page's own count. It throws `the page is a consent wall, not the site (consent manager <names>, <n> of <total> visible characters outside it, no control or image outside it). Findings would describe the consent dialog, so none are reported.` A short page under an ordinary banner (a sign-in form, a splash page with a button, an image-first page) is scanned. The probe runs before any banner is hidden, and again in the same evaluation as each later hide pass (after the reveal sweep, right after the capture, and after the `content-hidden-at-rest` scroll probe when it scrolled the page), whenever a manager's root is in the page, with the earlier hide rules switched off and its inline hides lifted for that read (an inline hide the manager has since replaced with its own display, or with the same `display: none !important` written by rewriting its style attribute, stays as the manager set it), so a wall that arrives after load, fills a root that was an empty stub, or arrives during the capture or the scroll probe is refused too (the evidence then carries that later probe). A manager's box is showing when it has a size and paints (`checkVisibility` with opacity and visibility), and is not parked out of sight: a fixed box wholly outside the viewport (unless a transformed, filtered or contained ancestor holds it), a box wholly above or beside the document, or a box an ancestor's overflow clips away. Controls and media outside the roots are counted in the page's open shadow roots at any depth, nested web components included. With `--no-consent-hiding` the gate is off and the page is scanned with its banner, which is what that flag asks for. The probe object (evidence) carries `consent` (the names of the managers showing), `consentChars` (visible characters inside them) and `consentOutside` (visible controls and sizable media outside them, counted up to 20).
  - **Consent banners** (Rust engine). A consent banner covers copy a visitor reads once they dismiss it: text under it was skipped as covered or scored against the banner, and the banner's own text was scored as the site's. After the validity gate, again after the reveal sweep (so a banner injected after load or on the first scroll is caught before the capture), once more right after the capture (when that pass changes the page, the page is captured again, so the capture, the live hit tests and the pixel reads agree), and after the `content-hidden-at-rest` scroll probe when it scrolled the page (so a banner that scrolling injects is off the page for the pixel pass; the capture already read stands), the engine hides the roots and backdrops of the consent managers in `crates/browser/src/consent.rs`: OneTrust, Cookiebot, Usercentrics, TrustArc, Didomi, Quantcast Choice, Sourcepoint, Osano, the open-source Cookie Consent library, CookieYes, Complianz, iubenda, Termly, Axeptio, Borlabs Cookie (its box, its floating `#BorlabsCookieWidget` and its `#BorlabsDialogBackdrop`), Klaro, Cookie Notice, Shopify's privacy banner, consentmanager (`#cmpwrapper`, the host of the open shadow root its box renders in, and `#cmpbox`), Tealium (`#__tealiumGDPRecModal`, its consent prompt, and `#__tealiumGDPRcpPrefs`, its preference center), Transcend (`#transcend-consent-manager`, the zero-size host of the open shadow root its banner renders in), Enzuzo (`#ez-cookie-notification`, its banner, and `#enzuzo-modal-wrap`, its preference modal), Tarteaucitron (`#tarteaucitronRoot`, the zero-height host of `#tarteaucitronAlertBig`, and its small banner, progress bar, preference panel `#tarteaucitron`, backdrop `#tarteaucitronBack` and reopen icon), and Google Funding Choices. Selectors are the vendor's own ids and classes, never a generic word. A cookie notice a site wrote for itself is hidden at the same points, by what it does. It is found from a choice control: a `button`, `[role=button]`, button input, an `a` with no `href` or one that names only a fragment or `javascript:`, or an element with an `onclick` or `cursor: pointer`, whose whole label (at most 40 characters, case and trailing punctuation aside) is an accept or refuse phrase (`Accept`, `Accept all`, `Reject non-essential`, `Allow all cookies`, `Alle Cookies akzeptieren`, `Tout accepter`, `Aceptar todo`, `接受`, `동의`, `Принять` and the like); a softer label (`OK`, `Got it`, `Close`, `Dismiss`, `Understood`, `Schließen`, `Fermer`, ...) counts only when the notice's text is a consent sentence (a use verb before `cookie`, or a policy, settings or preferences word after it). A plain link is never a choice. The notice is the nearest ancestor of the control whose own text outside links and controls contains `cookie` (any case), widened through every ancestor that adds no visible text, control or media of its own, so a strip inside a site header or a bar stacked with a buy bar is hidden alone. It must sit on a layer (itself, an ancestor or a box the widening passed through `position: fixed`, or `position: absolute` covering at least 90% of the viewport), the control must be in the viewport and be what `elementFromPoint` returns at its centre, and the notice must hold no text, email, password or other entry `input` (checkboxes and radios are allowed), `select`, `textarea`, `h1` or `main`, carry at most 1,500 visible characters, and leave at least 200 on the page outside it (a page that is little more than its notice is read as before). It is hidden with an inline `display: none !important` and marked `data-impeccable-cookie-notice="<name>"` (`<name>` is the tag plus its id, else `[role=...]`, else its first class). A lock layer beside it is hidden and marked `lock <name>`: a sibling `div` or `span` with no element children and no text, `position: fixed`, covering at least 90% of the viewport, with no background image, a fill under alpha 0.9, a positive `z-index`, and what `elementFromPoint` returns at one of the five preloader probe points while the notice shows. When the notice covered at least half the viewport, or a lock layer went with it, an inline `filter` that is one `blur()` of 12px or less on `html`, `body`, or an element up to two levels below `body` outside the notice whose box covers at least half the viewport is removed (`unlocked`: `filter <tag>#<id>`), and so is an inline `overflow` / `overflow-y: hidden` on `html` or `body` when no other dialog is showing. These are reported under the name `cookie notice`, after the managers, with the hidden elements' names as its `matched` selectors. Hiding is one injected `<style id="impeccable-consent-hide">` of `display: none !important` rules (plus an inline `display: none !important` on a matched element whose own inline style outranks the rule); nothing is clicked and no cookie or consent state is set. The scroll lock is undone only where the manager applied it: its own lock classes on `<html>` / `<body>` (Didomi `didomi-popup-open`, Sourcepoint `sp-message-open`, Enzuzo `ez-body-lock`) are removed, the `aria-hidden="true"` a manager put on the page behind its dialog is removed from exactly the elements it marked (Borlabs Cookie marks them `data-borlabs-cookie-aria-hidden`; reported in `unlocked` as `aria-hidden [<mark>]`), and an inline `overflow` / `overflow-y: hidden` on `<html>` or `<body>` is removed only while that manager's backdrop was showing the page is not an app shell (an element outside the managers at least half the viewport tall that scrolls itself with `overflow-y: auto` / `scroll`, whose body lock is the site's own), and no modal of the site's own (`dialog[open]`, `[aria-modal="true"]`, `[role="dialog"]`, `[role="alertdialog"]` outside the managers) is showing, since that modal may hold the lock. Every hide pass is best-effort: one that fails hides nothing and does not fail the scan. Every pass, the hit tests, and the evidence screenshot see the page without the banner (a last hide runs before the screenshot). Findings from a scan that hid a showing banner carry `consentHidden: ["<name>", ...]` (after `thirdParty`; `cookie notice` for a site's own), and text mode prints the note above. `--no-consent-hiding` turns all of it off, the site's own cookie notices included. The evidence API (`detect_url_evidence`) leaves the findings untagged, so they replay equal, and records `Evidence.consent`: `{ hidden, matched: { <name>: [selectors] }, unlocked }`. The static (file) engine is unaffected.
  - **Tours and preloaders** (Rust engine, `crates/browser/src/overlays.rs`). Right after the consent hide following the validity gate, the engine looks for a preloader still covering the page: a `position: fixed` layer other than `html` / `body` that covers at least 90% of the viewport, is what `elementFromPoint` returns (or contains it) at the center and at 20% / 80% of each axis (a layer with `pointer-events: none` is made hit-testable for that probe and put back), paints an opaque fill (alpha 0.9 or more) on itself or on a descendant covering as much, has at most 60 visible characters and no `a[href]`, `button`, visible `input`, `select`, `textarea`, `iframe`, `[role=button]`, dialog role, `aria-modal=true`, `dialog` or editable descendant, is not itself a dialog, and whose id or one of its classes names a loader (after trailing wrapper words such as `wrapper`, `overlay`, `screen`, `bg`, its last `-` / `_` word is `preloader`, `preload`, `preloading`, `loader`, `loading`, `pageloader`, `siteloader`, `loadingscreen` or `spinner`). A layer whose id or class names an intro instead (`intro`, `splash`, `splashscreen` as that last word) passes the same tests with up to 120 visible characters. A layer no loader or intro word names is an intro too when it carries `aria-hidden="true"` itself, passes the same tests with up to 120 visible characters, and is playing a finite animation (`getAnimations({ subtree: true })` holds a running CSS animation or transition whose end time is finite; an infinite one does not count, so such a layer is neither waited for nor hidden), and has no `cursor: pointer` or `onclick` of its own (such a layer is a gate); once seen it stays an intro after its animations end, and its name is the tag plus its id or first class (`div.spl`). It must stand in front of a page (below) to be waited for or hidden, except that while it animates and for 1s after its last animation the wait drops only the 200-character outside-text minimum (the `h1` and hit tests still apply, so a fixed hero the content scrolls over is never waited for). An opaque fill is a colour at alpha 0.9 or more, or a `background-image` that is gradients only with every stop at alpha 0.9 or more. A layer whose text asks to be entered (below) is never one. If one is there the engine polls every 250ms for up to 5s (12s while an intro is among them) for it to go on its own, then hides whatever is left with an inline `display: none !important` and marks it `data-impeccable-hidden="<name>"` (`<name>` is the tag plus the naming id or class, `div#preloader`; an intro also carries `data-impeccable-hidden-kind="intro"`). An intro (not a loader-named layer) is waited for only when it stands in front of a page (below). A splash gate is hidden at the same points without a wait, and nothing is clicked: a `position: fixed` layer covering at least 90% of the viewport, on top at the same five points, with an opaque fill on itself or a covering descendant, at most 60 visible characters among whose words is `enter`, `entrer`, `entrez`, `entrar`, `entra`, `eintreten` or `betreten` (or that contain `进入`, `進入` or `입장`), with no `18`, `21`, `+18`, `age`, `âge`, `alter`, `edad`, `idade`, `cookie`, `accept` or `agree`, not a dialog and holding no visible `input`, `select`, `textarea`, `iframe`, `form`, dialog or editable descendant, with at most one `a[href]` / `button` / `[role=button]`, and either `cursor: pointer`, an `onclick` attribute, or one control whose label asks to be entered; and it must stand in front of a page: it holds no `h1`, the page shows at least 200 visible characters outside it, and, with the layer set to `visibility: hidden` for the probe (put back in the same task), `elementFromPoint` finds something of the page under it at one of the five probe points (an element with text of its own, a replaced element or control, or a box smaller than 90% of the viewport; not `html` or `body`), so a one-screen site or a hero the content scrolls over from 100vh stays. It is marked `data-impeccable-hidden="<name>"` and `data-impeccable-hidden-kind="splash"`, `<name>` being the tag plus its id or first class and up to 40 characters of its text in quotes (`div.fixed ("Enter NoteForge →")`). Known product tours are hidden by their library's own markup with one injected `<style id="impeccable-overlay-hide">`: driver.js 1.x (`.driver-popover`, `svg.driver-overlay`), whose `driver-active` / `driver-fade` / `driver-simple` body classes (its stylesheet sets `pointer-events: none` under `.driver-active`, which hides the page from hit tests), highlight classes (`driver-active-element`, `driver-no-interaction`) and highlight aria (`aria-controls="driver-popover-content"` with `aria-haspopup` and `aria-expanded`) are removed too. Site-builder badges are hidden by the builder's own markup in the same style: Lovable's "Edit with Lovable" badge (`#lovable-badge`). The hide runs at the same points as the consent hide (after the validity gate, after the reveal sweep, after the capture with a recapture when it changed the page, after the scroll probe when it ran, before the screenshot) and is as best-effort. A gate the visitor answers, a site's own dialog, and any layer with controls or more text are left on the page. Findings from a scan that hid any carry `overlaysHidden: [{ "kind": "tour" | "badge" | "preloader" | "intro" | "splash", "name": "<name>" }, ...]` (tours, then badges, then preloaders, after `consentHidden`), and text mode prints the note above. `consentHidden` is unchanged and lists consent managers (and `cookie notice`) only. When a preloader is hidden, the scroll lock its page kept with it is undone too: a loader-named class on `<html>` or `<body>` (`is-loading`) is removed, and so is an inline `overflow` / `overflow-y: hidden` on either unless a modal of the site's own is open or an app shell scrolls inside itself (either may hold that lock) (recorded in `unlocked` as `body.is-loading`, `body style overflow`). `--no-overlay-hiding` turns all of it off, the wait included. The evidence API leaves the findings untagged and records `Evidence.overlays`: `{ hidden: [{ kind, name }], matched: { <library or builder>: [selectors] }, unlocked, waitedMs }` (`waitedMs` is 0 when no preloader was covering the page).
  - **What the visual pass refuses to read.** The JS reported whatever its sampling produced; the engine says nothing where the reading is not the text's own background. The candidate collector applies the element pass's text gates before a candidate takes one of the `maxCandidates` slots, is sampled, or is scrolled into view: text with no letter and no digit (`◯`, `{ }`, `/`) as well as emoji-only text; a disabled control (`[disabled]`, `[aria-disabled="true"]`); a computed font size under 1px; a `color` at alpha 0.02 or less, or a transparent `-webkit-text-fill-color`, unless the element clips its own background to its text; and an element the painted-at-capture predicate (`crates/core/src/browser/painted.rs`, text gate) says is not painted, such as a tab-strip cell past its clipping edge or a carousel slide parked beside the page. A background image read as a surface has to be under the text: an image drawn `no-repeat` (from the computed `background` shorthand's first layer) whose painted rect, at a `background-size` the capture can place (pixels, percentages, `cover`, or any size once the image's intrinsic size is loaded), covers neither the candidate's text box nor its own box (`body` and `html` are asked only about the text) ends the walk `background image does not cover the text` (`stop`), whether or not the pass could load the file. An icon sprite framing a count and a mark beside a button label are refused this way; a repeating tile and a placement the capture cannot state are read as before. In the stack walk (`impeccable_core::browser::visual`): a translucent surface no longer restarts the walk at its own parent (which re-entered the stack above the node it came from and composited that surface over itself until its alpha read as opaque, and which skipped siblings painted between the surface and that parent) — the walk keeps descending the one `elementsFromPoint` stack, collecting translucent surfaces until an opaque one grounds them, then composites them back in order (topmost surface still names the method, `...+alpha`); a walk that reaches the end of the stack with nothing opaque is unresolved rather than reporting the last translucent color as a surface. A gradient answers analytically only when its stops agree: any stop below 0.95 alpha (a scrim, a glow, a `from-x/10` wash composites differently at every point of the box) or two opaque stops more than 2× apart in contrast make it `gradient stops disagree`, and an `svg` in the stack makes it `<tag> paint`; both end the walk (`stop`) rather than letting the nodes under them answer for the text, so the candidate falls to the screenshot pass. In the screenshot flow: a candidate whose reasons include `background-clip text`, `filter`, or `backdrop filter` is refused before the two screenshots are taken; the verdict is the median over the *glyph cores* (channel delta ≥ 90% of the strongest delta in the box, ≥ 8 of them; when fewer than 8 pixels reach 90%, as on small or thin text, the pixels from 75% up) rather than the tenth percentile over every changed pixel, which antialiased edges drive to 1:1; and the pass reports nothing when the diff is not a glyph over its background — more than 55% of the clip changed (the page repainted), more than a quarter of the changed pixels moved the opposite way from the rest (text mid-animation leaves its old position and its new one in the same diff), most fully painted pixels are not the color the text declares (`preferRenderedForeground` aside, where the painted value *is* the claim), the surface under the glyphs stands 2:1 or further from the surface beside them in the same box (the page paints its own text twice, so hiding one copy leaves the other where the glyphs were), or the median of every measured pixel is more than 3× the verdict (the sample is two populations, not one surface). The snippet shape is unchanged. `median` in it is the median over the same glyph cores the verdict reads, so it prints the verdict's number and never reads below it (it used to be the median over every changed pixel, edges included, which printed verdicts above their own median such as `pixel contrast 3.5:1 median 1.7:1`). The median over every changed pixel still decides the bimodal refusal above, but it is not printed.
  - **Viewport edges and overflow.** `body-text-viewport-edge` reports a gutter under 16px, or under 12px when `innerWidth` is 480px or less. A paragraph whose measured text runs past a side of the viewport (the distance rounds below 0) reports no gutter on that side; it still reports a gutter from 0px to the floor on the other side (`(left 8px)`). Those paragraphs, through the same gates plus the Text paint gate and inline ignores, report once per page instead: one `body-text-viewport-edge` finding attributed to the page (`selector` `body`, in `pageLevel`) on the side the furthest text reaches. The message follows what the page does. It reads `text runs past the <side> edge of the <vw>px viewport and is cut off: <name> reaches <d>px past it, with text in <n> block(s)` when the text is cut off: the viewport clips x overflow (the root's `overflow-x`, or body's when the root's is `visible`, at `hidden` or `clip`), the side is not the one the page scrolls to (right, or left under `direction: rtl`), the root's recorded `scrollWidth` does not exceed its `clientWidth` by more than 1px, or for every paragraph on that side a box at `overflow-x: hidden` or `clip` (the paragraph or an ancestor below the body, with its own edge on that side inside the viewport) is passed by the text. `<name>` is then the box reaching furthest among the paragraphs and their ancestors. Otherwise it reads `page scrolls sideways at <vw>px: <name> reaches <d>px past the <side> edge, and text in <n> block(s) runs past it`, where `<name>` is the box reaching furthest past that side among those no ancestor below the root clips or scrolls on x and not `position: fixed`, and no further than the page scrolls (ties go to the outer box). When the engine cannot tell (nothing it can see clips the text and the root recorded no `scrollWidth`) the sideways wording stands and `<name>` is the box reaching furthest among the paragraphs and their ancestors. In both wordings the paragraph itself is named when its text reaches further than any such box. `<name>` is the element's `class_selector`, except that a `div` with no class is named `div#<id>`, else `div in <tag>#<id>` or `div in <tag>.<class>` (at most three classes) after its nearest ancestor below the body with an id or a class, else `div ("<text>")` with up to 40 characters of its text, cut at a word and ended with `…` when cut, double quotes in the text written as `'`. `text-overflow` reports a spill only when it does harm: a box at `overflow-x: hidden` or `clip` (the element itself or an ancestor below the body, with area) is passed by the spill's reach, the spill reaches within a quarter em of the viewport's side or past it, or the spilled part comes within a quarter em of another element's text or painted box on the element's lines (not an ancestor or descendant, not a box that holds the element's whole box, and not a decoration layer: an absolutely or fixed positioned box with no text, other than a replaced element or an `svg`, that is 2px thick or less or paints only a plain fill, with no background image or border, at alpha times opacity under 0.25). The reach is the painted extent, or the scroll extent where that is taken as read; text that runs past its box into free space reports nothing.
  - **Round 7 recall and gates** (corpus run 28). `line-length` and `body-text-viewport-edge` (its element and page-level forms) also measure prose written straight into a generic block: a `div`, `section`, `article`, `aside` or `main` laid out as `block` or `flow-root`, with more than 10 characters of its own text, only phrasing inside it, not set `white-space: pre*`, and outside any link, button, label, summary, select, textarea, button-, link-, tab-, menuitem- or option-role element and editable field; it is measured on its text runs only (where those cannot be measured it is not taken for a paragraph), and the edge snippet names it (`<div> with 228-char body ...`). The label collectors read the element that holds a label's text: its own text, else the one child whose text is not empty, at most three levels down, so a chip with an icon beside its text span is read through the span; `hero-eyebrow-chip` reads its sibling that way too, and its tracked-caps floor is 1.6px or 0.08em of the label's size; under 1.6px a label that is or holds a `time` element, or that names a year (1900 to 2099 as a word), is a dated meta line and not tracked caps (the recorded JS vectors pin the fixed floor alone). `kicker-above-heading` hands a label over a 48px-or-larger h1 to the hero rule at 1.6px, or, when the label is the h1's own previous sibling at 14px or less, at 0.08em where `hero-eyebrow-chip` reports that label (it does not read small-caps as capitals and passes over a dated line, so such a label stays a kicker; the static engine: at 14px or less). A card-context ancestor (`article`, `li`, `a`, `button`, ...) more than two viewports tall holds the page and is not a card for the kicker and numbered-label collectors. `icon-tile-stack` anchors on a card title set on a `div` or `span` too: laid out as a block, with no element children, 2 to 80 characters of its own text, weight 600 or more at 16px or more, outside any heading, link, button or label; it reads an `oklab()` or other modern tile fill. `tiny-text` skips a label typed in capitals (all its letters capitals, at most 40 letters, one line) and a run in a monospace face with `white-space: pre*`. `wide-tracking` counts a caps label's letters, not its characters, toward its 40-letter gate, and skips a one-line link or button label of at most 40 letters. The element contrast pass scores a box under 10px tall when it holds a line of its own text at 8px or more and is at least 0.9 of the font size tall. `ai-color-palette`'s gradient form skips a box whose gradient, sampled at nine points over the box from its computed size and position, shows one colour (every sample within 12 levels on each channel); an element reports the rule once: the computed gradient form stands for the utility-class gradient form, the computed purple-heading form for the class form (`text-purple-600 on heading`), and `gradient-text` on the element for both gradient forms. `gradient-text` skips a ramp whose strongest stop, times the element's own opacity, is under 0.15, and its stylesheet form does not stand when every element computing a clipped gradient is unpainted or under that floor. `gpt-thin-border-wide-shadow` reads, under one gradient ancestor with no picture, each of its stops composited over the fills below: a halo that shows over at most half of them does not count. `dark-glow`'s surface lift counts only layers carrying at least half the strength floor (1.5px of light), and its stylesheet form with no selector does not stand when an element's computed shadow carries the declaration: the element form read that element.
  - **Round 8 text and CSS-text forms** (corpus run 35). `line-length` divides the characters that reached the element's rendered lines, not the UTF-16 length of its `textContent`: the text of a `style`, `script`, `noscript` or `template` descendant and of a `display: none` one is left out, runs of collapsible white space (space, tab, line feed, carriage return, form feed) count once and none at either end unless the element computes `white-space: pre`, `pre-wrap` or `break-spaces`, and combining marks (Unicode `M*`) and format characters (`Cf`) are not counted. The gate that decides whether the element is long enough to measure still reads the raw length. `overused-font` (URL engine) keeps its element-count decision and snippet, and stands down when the face it would name sets under 5% of the characters in text that has a box (each element in its selector list weighs the characters of its own text nodes, white space collapsed; text that is or sits under `hidden`, `display: none`, `visibility: hidden` or `collapse`, or `content-visibility: hidden` is not weighed, opacity is not read); with no such text the element count stands; the static engine's form is unchanged. `side-tab`: the stylesheet scan for pseudo-element stripes (both engines) skips a stripe whose background has a `url()` layer and no token that parses as a colour or is a `var()`; in the URL engine the stylesheet form does not stand when an element its selector resolves to already carries the element form for the same pseudo-element. `marquee` (URL engine): the stylesheet form stands only when an element its selector resolves to carries something to read or look at: text, an `img`, `picture`, `video`, `canvas`, `iframe`, `object`, `embed` or `marquee` element (itself or inside it), an `svg`, `image` or `use` inside it, a `url()` background on it or under it, or generated content that is not empty; a selector that resolves to nothing stands as before. `bounce-easing` by animation name (both engines, element and stylesheet forms; the class and `cubic-bezier` forms are unchanged): the name does not report when the keyframes for every bounce-named animation are readable (the page's `@keyframes` in the URL engine's element form, the stylesheet text elsewhere) and only pulse: every declaration is `opacity`, a `transform` made only of `scale*()` functions with factors from 0 to 1 (or `none`), a `scale` with factors from 0 to 1, or an `animation-timing-function` with no `linear()`, no `var()` and no `cubic-bezier` whose y values leave 0 to 1. Any other property or value, an empty set of keyframes, or keyframes that cannot be found keeps the finding. The stylesheet form reads on past a pulse to the next declaration that names a bounce.
  - **Round 10 contrast surfaces** (corpus run 47; URL engine unless noted). A box at an opacity of 0.02 or less that a scroll or view timeline holds there (its computed `animation-timeline` is `scroll()`, `view()` or a named timeline, an animation moving its `opacity` was running at capture, and it is the box's only animation that sets `opacity`) counts at the opacity its end keyframe sets (the first keyframe for `reverse`) in `low-contrast`'s opacity gate and fold and wherever the painted-at-capture predicate counts an element's own opacity: the Text and Box paint gates, `painted_at_capture` (and the page checks that ask it), the text-box test and the shown-across test behind the colour-pair claim (`content-hidden-at-rest` is ungated and unchanged); a composition other than `replace`, a direction other than `normal` or `reverse`, or an end keyframe that sets no opacity keeps the captured value. A `filter` on the box that paints the text's surface, or on a box around it, repaints the ink, the surface, the gradient stops and the element's own fill alike, and `low-contrast` and `gray-on-color` read the painted colours (the snippet prints them); a filter below the surface's box that moves the ink, a filter the engine cannot model, a filter a running animation is moving, a colour-moving filter that also blurs, and SVG text with no readable fill still take no verdict. The structural layer climb follows children that each cover the whole text run past its usual six levels, up to 24, under the same node budget, so a picture nested deep in a sibling is found before the sibling's own fill. Ink in exactly the colour of the page ground (`body` or `html`) over a `body` or `html` that paints a raster image other than a small repeating texture takes no `1.0:1` verdict, as where the hit-test stacks are silent. A positioned `::before` or `::after` that covers the run, sits at a negative `z-index` that the capture says paints over its box (at `auto` or above it paints over the text, not under it), has no colour of its own (or one that an opaque layer filling the face hides), is not at an explicit `opacity` under 0.9, `visibility: hidden`, or moved, scaled or turned by a `transform`, `translate`, `scale` or `rotate` other than the identity, and paints only gradient layers whose bottom layer is opaque and fills the face, is the text's surface: the stops of the topmost layer that is opaque at every stop and fills the face (as the computed `background` names it: tiled with `repeat` or `round` on both axes at a size with no zero, or a size of `auto`, `cover`, `100% 100%`, `100% auto` or `auto 100%`, which for a gradient is the whole positioning area, at a percentage, keyword or zero position; a size or position holding any function such as `calc()` or `var()`, a `space` tile, or anything unparsed does not fill; the layers beneath a filling layer are hidden and not read), else the bottom layer's stops, plus each of them under every translucent stop above, printed as `gradient on <box>::after`. Where that face has a `filter` of its own, or its layers combine into more than 256 colours, it is still the surface (the box's own background is not scored in its place) but the verdict is withheld. A fade that the element pass's opacity fold scores (every faded box below the surface's box, the surface resolved and not a pseudo-element's, not on a page ground that paints a picture, the fold not giving up) is no longer a visual-contrast reason, so such text is not read from pixels on that account. A pixel-pass finding on routed text does not report a colour pair (ink, severity, ratio, median, bar and reasons) the page's pixel pass already reported.
  - **Round 10 labels, cards and colour tests** (corpus run 47; both engines unless noted). *Demo windows on landmarks.* A `section`, `article`, `header`, `footer` or `nav` is still never a demo frame by its scale, tilt, size, preview caption or sample caption, but it is a demo window by its traffic lights: its first child is a title bar (at least 60% of the box's width, within 24px of its top, 12px to 72px tall, the box at least twice that and at least 120px wide) holding a trio of dots that ends in the bar's leading half, and the box, the bar and every dot have rects. A landmark that holds the page's `h1`, or at least half the page's rendered text (`script`, `style`, `noscript` and `template` text left out; a walk past 2,000 nodes counts as holding the page), is the page's own content and is not a window. Text in such a window takes the framed-demo treatment (r5-p26). *gray-on-color.* A background that clears the channel-spread bar reads as colour only when its OKLCH lightness is under 0.9 or its OKLCH chroma is 0.09 or more, so cream `#feeed4` (L 0.955, C 0.038) and amber-100 `#fef3c7` (C 0.058) carry gray ink as an off-white does, while amber-300 `#fcd34d` (C 0.153) still reads as colour. The class form (both engines and the text engine's markup matcher) no longer counts a `bg-<hue>-50` or `bg-<hue>-100` utility as the coloured fill, since every Tailwind hue at those shades sits on the pale side of that line; `-200` and darker still count, and the side-tab stripe-child reader still sees the pale shades. *gradient-text.* The element form (both engines) and, in the URL engine, the ramp test behind it do not report a gradient whose colour stops all lie within 12 of the first stop on each channel and on alpha (scaled to 255), with at least two stops, and every stop readable: a hex colour or a colour function the parser resolves, after an optional first argument that sets direction, shape, position or colour space, with bare lengths read as transition hints. A `var()`, a named colour or a colour function the parser cannot read makes the value unread, and it reports as before. The stylesheet form stands down only when every gradient function read whole inside its 200-character window is one colour and no `)` in the window closes a function opened before it. The clipped-ancestor test (both engines) and the visual pass (URL engine) read a per-layer `background-clip: text, text` as clipped text; gradient-text's element form still reads only a single `text` clip (taste call r9-t4). *nested-cards* (URL engine). A box with no element child whose text renders on one recorded line, at most three line-heights tall, is a single-line label, whatever its padding; with no recorded lines it is read as before. A card's border side counts as an edge only when the border, composited over the box's own fill over the painted surface under the box, sits at least 8 from that surface on some channel. That surface is used only when the text the box holds reads on it at 3:1 or better (the box's own ink and up to eight elements with painted text of their own, in a 64-node walk that skips subtrees at `display: none`, `content-visibility: hidden` or opacity 0.01 and below, and own text at `visibility: hidden` or `collapse`); otherwise, and where no surface is read, every drawn border counts as before. Whether an inner box shows a border at all (r4-p16) is still read from its computed style. *Vanishing ink* (URL engine). When an element's `low-contrast` finding prints a ratio under 1.25:1 (`VANISHING_INK_RATIO`; one decimal at that range, so a printed `1.2` is a measured ratio under 1.25), its `tiny-text` and `undersized-ui-text` findings are dropped and `low-contrast` reports alone. Size findings stand when that `low-contrast` finding is turned off (`disabledRules`), ignored inline, handed to the pixel pass, withdrawn when a colour claim moves to a copy wholly on screen, or removed by the CLI after the scan: `ignoreRules`, or any `--scope` (`low-contrast` declares none). The CLI passes those rules to the engine for this one test; nothing else reads them. *Also:* class markers of page furniture (`label`, `nav`, `badge` and the rest) are not read off `html`, which holds script state flags, and are still read on `body` and below; a heading inside a `blockquote` or `q` sets no rhythm and counts toward no page minimum (URL engine); a heading level other than `h1` whose second most frequent size is a 1.25 step or more above its most frequent one and within 25% of it in count has no settled size and leaves the flat-type-hierarchy ladder; `organic-clip-path` reads a polygon whose vertices take two to four values on one axis and step evenly on the other, swinging back and forth by one amount at least three times, as a zigzag edge, and a `path()` whose straight sides lie on all four edges of the box its points and sampled curves span, joined by curves that each turn a corner, as a rounded rectangle, and reports the same snippet under the same selector once (inline styles report under `body`, so two inline shapes with the same vertex count report once); `wide-tracking` does not report text whose own letters, or whole run when it has none of its own, are at least half Han, kana or Hangul; and `overused-font` (URL engine) does not name a face that heads the stack of a page whose letters are mostly CJK when it sets under 5% of those letters.
  - **Round 11 labels, cards and exemptions** (corpus run 1004; URL engine unless noted). *Button classes.* A text element is drawn as a button when a class token is `btn` or ends in `-btn` or `_btn`, on its own box or on one of the two boxes above it that holds no more than 80 characters of text, and the text is not legal fine print (r5-p27). `tiny-text` reads such text as UI context, and `undersized-ui-text` reads it under its floor as functional text at any length (both engines). A wrapper such as `hero-btns` or `btn-group` names no button. *Short title bars.* A demo window's title bar may be 8px to 12px tall when it carries a trio of dots; a bar read by a preview caption still needs 12px. *Preformatted runs.* `tight-leading` skips text set in a monospace face with `white-space` `pre`, `pre-wrap` or `break-spaces` that sits in a `pre`, `code` or `samp` (itself included) or draws with box-drawing characters (U+2500 to U+257F); in the static engine, text in a `pre` whose recorded face is monospace or unset. *Purple text.* The large-text form of `ai-color-palette`'s purple text (20px or more, not an h1 to h3) needs HSL saturation 0.3 or more; h1 to h3 are unchanged (both engines). *Fade strips.* The gradient form does not report a gradient whose colour-painting stops all lie within 12 of the element's opaque `background-color` on each channel, with every stop opaque, when at least half of the points sampled over the box show that colour; a gradient the sampler cannot read reports as before. *Pulses* (both engines). A keyframes block is not a pulse when two steps' translations (the `translate*()` functions of `transform` summed with `translate`, per axis, as a px part and a percentage part: `none` and zero lengths read as 0, px, rem and em lengths into the px part, percentages into the percentage part, and any other value, such as `calc()`, making that axis of that step unread while the other axes still read) differ by 4px or more in the px part on an axis whose percentage part is the same in both steps, or when its steps declare two or more different `height` values and the same `width` (or none); steps that declare neither are not read. `pulsing-dot` also needs the box's longer side at most 1.5 times its shorter; its utility-class form, when the class list names both an unprefixed `w-<n>` and `h-<n>` (read at 4px a step), needs both sides 2px to 16px and the same 1.5 ratio. *Selectors in at-rule blocks* (both engines). `enclosingCssSelector` takes the selector after the last `}`, `;` or `{` before its block, so the first rule inside `@media` or `@supports` resolves to its selector, and the URL engine's pattern-pass selector match and paint gates apply to it (a selector that matches nothing drops the finding). A rule nested in a style rule (CSS nesting), or a selector holding `&`, resolves to none, so its finding stays unscoped as before. In the URL engine, `bounce-easing`, `dark-glow`, `radial-halo`, `repeating-stripes-gradient` and `codex-grid-background`, which report one candidate each, report the first candidate that passes those gates rather than the first in the text, so a candidate the page does not show hides no later one. Each rule offers the gates its first 16 candidates (`PICK_MAX_CANDIDATES`; the animation-name and `cubic-bezier` forms of `bounce-easing` 16 each) and reports none when none of them passes. *Unread curves* (URL engine; the static engine's pattern pass reports no `bounce-easing`). The `cubic-bezier` form of `bounce-easing` skips a curve that is the whole value of a custom property no `var(<name>)` in the style text reads, and reads the next curve; the style text is the page's inline styles and readable linked sheets, so a property read only from an unreadable sheet or from script counts as unread. *Code rows.* A monospace output block may be a box whose element children stand one under another, are each one run of text, hold all of its text, and more than half of which read as a line of code (they hold `=`, `{` or `}`, a call `name(...)`, end with `;` or open with `//`). *Scroller insets.* `edge-flush-cards` does not call a card flush on a side where its gap is 0 or more and the scroller sits 12px to 48px inside the viewport on that side with nothing drawn at its clip edge there: no fill, background image, visible shadow or visible border on the scroller, and no ancestor (up to six levels, below `body`) whose edge lies within 2px of the scroller's with a visible border on that side (a border shows when it is 0.5px or wider, not `none` or `hidden`, and its colour is not transparent), a fill, a background image or a visible shadow. *Wrapped rails.* `first-viewport-column-overflow` leaves out a column that reaches a `nav` or `role="navigation"` through at most four boxes that each hold one element child and no text of their own. *Consent.* Enzuzo's banner and modal are hidden as listed under Consent banners.
  - **Round 11 text against its box and face** (corpus run 1004; URL engine unless noted). *text-overflow.* `pre` and `code` are no longer skipped by tag; a code sample that scrolls on x, or sits under a box that does, is exempt as every scroll region is. For an element that is or sits in a `pre` or `code`, a box that hides or clips x overflow and cuts the spill (a terminal window, a clipped excerpt) ends it and is not harm, when the cut it makes ends more than a quarter em inside the viewport (a page shell such as `#__next` that hides x overflow cuts at the screen edge and does not count) and the box can clip the code (an absolute or fixed sample escapes a clip below its containing block); the spill is reported only when it is not cut and reaches the viewport edge (or past it) or meets another box. A box that generates a box, does not clip x overflow, and paints an edge a reader sees (a fill that differs from the surface under it, a background image, or a border with a width over 0, a drawing style and a colour that is not transparent) also reports when its own text rect overruns its content box (padding and border off the client box) by 15px or more and reaches past its border box by a quarter of its font size or 2px, whichever is more, after a positive `letter-spacing` is taken off the end the text runs past; the text rect has to cross the content box, so text indented wholly out of it is not read. The snippet then gives that reach past the border box (`a.btn overflows its box by 6px`). This is read only when the `scrollWidth` test does not fire; a spill that test measures into free space stays quiet whatever the box paints (r4-p19). A box that is, or sits under, a box with a running animation or transition setting `width`, `min-width`, `max-width` or an inline-size longhand, traced to an end (every animation whose keyframes set it is finite, on the document timeline and not paused, or a transition names it), is not at rest and reports no overflow. *overused-font* (both engines). A primary face is looked up on the list with spaces, hyphens and underscores ignored and counted, named and checked for its brand domain under the list's spelling, so `GeistSans` and `Geist Sans` are one face (`Primary font: geist sans`). *icon-tile-stack.* A tile whose own text is blank and whose one element child has no children and only emoji for text holds an icon; that child's rect is the icon's for the fills-the-tile test. *numbered-section-labels.* A label that reads `#` then one or two digits, alone or followed by a space and more text (`#1 Connect`, at most 40 characters, index 40 or less), is a numbered label, unless the word after the number is a ranking word (`rated`, `ranked`, `rank`, `best`, `top`, `in`, `pick`, `choice`, `overall`, `seller`, `bestseller`, `selling`, `voted`, `place`, `trending`) or every `#` label on the page carries the same word after its number; the shared parser is unchanged. For every numbered label, with or without the `#`, an `article` is a card context only when a link, button, list item, `listitem` or `option` holds it, or a box that holds the label is drawn (card-like, a fill that differs from the surface under it, a shadow, or a border drawn on three sides or more): from the heading's parent up to the article, then up to 3 wrappers past it whose only element child is the box below; the first wrapper that holds more than that still counts when it is drawn and holds no other `article` (an image over the article). A list item, link or button that is the closest card context stays one whatever it paints, and `kicker-above-heading` is unchanged. *body-text-viewport-edge* (element and page-level forms). Only a `<header>` no taller than `innerHeight` exempts the text in it; a taller one (a page that wraps its whole first screen in `<header>`) is measured. A header whose height or the viewport's is not measured still exempts.
  - **Round 12 the box a rule measures** (corpus run 1006; URL engine unless noted). *justified-text.* An element at `display: inline` is measured by the width of its nearest ancestor that is not `inline` or `contents`, and reports only when that block has no direct text of its own (over 10 characters); a link inheriting `justify` in a wide paragraph is not a narrow column. Text must render on two lines or more, counted from its line pitch over the text it sets (for a block, its own text and its inline children's; for an inline run, its own text rect); a capture with no text rect keeps the text. The static engine (both engines) skips text whose trimmed length fits one line of its declared measure (`width / (font-size × 0.5)` characters). *cramped-padding.* The vertical inset between a box's own text and its edges is measured from the glyph band (each line's em box centred on its content area) instead of the Range content area, as the small-chip path already did; the snippet prints that inset. The flush form is unchanged. *body-text-viewport-edge* (element and page-level forms). A wrapped block filling 90% of its content box is measured at the box's edges unless it is not justified and its glyphs keep 16px on both sides of the viewport, where the glyphs are measured. The scroller test walks the flat tree (`flat_parent`), so a horizontal scroller in a component's shadow tree cuts the items slotted into it; a transform-moved track is still looked for on the light tree only. *shape-assembled-illustration* (both engines). A `width` or `height` in `em` or `rem` is read at 16px per unit instead of falling back to the viewBox, and a scene needs `rect`, `circle`, `ellipse` and `polygon` to make up at least a quarter of its drawn shapes (those plus `path`, `line`, `polyline`). *text-overflow.* An absolutely positioned `::before` or `::after` whose `content` is one quoted character is measured, not taken as unmeasured, when its box, placed from its containing block by its pixel offsets and size and moved by its transform (bounds about its centre), lies within its host's border box (half a pixel of slack): a rotated slash striking a size swatch. *flat-type-hierarchy.* A body element (`p`, `li`, `td`, `th`, `dd`, `blockquote`, `figcaption`) that sets one or two non-space characters of its own (its text less the text of type-sampled descendants, `script`, `style`, `template` and `noscript`) is not sampled; one that sets none keeps its sample. *text-occlusion.* Text answering a probe over other text does not count when both are in flow under their nearest common ancestor (no box between either and it is positioned other than `relative` with every offset recorded as `auto` or 0, transformed, floated or given a negative margin), their line bands (each line's content area grown or shrunk to its line pitch) do not overlap by more than 0.5px, and their em boxes overlap by at most 15% of the smaller font size. A decorated box with no fill over 0.6 alpha, no `background-image` and no `backdrop-filter`, that is not an `input`, `textarea`, `select` or `button`, covers a point only within its border band (border width plus 1px from its rect's edges), or where a box holding it but not the covered text paints a fill over 0.6 alpha at that point, and none when its own `transform` is not a pure translation. *nested-cards.* An inner box is not a card when its parent scrolls on x, or its element children are three or more `a`, `button` or `role=link|button|tab` elements, none drawn as a card and none taller than 64px, set in one row (each overlapping the first's vertical span by half the smaller height). A box running along two adjacent edges of the outer card's padding box (within 1.5px) is part of it when the outer, or a box between, clips overflow on any axis and the inner box spans that box's full width or height (within 1.5px) while falling short of it on the other axis by more than 1.5px.
  - **Round 11 contrast: the pixel pass and the gates before it** (corpus run 1004; URL engine unless noted). The pixel pass still hides a candidate's `text-shadow` with its glyphs. Where that reading is refused as a box repainted between captures, the candidate is not outlined (the `text outline` reason or routing), and every layer of its `text-shadow`, composited over its ink, sits at 2:1 or more from the ink (`haloSeparates` on the candidate), the pass takes a second reading with the shadow kept and reads the glyphs over it; a glow in the ink's own colour, a drop shadow under grey ink, and a layer without its own colour are never kept. A `backdrop-filter` on a box whose own `background-color` is at least 0.8 opaque (and under 0.95, where the existing "under an opaque fill" reason applies) gives the reason `backdrop filter under a near-opaque fill`, which the pixel pass reads and the sampled pass refuses; the reason walk continues past that box; a backdrop filter under a thinner fill still blocks both. In `low-contrast`'s element pass, the hit-test stacks take the innermost box between the text and the walk's host whose fill is at least 0.95 opaque as the surface the text sits on (the colour scored is unchanged: the walk still folds such a fill over what lies below). On the link and span path a surface in exactly the ink's colour keeps its verdict only where the full pass's guard does not hold (the stacks confirm the surface and the page ground carries no picture), the surface is not the page ground, and the text is the whole visible content of the `a`, `button` or `[role=button]` around it (the control's text is the element's text, and no other box in it that does not hold the element is an `svg`, `img`, `picture`, `canvas`, `video`, `i`, `object` or `embed` or paints a fill, a background image or a border; inside the element, an `svg`, `img`, `picture`, `canvas`, `video`, `object` or `embed` counts whatever text it holds, and any other such box counts only when it holds no text; a box with no rendered area or at `visibility: hidden` or `collapse` never counts); every other such pair is dropped, and the static engine still drops every one. A box at an opacity of 0.02 or less whose only animation setting `opacity` is running once on the document timeline, holds its end frame (`forwards` or `both`), uses `replace` composition, and ends within 60 seconds of delay plus duration counts at its end frame's opacity wherever the Round 10 scroll-held opacity counts (the opacity gate and fold, the Text and Box paint gates, `painted_at_capture` and the page checks that ask it, the text-box and shown-across tests). A box below full opacity whose only animation setting `opacity` is `infinite`, paused, and has a keyframe at 0.1 or less is not at rest: the opacity fold does not blend it and the pixel pass does not read it. Text whose computed font size is at least 120px, or at least three times the page's largest `h1` and at least 80px, whose settled effective opacity (a scroll-held box, or one a timed entrance moves at capture at any captured value, at its end frame; a box whose opacity an animation or transition moves toward an end the capture cannot read, or that a paused loop holds, is not faint) times its colour's alpha is 0.25 or less, which is not a control's whole label, and whose words run together equal a site name (the host name without `www.`, with and without its last label; `og:site_name`; `application-name`; where neither meta names the site, the title when it has one part between `|`, `·`, `•`, `—`, `–` and `:`, and otherwise only the parts whose letters and digits, lower-cased and run together, are 3 or more characters inside the host name's labels without its last one, read the same way) or open one word for word, is a decorative wordmark (taste call r3-04's advisory); the static engine reads no layout and never applies it.
  - **Round 12 labels beside headings and labels that are code** (corpus run 1006; URL engine unless noted). *Code tokens.* The ink form of `ai-color-palette` skips an inline run set in a monospace face (the first family, as `tight-leading` reads it) at `white-space` `pre`, `pre-wrap` or `break-spaces` when its parent holds its own text, or another inline element with text, in a colour more than 24 away summed over the channels (or 0.1 in alpha): a syntax highlighter's keywords supply no tell hue. A one-colour monospace line, a `pre-line` run and a block are read as before. *Kickers beside a heading.* `kicker-above-heading` measures the box that sets the label's text (the label, or the one child that holds its text, at most three levels down) against the heading: a label whose box starts at or below the heading's bottom (within 1px), or lies wholly to one side of the heading (within 1px) and ends lower than the heading's top plus half its own height, is not a kicker, whatever wrappers the collector climbed; a label with no box keeps the candidate. The proximity test the numbered-label and hero rules share is unchanged. *Heading clusters.* `heading-rhythm` folds a line above the heading into the heading's cluster when it opens its group (no box laid out before it), touches the heading (a gap of 2px or less), holds 80 characters or fewer, renders on one recorded line (all line boxes within 2px of one top; a capture with no recorded lines folds nothing this way) and is set no larger than the heading, however it is styled; and it folds an eyebrow that opens its group past the 28px limit, up to 64px, when its gap to the heading is smaller than the heading's gap to the content below. *Composer fields.* `nested-cards` reads a box as a field when its element children are fields that draw a text box (`textarea`, `select`, or an `input` whose `type` is not `hidden`, `checkbox`, `radio`, `submit`, `button`, `image` or `reset`; at least one) plus any number of hidden inputs and 1px-or-smaller boxes clipped by `clip` or `clip-path`, and at most one button row (a `button` or `[role=button]`, or a box whose element children are all such buttons) that holds no heading and at most 20 characters of text. *Furniture classes* (both engines). A class names page furniture when one of its tokens has a part (split on `-`, `_`, `:`, `/`, `.` and lower-to-upper case changes, lower-cased, with everything but letters and digits removed, so `bg-[var(--navy)]` reads `navy`) that starts with `meta`, `label`, `badge`, `chip`, `pill`, `tag`, `kicker`, `eyebrow`, `breadcrumb`, `timestamp`, `category`, `caption` or `nav`, or ends with one of them or its plural (`nav` also as `navi` or `navigation`, `category` as `categories`); `pillar`, `pillars` and `navy` name none. A word inside a part (`stage`, `instagram`, `vantagens`, `percentage`, `unavailable`) no longer counts.
  - **Round 12 contrast surfaces** (corpus run 1006; URL engine unless noted). *dark-glow.* A shadow layer cast with an offset whose HSL lightness is under 0.25, whose relative luminance is under the surface's, and whose hue is within 50 degrees of a chromatic surface's lifts it by nothing (shade, not light); every other layer, a zero-offset halo and a dark shadow in a hue of its own included, keeps the channel distance. Where the walk under the element ends on an ancestor gradient whose stops are all opaque (no `url()` layer with it), the lift test runs against each stop and the glow is passed over only where it lifts none of them to the floor; a translucent stop leaves the floor to decide alone, as before. *Stack walk.* A box whose `background-clip` (or `-webkit-background-clip`) clips every layer to its text paints no surface for the text of the box holding it: the walk reads it as `no readable background` and goes on beneath it. A translucent pixel of a `url()` background is laid over the same box's own `background-color` (alpha over 0.05) before the walk goes on, with method `canvas-background-image+fill`: opaque where the fill is, still translucent (the two composited) where it is not. The in-page bundle's `vc_css_url_finish` takes the node handle first. *Gradient text.* Text whose glyph fill is transparent (`color` at alpha 0.02 or less, or a transparent `-webkit-text-fill-color`) under `background-clip: text` with a gradient whose stops are all opaque and readable (no `url()` layer) is scored by its best stop against the surface under its parent, `N:1 (need T:1) — gradient text, best stop #stop on #ground`, and reports only where even that stop fails. The surface has to be one flat colour the text-surface walk resolves from the parent (no gradient host or samples, no background image on the host), the structural climb from the parent over the text has to answer with an ancestor's own fill (from the element itself its own clipped gradient answers first, and a dark backdrop laid beneath it is never seen), the stacks must not see it covered, and the glyphs carry no `text-shadow` or outline. Near-bar ratios report as advisory, and decorative text as advisory. Gradient grounds are not scored (taste call r10-t4).
  - **Round 13 resting states** (craft run 1009; URL engine). *low-contrast.* A box below full opacity whose `style` attribute sets `opacity` is not at rest (the opacity fold does not blend it and the pixel pass does not read it) when the attribute also sets `transform`, `translate`, `scale` or `rotate`, the box is displaced and its `will-change` names `opacity`, `transform`, `translate`, `scale` or `rotate` (an animation library's start values); or when all of these hold: its own opacity is under 0.5; it declares an `opacity` transition; it is not a control, sits in none and holds none (below); no same-parent sibling of its tag sits at opacity 1 carrying every class token of the box plus exactly one more; and either the attribute's `opacity` value reads a custom property inside `max(`, `min(`, `clamp(` or `calc(`, or reads one a `style` attribute on the box or an ancestor sets, or the box is one of at least three boxes of its tag and class list parked at the same inline opacity among its siblings or its parent's siblings' children while another element of its tag on the page sits at opacity 1 with an `opacity` transition, carrying every class token of the box plus exactly one more. Text whose fold passes through a box at rest under 0.5 opacity that declares an `opacity` transition and has such a sibling at opacity 1 (a stepper's inactive items beside the active one, a checkout stepper's next steps) reports its `low-contrast` verdict as advisory and claims no colour pair for the page's failing copies, unless that box, or a box between it and the text, is a control, which keeps its severity (taste call r13-t1). A control is a button, field or tab by tag or role, an `a` with `href`, a box carrying `aria-selected`, role `link`, `option`, `menuitem`, `menuitemradio` or `treeitem`, or a box whose computed `cursor` is `pointer` while its parent's is not and whose text is at most 80 UTF-16 units. The snapshot records computed `cursor`; a recording made before reads it as empty. The static engine reads no transition, transform or `will-change` and applies neither.
  - **Round 13 contrast grounds** (corpus craft run 1009, rows 4 to 7; URL engine unless noted). *Pseudo-elements placed by their shape.* A positioned `::before` or `::after` whose transform skews, turns or scales it is placed under a run for certain where the parallelogram it paints covers the run (its top, middle and bottom edges sampled at least every 4px, within 1px) with its `transform-origin` at its centre and at each corner of its laid-out box, since the capture does not record the origin; one whose bounds cover the run but whose shape does not, for any of those origins, stays uncertain, as before. A pseudo with a `scale` or `rotate` property other than the identity is not placed. Where neither pseudo covers the run alone, the capture places both (2D transforms, pixel sizes and offsets), the two together cover it about their centres, and neither paints beneath an opaque fill, they are one ground (panic.com's chevron sections, two halves sheared apart at `z-index: -1`), certain only where they cover it for every origin: a picture in either is a picture, the same paint in both is that paint, and anything else is paint the walk cannot read. A pseudo with a recorded `mask-image` paints what the walk cannot read, whatever its fill or gradient. A pseudo's single gradient layer over its own opaque fill, at `opacity` 1 or unrecorded and with no `filter`, is read as the colours its stops make composited over that fill, where the pseudo is placed for certain, its order is known, and every channel that moves by more than 8 runs one way along the stops; otherwise it stays paint the walk cannot read.
