A pseudo's fill colour is a solid ground only at its alpha times the pseudo's `opacity` of 0.9 or more; under that it is paint the walk cannot read. A pseudo with a `filter` other than `none` paints a fill the walk cannot read. Where both pseudo-elements cover the run, the one painted last (higher `z-index`, else `::after`) is the ground where it is opaque (a fill, a picture, a gradient over its own fill), and otherwise the ground is paint the walk cannot read: a pseudo under an opaque one never stands as the ground. Where the hit-test stacks confirm the walk's surface, such a pseudo on an ancestor of the text still decides, as a layer with `pointer-events: none` does: a stack lists no pseudo-element and reads past the text's ancestors. *Hover faces.* The contrast surface read from a pseudo-element's opaque fill counts an explicit `opacity: 0` as 0 (only an unrecorded opacity counts as 1), so a hover face parked out of sight is not the ground. *Clipped glyphs* (both engines for the first). The full and SAFE_TAGS contrast paths take no verdict on text whose `background-clip` (or `-webkit-background-clip`) clips every layer to the text (`text, text`), as for `text`; `gradient-text` is unchanged. In the URL engine the full path also takes none where the glyphs' fill is transparent (`-webkit-text-fill-color`, else `color` where no fill is recorded, at an alpha of 0.02 or less), as a word inside a gradient-clipped heading inherits. *Blended echoes.* A run is handed to the pixels as `unread layer` where a later sibling of it or of one of its first three ancestors, not at a negative `z-index`, holds within its first 40 boxes one with the same direct text (whitespace collapsed) whose text box is within 4px of the run's on every edge, painted (not `visibility: hidden`, effective opacity over 0.05), with a `mix-blend-mode` other than `normal` on it or on a box between it and that sibling, or a translucent ink (alpha under 0.98); a copy whose ink alpha is 0.02 or less paints no glyph and does not count. An opaque copy at `normal` over a run hides it and the run is read as before.
  - **Round 14 contrast grounds** (corpus run 1010, rows 8 and 10; URL engine unless noted). *Parked pseudo faces.* The contrast surface read from a pseudo-element's opaque fill, and the gradient face reader, refuse a face at `visibility: hidden`, and a face whose `transform`, `translate`, `rotate` and `scale` (in that CSS order) move it off the text: where the capture places the face (absolute, pixel offsets and size, a known containing block), the parallelogram it paints has to cover the text run for every `transform-origin` inside its box; where it does not, the transform is read alone and keeps the face only where it moves it by at most half its size on each axis, scales it by 0.5 or more on each axis and turns it by at most 15 degrees. A 3D transform or an unreadable value refuses. A sweep `::before` parked a full width off its button inside `overflow: hidden` is not the label's ground at rest; a centred chip (`left: 50%; translate(-50%, -50%)`) or one turned 2deg, skewed 10deg or scaled 1.04 still is. *Dot textures.* Where a box with an opaque fill also paints background layers that are all sparse `radial-gradient` dots, the fill is the surface and the dots are not sampled. A layer is such a dot where it is a circle (written `circle`, or the default shape in a square cell), its coloured stops (alpha over 0.05) come first and the rest are transparent, every stop position is in pixels, the pattern repeats in cells of at most 64px on both axes, it has a transparent stop and a stop placed within 3px (or most of its stops are transparent), and the dot's area (radius the first transparent stop's position, or the last coloured one's if larger) over the cell's, times its strongest alpha, is at most 0.15. A pinhole mesh (clear centre), a dense halftone, grid lines drawn with `linear-gradient` hairlines, and opaque stripes or checkers are still sampled. *Underlines parked at zero size* (pixel pass). In `css_plan`, a gradient layer drawn at zero size on either axis, or at most 3px tall or wide on an axis the layer does not repeat along (a hover underline at `background-size: 0 1px`, or drawn at `100% 1px no-repeat`), paints no surface and is left out, the other layers keeping their places in the size, position and repeat lists; where every layer is such a layer, the walk reads the box's fill, or goes on beneath it. *Scrims under translucent panels.* Where the element pass reads a verdict against unread paint (`unread_verdict`), the climb behind that reading also takes a background colour with alpha over 0.1 and under 0.9, on a box that is nobody's ancestor, covers the run and carries no mask or `clip-path`, as a layer at its alpha, and reads down through it, where the climb the other readers share answered with a fill or gradient (over a picture the pixels still answer): a dialog panel at `rgba(235, 230, 224, 0.3)` over a sibling `bg-black/80` scrim is read on the scrim, not on the pale page beneath both. The climb every other reader asks is unchanged. *Ghost words* (taste call r14-t3). Text at 120px or more that is `aria-hidden="true"` (itself or a box it sits in), sets `pointer-events: none` on itself (its parent takes pointer events), or sits in a box at a negative `z-index` where that applies (a positioned box, or a flex or grid item), that is not a control's label or inside one, whose words do not run word for word inside a visually hidden heading on the page or the document title, and that shows at a settled opacity times ink alpha of 0.25 or less, or whose ink stands at 1.5:1 or less from the ground the element pass read, reports `low-contrast` as advisory whatever it says; the site-name test is the wordmark's alone. The pixel pass reads the opacity arm only. The static engine reads no layout and applies none of these.
  - **Round 13 legal blocks** (corpus run 1009; both engines; decision 3). Fine print (taste call r5-p27: text set under 15px, not a heading, not in a `label`, holding no form control and with no checkbox or radio beside it) splits in two tiers. A *legal block* carries a class or id token naming `legal`, `disclaimer`, `footnote`, `fineprint`/`fine-print`, `smallprint`/`small-print` (a whole word, split at `-`, `_` and a lower-to-upper case step, the last word optionally plural) or the plural `disclosures` on the element or up to three levels up (stopping at `html`, `body`, `main`, `article` and `form`); the walk climbs past a `small` or a weaker marker to reach it. A `footnote` right after `type`, `text`, `typography`, `font` or `fs` in the same token (`text-footnote`, a type size) does not count. A legal block reports no `line-length` and no `tiny-text`, and stays advisory under `tight-leading`. The text is demoted to the weaker tier when any marked box in that reach is, or has a child that is, a `details`, a `summary` or an element with `aria-expanded` (an accordion panel), and when the outermost marked box is a policy page's body: it has no `footer` or `role="contentinfo"` ancestor, holds 2000 or more non-space characters, and holds at least half of the page's. Both counts skip a subtree with `display: none`, `aria-hidden="true"`, or a `hidden` attribute that no author `display` undoes (`hidden="until-found"` always counts as hidden). The weaker tier (a `small` element, a `copyright`, `terms` or singular `disclosure` token, a type-size `footnote`, or a block of 60 characters or more that opens with an asterisk or a footnote mark) reports as advisory under the three rules, as before. `undersized-ui-text` reads neither tier: a link or `summary` inside a legal block keeps its 11px floor and its severity. A legal block still counts as fine print for the button-class test (Round 11), so a `-btn` class does not make its text UI.
  - **Round 11 deferred edge cases** (#1076; URL engine unless noted). *At-rule headers* (both engines). `enclosingCssSelector` reads each enclosing block's header from the last `}`, `;` or `{` outside a quoted string (a newline ends an unterminated one), so a quoted brace or semicolon in an `@supports` condition does not cut the header short and the rule inside keeps its selector. *First candidates that paint.* A `dark-glow`, `radial-halo` or `codex-grid-background` candidate passes the pattern pass's gates only when an element its selector matches computes what it declares, as `repeating-stripes-gradient` already did: the shadow property its snippet names not `none` (a glow), a `background-image` holding `radial-gradient` (a halo) or `gradient` (a grid). A part of the selector list that names `::before` or `::after` answers yes for the three, since the capture does not record those boxes' shadows or a `content: ''` box; a value the capture did not record answers yes. A rule inside an `@media` or `@supports` block that does not apply, or one a later rule overrides, therefore hides no later candidate the page shows; `codex-grid-background` offers every grid block in source order, not only the first. *Code in a scrolling page shell.* For an element that is or sits in a `pre` or `code`, an ancestor that scrolls on x and is the page shell (left edge within 1px of 0, at least 98% of `innerWidth` wide, top within 1px of 0 and at least 98% of the root's height) does not exempt it from `text-overflow`; the code tests above decide, so a line that runs past the screen edge reports. *Scaled 1px text.* The paint gate's sub-1px descendant test and `text-occlusion`'s top-text and victim tests read a computed font size of 1px or less at its drawn size (the same `drawn_enlargement` the paint gate's own test uses), so text a transform or `scale` draws above 1px counts as text; the cramped-padding flush form already keeps such text by its measured rect.
  - **Round 13 small measurement defects** (craft run 1009; URL engine unless noted). *Paint gates.* `buried-raster`'s gate also drops an image with no area whose content cannot show past its edge (a 0x0 tracking pixel). Below the fold, text is covered when its first ancestor-or-self with an applying `z-index` (positioned, or a flex or grid item) has a sibling, earlier or later, at a strictly higher `z-index` holding an opaque fill over all of the text; `straggler` and `unbalanced-heading` join the rules covered text does not report. The paint walk reads a `mask-image` that is one `linear-gradient` along an axis (`to bottom`/`180deg`, `to top`/`0deg`, `to right`/`90deg`, `to left`/`270deg`, or none) with `px` or `%` stops, on the element and every ancestor: where the combined alpha at the middle of the text (or box) is under 0.2 the element is not painted; any other mask, and a mask on a box that scrolls along the gradient's axis, is taken as painted. A single line whose em box shows less than half inside the boxes that hide vertical overflow is not painted for the text rules, unless a box on the way scrolls on y or is translated on y. `nested-cards` passes the Box paint gate. *Line breaks.* `straggler` and `unbalanced-heading` do not judge lines that run past a line clamp (its content edge) or a box clipping y (its padding edge), on the element or within 4 ancestors. *heading-rhythm* skips a run-in heading: computed `display: inline`, followed on its line by running text of 20 characters or more (a text node, or an inline-level sibling whose box starts above the heading's bottom). *line-length* leaves out the lines and characters of a nested `ul`, `ol`, `dl` or `menu` laid out as a block. *tight-leading* (taste call r13-t3): text at 20px or more over three rendered lines or fewer at a ratio of 1.15 or more keeps its leading, and so does a label in capitals at a ratio of 1.0 or more: `text-transform: uppercase` over three rendered lines or fewer (a line clamp counts at most its clamp), or typed in capitals under 80 characters. The static engine reads the transform alone, bounded to under 80 characters. *gradient-text*: a loading shimmer (first and last stops the text colour within 12 per channel, three stops or more, `background-size` wider than 100% on x, an `infinite` animation not `paused` whose keyframes move `background-position`, and the text colour held solid over half the layer or more, every stop a colour at a `%` position or none) reports nothing, its stylesheet form included; a gradient whose stops share one RGB and differ only in alpha reports as advisory (taste call r13-t6). *cramped-padding*: text a clip cuts by more than half a pixel is cut on that side, on x and on y; a box that scrolls on y and holds more than it shows cuts the line within 4px of its bottom edge; under a lone rule of 1px or less (no rule on the opposite side, no fill) the text is measured to its glyph band. *shape-assembled-illustration* (both engines): an SVG whose `<rect>`s sharing one width or height (within 1px), the other side varying, are half or more of its primitives, with fewer than a quarter circles, ellipses or polygons, is a bar series and reports nothing; an SVG of more than 400 primitives is not read as one. *monotonous-spacing* (both engines, and the regex engine) stands down when the dominant value is 0. *script-error*: an error thrown from `cdn.vector.co` is ad tech (`Vector`, advisory), and an `AbortError` from an interrupted `play()` reports as advisory with its snippet unchanged.
  - **Round 14 page-level premises** (corpus run 1010, rows 4, 5, 6, 11, 14 and 17, and taste call r12-t2; URL engine unless noted). *The page's ground.* Where neither `html` nor `body` paints a fill or an image, the page shows the browser's white canvas and its root reads light, unless any of these holds, and then the stylesheet decides as before: the root or body computes a `color-scheme` containing `dark`, or a `<meta name="color-scheme">` content contains `dark`; a `::before` or `::after` of `html` or `body` generates content and paints a fill or a background image; an element at `position: fixed` or `absolute` painted at capture covers 90% of the viewport on both axes with a fill over 0.5 alpha, a background image, or by its tag (`canvas`, `video`, `img`, `iframe`); in-flow boxes spanning 95% of the body's width that paint a fill over 0.5 alpha at a relative luminance under 0.1, or a background image, cover half the page's height or more, looked for through transparent boxes to 8 levels, with `display: contents` boxes and transparent boxes of zero height passed through at no level, and over at most 4,000 boxes; or the characters of the page's visible text set at a relative luminance of 0.4 or more are as many as the darker ones, or there are none. A root fill under a background image made only of gradients whose stops are all at 0.1 alpha or less is read as the fill. A `dark-glow` or `radial-halo` page form that stands on the dark surfaces under its elements while the root reads light says `on dark background` instead of `on dark page`. *Script text.* The URL engine's stylesheet and class corpora leave out the text inside every `<script>` element, so a `style` attribute or `<style>` block written in a JS string reports nothing. A `<script` inside an HTML comment or a `<style>` block is not a script, a script ends only at `</script` followed by white space, `>` or `/`, and a script with no such end keeps its text. *Dark declarations* (both engines). A `background` or `background-color` the dark-stylesheet test matches (a hex leading with `#00` to `#23`, a short hex leading with `0` or `1`, an `rgb()` of one- or two-digit channels) counts only where that colour's relative luminance is under 0.1, or where it cannot be parsed. *marquee.* A stylesheet form whose every selector part names a `::before` or `::after` asks each part's own pseudo-element of that part's own hosts, not the hosts, whether it carries content (where a part cannot be split off or resolved, every named pseudo-element of every matched host is asked): a `content` that is not empty (`none`, `normal`, `""`), or a `url()` background. *side-tab and border-accent-on-rounded* (both engines). A box whose width and height are within 1.1 of each other and whose four corner radii are each at least half the shorter side, less 0.5px, is a circle and reports neither rule; a `border-radius` with a vertical part (`/`) is never read as a circle, and the static engine reads the size only from `width` and `height` declared in px, adding the border on each side (in px, or `0`), with a percentage radius read against that size; a box with padding on any side (whose border box the static cascade cannot know without `box-sizing`), or a length in another unit, skips the circle test. `border-accent-on-rounded` is dropped, as `side-tab` already was, on a box the text paint gate reads as not painted at capture. *pulsing-dot* (both engines). Keyframes whose only scales in `transform` are all `scaleX()` or all `scaleY()`, with no `scale` property, are not a pulse by their scale (a blink); opacity or a `box-shadow` still makes them one. Keyframes travel, and are not a pulse, when two steps give one-sided offset values (`left`, `right`, `top`, `bottom`, `inset-inline-start`, `inset-inline-end`, `inset-block-start`, `inset-block-end`, `margin-left`, `margin-right`, `margin-top`, `margin-bottom`, `margin-inline-start`, `margin-inline-end`, `margin-block-start`, `margin-block-end`) 4px or more apart (a zero in any unit is 0px), or differing values that cannot both be read in px, unless the same two steps also change `width` or `height`, or the offset on the opposite side, which keeps a growing ripple centred. The shorthands that set every side (`inset`, `margin`) are not read. *ai-color-palette.* A gradient whose stops all share one RGB colour, every stop at 0.75 alpha or more, is that colour laid flat and reports nothing. *overused-font* (taste call r12-t2). The snapshot records `fontFaces`, each face in `document.fonts` as `[family, status]` with edge quotes removed (`null` where the set cannot be read; absent in older captures). It also records `renderedFaces`: for each face (at most 40) heading the computed `font-family` of an element holding a text node (the first 5,000 text nodes), `[face, drawn]`, where drawn means a canvas measures a probe string at 72px differently in `"<face>", <generic>` than in `<generic>` alone for at least one of `monospace`, `serif` and `sans-serif`; a face installed on the system or loaded by the page is drawn (`null` without a 2D canvas; absent in older captures). The finding stands down when the capture recorded both lists, the named face is not `arial`, `helvetica` or `roboto`, every measured entry for it is not drawn (and there is one), and no face of that family (matched as the overused-font list spells it) is listed in `document.fonts` at a status other than `error`. A face drawn from the capture machine's own fonts, a face at `loading` or `unloaded`, and a capture without either list keep the finding. The in-page bundle (live mode, the extension's page reads) asks the page for the same two lists; the static engine keeps reporting.
  - **The page's own built-ins** (Rust engine). Before any page script runs, every document the URL engine loads gets the built-ins the engine's page scripts use (`Map`, `Set`, `Array.from`, `JSON.stringify`, ...) captured from its untouched globals, so a page that declares its own global `Map` or reassigns `Array.from` still scans. The stash is a page-visible, non-enumerable `window.__impeccableNatives`, so a page can detect the scan, and a page script declaring `let __impeccableNatives` throws.
  - **Round 13: mockups the page declares** (corpus run 1009, decision 1; URL engine and in-page bundle, `crates/core/src/browser/hidden_mockup.rs`; the static engine reads no layout and never applies it). An element carrying `inert` or `aria-hidden="true"` is a declared mockup when all of these hold: its box is at least 120x80px; it is not `html`, `body`, a landmark or sectioning tag, `form`, `dialog`, `li`, a table part, `details` or `summary`; it has no role other than `presentation`, `none`, `img`, `figure` or `group`, and no `aria-modal` or `data-aria-hidden`; it is not `position: fixed` or `sticky`; no class or id part names a copy, slide, menu, popup, modal, drawer, dialog, tooltip, overlay, nav, sidebar, panel, tab, accordion, intro, loader, banner or consent box; no heading (`h1` to `h6`), `p`, `a`, `button`, `label`, `summary`, `legend`, `caption`, `figcaption`, `blockquote` or `q` holds it; it holds three or more rendered elements with text of their own (text under `display: none` or `visibility: hidden` is no run), no `h1`, at most 3000 nodes and under half of the page's rendered characters (text under `display: none` or `visibility: hidden` is not counted); it is not working UI (two or more form fields, a field with a `label`, or half or more of its text runs inside links with an `href`); no ancestor's `aria-label` contains its characters (the lines of a split-text headline); no sibling of the same tag that wears every class it wears (or neither has a class) is shown and not itself hidden, and no sibling carries the same characters; no shown, unhidden sibling carries text for screen readers only (visually hidden, or a box 1px or less on a side) unless that text describes a picture (demo, illustration, interface, screenshot, graphic, animation, preview, image, video, visual, diagram, shows, showing, displayed); and no shown dialog (`aria-modal="true"`, `role="dialog"` or `"alertdialog"`, an open `<dialog>`) that is not itself hidden sits outside it. Inside a declared mockup `low-contrast`, `nested-cards`, `tiny-text`, `undersized-ui-text`, `cramped-padding` and `clipped-overflow-container` report nothing (a `low-contrast` verdict there claims no colour pair, so the same pair outside still reports), its text takes no slot of either visual-contrast candidate budget, so the pixel pass reads none of it, and `skipped-heading` reads the outline as if its headings were not there. Under a box of at least 120x80px whose `aria-label` names a screenshot, screen capture, illustration or mockup, or says `preview of` (not a control, no role outside the list above), those six rules report as advisory (taste call r13-t5): such a `low-contrast` verdict claims only the advisory colour pairs, so the page's own text wearing the same pair still reports as a warning, and its visual-contrast candidates carry `decorative`, so a pixel-pass verdict on them is advisory too.
  - **Round 14 structure and labels** (corpus run 1010, observations-1010 branch D; URL engine unless noted). *heading-rhythm.* An empty spacer box whose only paint is an opaque background colour equal to the nearest painted background behind it (the canvas when none is) is space, as an unpainted spacer is: a row painted the colour of its band no longer stands as the block above a heading. A spacer whose fill differs, that sits under an ancestor painted with a background image band (the band's colour is not read, and the image is read before the ancestor's own fill), or that carries a background image band, a bottom border or a shadow, is still a block. *nested-cards.* A box from the viewport's left edge across the root's client width (`innerWidth` where the root recorded none) that covers at least 75% of the document's height (the root's rect height or recorded `scrollHeight`, whichever is larger), and shows no edge of its own (no border, no outline, no outer shadow layer above alpha 0.05, no fill that differs from the surface under it), is the page, not an outer card: the climb to an inner card's outer card passes over it. *cramped-padding.* A fill runs on past a side, and that side draws no edge, when a box of the same fill, painted at capture, abuts it there across 90% of the side: a sibling, as before, or a box in a sibling of one of the band's first four ancestors, found by following boxes that reach that side's line up to six levels down (at most 200 boxes per side); the walk runs only for a side that would otherwise report. *em-dash-overuse* (all engines). A run of exactly two U+2014 marks (the Chinese paired dash) counts as one dash; other runs count each mark. In the URL engine the count also leaves out list separators: the first dash of each rendered `li` (holding no `li` of its own) whose text, past any leading bullet or check glyph, opens with a term of 1 to 6 words and at most 60 characters with no sentence end (`.`, `!`, `?`, `。`, `！`, `？`) followed by the dash and more text, where the term reads as a label (it is the whole text of an element in the item, or the text after the dash opens with a letter that is not lowercase, which takes in uncased scripts), the item is painted at capture, the term followed by its dash appears in the rendered text the count reads, and at least three such items share the list's parent; the snippet's number is the count after that. *kicker-above-heading.* A label in the painted card that holds its h3-or-lower heading is the card's metadata at any heading size, not only under 24px, when the label is pinned to the card as a badge: `position: absolute`, and either its box reaches to within 2px of the card's top edge or across it, or it paints a fill at alpha 0.9 or more that differs from the card's. An absolutely placed eyebrow inside the card's padding in the card's colour, and a filled pill in the card's flow, keep the size test. *skipped-heading.* The outline skips a heading not painted at capture (taste call r9-t3): `display: none` or `content-visibility: hidden` on it or an ancestor, `visibility: hidden`, an `inert` dialog, or no area. A heading a clipping ancestor cuts out (a parked carousel slide, a scroller's item), a visually hidden (`.sr-only`) heading, one at opacity 0, and one turned away or masked stay in the outline. The skip is read on as if the heading were not there, so a visible heading after a hidden one can report a skip of its own; the static engine is unchanged. *body-text-viewport-edge.* When the page cannot scroll to the text, the page-level name passes over a box that also runs past the other side of the viewport and lays out at least two of its children side by side, is moved by a translate of 1px or more on x (its own `transform` matrix or `translate`, or its parent's), and runs past one side more than 16px further than past the other: a slider track. A row translated only to centre it runs past both sides alike and is still named. It names the widest box around the text that is not one; a box too wide on both sides that stacks its content, or a row nothing translates, is still named.
  - **Round 14: mock frames by mechanical cues** (corpus run 1010, decision r14-t1; framed demos read by `crates/core/src/checks/text_context.rs`). Text in a recognised framed demo reports `undersized-ui-text`, `tiny-text`, `low-contrast` and `nested-cards` as advisory, as before (sentence-length copy, controls and the caption itself excepted). *Title bars* (both engines). Leading children that hold no text and are `aria-hidden="true"` or `position: absolute` or `fixed` are passed over before the title bar is read, and the bar may start as far below the box's top as the box's top border plus top padding plus 2px when that is more than 24px, up to 40px. *Code windows* (both engines; decision r14-t7). Title-bar dots stop being a mock cue when the window's text is mostly code: more than half of its shown non-space characters (`script`, `style`, `noscript` and `template` left out, a `display: none` subtree left out, and the own text of a box at `visibility: hidden` or `collapse` left out; at most 2,000 nodes walked) sit in a `pre`, `code`, `kbd` or `samp`, or in a box whose own computed `font-family` is monospace. Such a window is reading content, so `low-contrast`, `tiny-text`, `undersized-ui-text` and `nested-cards` report in it at full severity (the existing exemptions for `pre` and `code` still apply). A window recognised by a preview caption, a scale, a tilt or a sample caption keeps those cues. *Phones* (URL engine; every fact is measured, so the static engine never reads one). A box 120px to 500px wide, 1.6 to 2.4 times as tall as it is wide, with a first corner radius of 16px or more, and a shadow, a border on all four sides, a fill at 0.5 alpha or more or a background image is a demo frame when it holds a status bar: a text box with no element children that holds only an `h:mm` time (hours 0 to 23, one or two digits), whose top lies within 50px below the frame's top and whose box lies inside the frame across, and whose parent row is at most 36px tall, spans at least 70% of the frame's width, has the time as its first or last child, and holds another child whose text has no letters (a signal glyph, a battery, a notch). It is not one when it is 90% or more of the page's width or holds the page's `h1` or at least half its rendered text (the landmark test of Round 10). A duration badge or a start time alone on a portrait card is no status bar. *Captions* (both engines). A sample caption's label also ends at a comma (`,` or `，`) once it holds `illustrative` or `fictional`, so "Illustrative values, shown only to explain each workflow." names a mock while "Sample data, exports and reports are kept for 30 days" does not. `fictional` names a mock alone or followed by a data noun or `company` (and then optionally a data noun or `only`): "Fictional", "Fictional company data", not "Fictional worlds, real heartbreak.". *SVG text* (URL engine). `tiny-text` tests a `text`, `tspan` or `textPath` element's computed size times the scale of its nearest `svg`: the content box's width (padding and borders left out) over `viewBox` width or its height over `viewBox` height, the smaller of the two, the larger under `preserveAspectRatio` `slice`, the vertical one under `none`; times the even scale of a `transform` on the text or any element between it and the `svg` (the computed `transform`, else the attribute; a translation or a turn leaves the scale alone). The snippet keeps the computed size. Padding and borders come off only when the measured box matches the computed `width` and `height` (as a content box or a border box); a transformed `svg` with padding or borders leaves the computed size. An `svg` with no readable four-number `viewBox`, with no measured box, or a transform between that skews, tilts or scales the axes apart leaves the computed size.
  - **Line breaks** (Rust engine, `crates/core/src/browser/line_breaks.rs`; issue #1026). Two advisory rules read the rendered line boxes (`text_line_rects`), so the static engine never reports them; the URL engine and the in-page bundle (extension, live overlay) do, and both pass the Text paint gate. `straggler`: a `p`, `li`, `dd`, `blockquote` or `figcaption` of two or more lines whose last line holds one word and is at most 30% as wide as the widest line. The last word holds the line alone when the word before it ends on another row: both are measured with a DOM `Range` (the lowest client rect of each, so a word hyphenated across the break counts by its tail), captured only on those five tags, and a DOM that did not measure them reports nothing. The words are read from the rendered text nodes, so text under a descendant that renders none (`display: none`, an inline `script`) is never the last word; soft hyphens and zero-width spaces are dropped. Snippet `"<word>" alone on the last of <n> lines (<pct>% of the measure)`, double quotes in the word written as `'`. `unbalanced-heading`: an `h1` to `h6` of two or three lines whose last line is under 35% as wide as the line above it. Snippet `last of <n> lines is <pct>% as wide as the line above`. Both stand down on `text-wrap-style: balance` (`straggler` only up to six lines, the most Chromium balances, and also on `pretty`; the `text-wrap` longhand of an older Chrome counts), a `<br>` among the element's phrasing, a block child, `white-space: pre*` or `nowrap`, visually hidden text, text in a box whose computed `visibility` is `hidden` or `collapse` (it keeps its room on the line boxes, so the line widths would measure it), and fewer than three words. Where the browser does not apply `pretty` (`CSS.supports` false on both `text-wrap` and `text-wrap-style`, recorded per capture as `prettyWrap`; issue #1136), `straggler` also stands down when the raw text declares it: `text-wrap` or `text-wrap-style` set to `pretty` in the `style` attribute of the element or an ancestor, or in a `<style>` rule whose selector matches one of them. The nearest element up the chain that declares `text-wrap` at all decides, by the last declaration reaching it (an `!important` one first, the `style` attribute after the `<style>` rules, specificity not weighed), so an explicit reset keeps the finding, and an `inherit` or `unset` passes the decision to the ancestor. Comments are removed first; a rule under a grouping at-rule counts as applying and a rule nested in a style rule is not read; linked stylesheets, `@import`, `<style media>` conditions and shadow-tree styles are not read. A capture without the flag trusts the computed style. `straggler` also stands down on CJK text, a column that holds under 30 characters a line, and text with an atomic inline (a replaced element or an `inline-*` box) or a descendant in another font family or size. `unbalanced-heading` also stands down on a column that holds under 20 characters a line, measured only when the heading is set in one face.
  - **Round 14 counted repeats** (corpus run 1010, taste calls r14-t2 and r14-t5; both engines and the in-page bundle). *low-contrast.* A page reports each colour pair once on every path, the full `check_colors` pass as well as the SAFE_TAGS text path, and that finding's snippet ends ` (and N more)` when N other elements wear the pair; with no others the snippet is unchanged. In the URL engine and the in-page bundle the pair is the finding's snippet (ratio, bar, ink, ground, and any `(gradient on ...)` or `(layer on ...)` tail), its severity, whether the element pass hands the text to the pixels (`visual::routed_reason`), and the widget vendor whose markup holds it, so an advisory copy, a handed-over copy and a vendor's copy never speak for a counted page copy; the static engine keys on snippet and severity. The element that reports is the first in document order among those whose finding survived every waiver (inline `data-impeccable-ignore`, the paint gates, declared and labelled mockups, `ignoreValues` in extension mode); the SAFE_TAGS path keeps its own choice (the first copy wholly on screen, a copy cut by the page's edge only until one is). When that element's text holds fewer than three letters (an icon glyph such as `𝕏`, a price, a digit; mathematical alphanumerics do not count as letters), the first copy in document order whose text holds three, among the other findings and the counted SAFE_TAGS copies wholly within the page width, reports instead (URL engine and bundle). N counts those other findings plus the copies the SAFE_TAGS dedupe dropped, each counted only where the engine's own verdict on it would have let it report (the same checks the first copy passed: inline ignore, a picture or unread layer under the text, the paint gate, a colour caught mid-reveal, unread ink, a declared mockup), and in the URL engine also painted at capture. An advisory copy dropped where a failing copy reported the pair is not counted. The static engine keys on snippet and severity only, so a vendor's copy shares the page's pair there. The collapse runs before the visual-contrast pass, so the reporting element is the one handed to the pixels, and the elements it counts are treated as already reported there: the analytic and pixel passes neither report them again nor spend a candidate slot on them (text routed to the pixels stays readable by them, as before). The in-page bundle and the extension's offscreen reader get the same list as `collapsed` in the collect result and drop a visual-contrast result on those elements. A SAFE_TAGS copy is counted only where it sits on the owner's side of the hand-over and vendor boundaries, and a worded copy that takes the report joins the copy's existing finding group. Pixel-pass findings (`pixel contrast ...`, `browser contrast ...`) are not collapsed or counted here and keep their own once-per-pair rule for routed text; where the pixels replace a counted handed-over verdict, the count goes with it. *nested-cards.* An outer panel holding several inner cards reports once, on the first inner card in document order, ending ` (and N more)` for the other inner cards it holds that would have reported (the innermost of each flagged chain, after the same waivers); the panel is the card's nearest card-like ancestor, and an advisory mockup card and a counted card never share a finding. In the static engine the snippet is `Card inside card (<tag>) (and N more)`. The hook's per-session repeat cache keys a snippet without the count, so a reported pair or panel that gains a copy is not reported again.
- `createBrowserDetector(options)`: shared browser, defaults `waitUntil:'load'`, `settleMs:100`, `viewport 1280x800`; `close()` closes only an owned browser.
- Tests: `tests/detect-url-launch.test.mjs`, `tests/detect-antipatterns-browser.test.mjs`.

#### Attached pages: `impeccable detect --cdp` (Rust engine, `crates/browser/src/attach.rs`)

Scans a page of an app that is already running (Electron, NW.js, CEF, WebView2, or a Chrome started with `--remote-debugging-port`) and prints exactly what a URL scan prints: the same findings array under `--json`, the same text format, notes and exit codes, with every finding's `file` the page's URL (`cdp-target:<id>` when it has none). One difference: under `--json` its capture notes (the hidden-window and stalled-screenshot lines below) go to stderr, one per line, since they say which contrast reads did not run; stdout is still only the JSON. Issue #951.

- **Endpoint.** `GET http://<host>:<port>/json/version` (5 s timeouts) gives `webSocketDebuggerUrl`; its path is dialled at the host and port given, whatever host the endpoint writes into the URL. A `ws://.../devtools/browser/<id>` value skips that request. Before connecting, a host that resolves to any address that is not loopback is refused. Failures, each `stderr> Error: <message>\n` and `exit 1` (no findings; `[]` on stdout under `--json`):
  - unreachable: `could not reach a DevTools endpoint at <host:port> (<os error>). Start the app with --remote-debugging-port=<port> (Electron, NW.js, CEF; for WebView2 set WEBVIEW2_ADDITIONAL_BROWSER_ARGUMENTS=--remote-debugging-port=<port>), then run this again.`
  - not DevTools: `<host:port> answered, but not as a DevTools endpoint (GET /json/version: HTTP <status>|no webSocketDebuggerUrl). <the same hint>`
- **Picking the page.** Candidates are `Target.getTargets` entries of type `page` whose URL does not start with `devtools://`, in the browser's order. A `ws://.../devtools/page/<id>` value picks that id (`no page with id <id> is open on <host:port>. Open pages:<list>` otherwise). Else `--target` keeps the pages whose URL or title contains it, case-insensitively, or whose id it is; without `--target` every candidate is kept. Exactly one left is scanned. Otherwise:
  - none open: `no pages are open on <host:port> to scan.`
  - `--target` matched none: `no page on <host:port> has a URL or title containing "<t>". Open pages:<list>`
  - `--target` matched several: `<n> pages on <host:port> match "<t>". Rerun with a --target that names one of them (a longer part of its URL or title, or its id):<list>`
  - several and no `--target`: `<n> pages are open on <host:port>. Rerun with --target and part of the URL or title of the one to scan (or its id):<list>`
  - `<list>` is one line per page, `\n  <url>  "<title>"  (id <targetId>)`, the title part left out when empty, `(no URL)` for an empty URL.
- **A session that owns nothing.** The engine never launches or closes the browser, never navigates or reloads, never calls `Emulation.*` (no viewport, device scale or media override), and detaches (`Target.detachFromTarget`) instead of closing the page. It enables `Page` and `Runtime` on its own session only (no `Network`, no auto-attach, no new-document scripts). No evaluation is a user gesture (`userGesture: false`), so the page's user activation is unchanged.
- **An isolated world.** Every evaluation runs in an isolated world created on the page's current document (`Page.createIsolatedWorld`): the page's scripts never see the scan's globals, and the capture reads untouched built-ins whatever the page did to `Map` or `Array.from` (the new-document natives stash URL scans rely on cannot apply to a document that is already loaded). A navigation during the scan destroys the world and the scan fails (`Error: isolated capture context was destroyed or document changed`) instead of reading another document. `Runtime.evaluate` is not subject to the page's CSP, so a strict CSP does not stop the capture; a `style-src` without `'unsafe-inline'` does block the pixel pass's hide style there as on a URL scan, and that pass then gives no pixel verdicts.
- **Hidden windows.** In that world `requestAnimationFrame` fires on the next frame or after 100 ms, whichever comes first, and while `document.visibilityState` is `hidden` (a window behind others, minimized, or a background tab) `setTimeout` waits by posting messages until its time is up, since a hidden page's timers fire a second or more late. The page's own timers and frames are not touched. A hidden page paints no frames, and a screenshot of it can wait for one indefinitely (it does on Linux), so when the page is hidden at the visual pass the pixel reads are skipped: the analyses that read no screenshot still run, the element pass's verdicts on text it handed to the pixels stand as advisory, or drop where they measured nothing (as for any unread pixel verdict), and the text output adds `Capture note: <url> was hidden (minimized, or a background tab or window), so the pixel pass read nothing there. Bring it on screen, focused or not, and scan again for those contrast verdicts.` A screenshot of an attached page that takes over 15 s ends the pixel reads instead of the scan (any other error during them, a navigation included, still fails the scan), with `Capture note: the pixel pass on <url> stopped when a screenshot stalled (<error>). Bring the window on screen and scan again for the contrast verdicts it did not read.`
- **The same passes, with three differences.** The page is scanned as it stands: no `goto`, no settle, and the viewport is the page's own (`Page.getLayoutMetrics` layout viewport). Consent and overlay hiding are off (as with `--no-consent-hiding --no-overlay-hiding`): the person seeded the state under review. The pixel pass reads what is on screen (`captureBeyondViewport: false`, which never lays the window out at another size), so a candidate whose clip is outside the visual viewport is scrolled into view first, as one past the content box is in a URL scan, and scrolled back; that also reaches text inside an inner scroller, which a URL scan's beyond-viewport capture leaves unread. Design systems: like an `http(s)` URL scan, none is loaded.
- **Left as found.** Before the passes the engine records the window's scroll position and every element (open shadow roots included) scrolled off 0, and the pixel pass records each `video` it pauses. After them, on success and on error, it first cancels the scan's waits in the isolated world (so an evaluation that outlived its protocol timeout cannot scroll the page again), then scrolls each recorded element back, any other element the scan moved off 0 back to 0, the window last, plays again each video the pixel pass paused that has not ended, removes the pixel pass's `#impeccable-visual-contrast-hide-style` (unless the page already had one) and its marker attributes, and deletes the scan's globals from the isolated world. If that step fails the scan fails, findings and all: `stderr> Error: scanned <url> but could not put it back as it was (<why>); check its scroll position and any video it was playing.\n`, `exit 1`. The reveal sweep and the scroll probe still scroll the window through the document during the scan, so the page's scroll handlers run.
- Tests: `crates/browser/tests/cdp_attach.rs` (a headless Chrome the test launches, attached to as an outside client).

#### Static and regex engines (only what affects the contract)

- **`flat-type-hierarchy` is advisory when weight separates the roles** (Rust engine, corpus decision r4-p23-flat-type-hierarchy-commerce; static HTML and URL engines, which read computed weights). The rule reads each sampled element's computed `font-weight` (`normal` 400, `bold` 700, a number as itself); when at least four in five heading samples are set at least 200 heavier than the body samples' most common weight, the finding keeps reporting with `severity: 'advisory'` and its snippet's parenthesis ends `; weight separates headings from body text)`. Otherwise it reports as before. The static engine's cascade has no user-agent stylesheet, so a heading with no declared weight reads 400 there, where the URL engine reads the browser's bold.

- `detectHtml`: reads file, imports `htmlparser2`, `css-select`, `css-tree`, `domutils`; on import failure prints once to stderr `impeccable detect: DEGRADED - HTML parser modules unavailable (htmlparser2, css-select, css-tree, domutils).\nFalling back to regex matching. Custom properties, selector matching and computed contrast are NOT evaluated; findings are an undercount, not a clean bill of health.\n` and falls back to `detectText`. Inlines `<link rel=stylesheet href>` that are local (not `/^(https?:)?\/\//i`), query/hash stripped. Runs element rules, design-system rules (`checkSourceDesignSystem` + `collectStaticDesignSystemFindings`, merged), then page rules only when `isFullPage(html)` (`/<!doctype\s|<html[\s>]|<head[\s>]/i` after stripping comments), plus text-content analyzers; ends with inline-ignore filtering.
- `detectText`: regex line matchers (ids: side-tab including Tailwind stripe-child empty `w-*` + chromatic `bg-*` plus a `shrink-0` / edge-round cue, border-accent-on-rounded, overused-font, gradient-text, ai-color-palette, gray-on-color, bounce-easing, broken-image), inset-stripe/pseudo-stripe CSS scans, `codex-grid-background`, `<style>` blocks (Astro/Vue/Svelte), CSS-in-JS templates, design-system source checks; dedupe (same antipattern+snippet within 2 lines); page analyzers only when `isFullPage` and ext ∈ `{'.html','.htm','.astro','.vue','.svelte'}` or no ext (`<stdin>`): flat-type-hierarchy, monotonous-spacing, em-dash-overuse, marketing-buzzword, aphoristic-cadence, dark-glow (+ radial-halo, marquee); inline ignores last.

#### Profiler (`cli/engine/profile/profiler.mjs`)

- **Not exposed via any CLI flag** (no `--profile`; `grep` finds none). Library only: `createDetectorProfile()` → `{events: []}`; engines call `profileFindings/profileStep/(Async)` when `options.profile` is passed programmatically. `recordProfileEvent` normalizes to `{engine, phase, ruleId, target, ms, findings, [detail], [findingIds]}` (defaults `'unknown'`/`''`/0) and pushes to `profile.events` (or calls `profile(event)`, `profile.record`, or pushes to an array). `summarizeDetectorProfile(profile)` groups by `engine\0phase\0ruleId\0target` and returns `[{engine, phase, ruleId, target, calls, totalMs, avgMs, p50, p95, findings}]` (ms values `Number(x.toFixed(3))`, percentile index `min(n-1, max(0, ceil(pct/100*n)-1))` over sorted samples), sorted by `totalMs` desc. Event vocabulary used: engine `browser|static-html|regex`; phases `setup|load|scan|visual-contrast|element|page|parse-html|source|style-block|css-in-js|extract|text-content|page-analyzer`; ruleIds like `import-puppeteer`, `read-browser-script`, `launch-browser`, `new-page`, `set-viewport`, `goto:${waitUntil}`, `settle`, `configure-pure-detect`, `inject-browser-script`, `browser-scan`, `content-hidden-at-rest`, `browser-fallback`, `collect-candidates`, `pixel-diff`, `close-page`, `close-browser`, `read-html`, `import-static-parser`, `parse-document`, `inline-linked-stylesheet`, `design-system`, `typography-rules`, `kicker-above-heading`, `numbered-section-labels`, `repeated-container-text`, `layout-rules`, `cream-palette`, `skipped-heading`, `html-patterns`, `text-content`, `codex-grid-background`, `style-blocks`, `css-in-js`, or the rule id.

#### `impeccable help|install|link|update|check` and `impeccable skills <verb>` (`cli/bin/commands/skills.mjs`)

**Rust authenticity addition (#479):** the historical JS bundle flow below
is superseded for remote downloads. The Rust installer resolves the site's
redirect (301/302/303/307/308) to a versioned Impeccable GitHub release and verifies
`universal.zip.sig.json` against a compiled-in Ed25519 public key before ZIP
extraction. Missing/invalid signatures, unknown keys, mismatched metadata or
content, and failures fetching either asset exit nonzero, including when
`install` finds an existing installation. No downloaded content reaches the
installed skill or hook files. Explicit `IMPECCABLE_BUNDLE_PATH` and `link`
retain their local-development trust behavior. See [bundle signing](BUNDLE-SIGNING.md).

**TLS trust (#757):** every HTTPS request the engine makes (bundle and signature downloads, `/api/version`, `/api/commands`, the roll API, image generation) verifies against the OS trust store plus the bundled Mozilla roots, built once in `crates/context/src/http.rs`. `SSL_CERT_FILE` / `SSL_CERT_DIR` replace the OS store, never the bundled roots. The live server calls on localhost are plain HTTP and unaffected.

- **Invoked from**: README.md ("npx impeccable install / update"), README.npm.md Quick Start (`npx impeccable skills install`, `... install -y --providers=claude,codex --scope=project`, `... update`, `... install --no-hooks`, `... link --source=.impeccable --providers=claude,cursor`, `... skills help`), `README.md:360` (hook consent explanation).
- `run(args)`: `args[0]` ∈ `undefined|help|--help|-h` → `showHelp()`; `install` → `install(rest)`; `link`; `update`; `check` (ignores flags); else `stderr> Unknown skills command: ${sub}` + `Run 'impeccable --help' for available commands.`, `exit 1`.
- Constants: `API_BASE = 'https://impeccable.style'`; `PROVIDER_DIRS = ['.claude','.cursor','.devin','.dsh','.gemini','.agents','.agent','.github','.grok','.hermes','.kiro','.opencode','.pi','.omp','.qoder','.trae','.trae-cn','.rovodev','.vibe']`; aliases (`agent`→`.agent`, `agents`/`codex`→`.agents`, `antigravity`→`.agent`, `claude`/`claude-code`→`.claude`, `copilot`/`github`→`.github`, `cursor`, `devin`/`windsurf`→`.devin`, `deepseek`/`deepseek-harness`/`dsh`→`.dsh`, `gemini`, `grok`/`grok-build`/`xai`→`.grok`, `hermes`, `kiro`, `opencode`, `pi`, `omp`/`oh-my-pi`→`.omp`, `qoder`, `replit`→`.agents`, `rovo-dev`/`rovodev`→`.rovodev`, `trae`, `trae-cn`, `vibe`); leading `.` stripped and lowercased before alias lookup; a literal PROVIDER_DIR value is accepted as-is. `DEFAULT_TARGETS = ['.claude','.agents']`. User-scope skill dir overrides: `.agent`→`~/.gemini/config/skills`, `.devin`→`%APPDATA%\devin\skills` on Windows, else `$XDG_CONFIG_HOME/devin/skills`, else `~/.config/devin/skills`, `.dsh`→`$DSH_HOME/skills` (only when DSH_HOME under home) else `~/.dsh/skills`, `.hermes`→`$HERMES_HOME/skills` (only when HERMES_HOME under home) else `~/.hermes/skills`, `.pi`→`~/.pi/agent/skills`, `.omp`→`~/.omp/agent/skills`, `.opencode`→`$OPENCODE_CONFIG_DIR|$XDG_CONFIG_HOME/opencode|~/.config/opencode` + `/skills`; others `~/<provider>/skills`. Project scope: `<root>/<provider>/skills`.
- **help**: `fetch('https://impeccable.style/api/commands')` → JSON array `[{id, description}]`; failure → `stderr> Could not fetch command list from impeccable.style. Check your network connection.`, `exit 1`. Prints:
  ```

    Impeccable Skills & Commands

    Install:  npx impeccable install
    Link:     npx impeccable link --source=.impeccable
    Update:   npx impeccable update
    Docs:     https://impeccable.style/cheatsheet

    Command                Description
    ---------------------- ----------------------------------------------------
    /<id padded to 22>     <description, truncated to 69 chars + '...' if > 72>
    ...

    N commands available. Run /<command> in your AI harness.

  ```
  sorted by `id.localeCompare`.
- **Flag parsing** (`getFlagValue`): `--name=value` or `--name value` (next arg not starting with `-`). Boolean flags via `includes`.
- **install flags**: `--force`, `-y|--yes`, `--no-hooks`, `--providers=<list>`, scope: `--user|--home|--global` → user; `--project|--local` → project; `--scope=<v>`/`--install-scope=<v>` normalized (`u|user|home|global`→user, `p|project|local|repo`→project; unknown → error `Unknown install scope: ${v}. Use --scope=project or --scope=global.`). Project root = nearest ancestor with `.git`, else cwd. Detection: project harness dirs present in root, plus `HARNESS_HINTS`: the project-scope `.replit` → `.agents` labeled "Replit Agent" (a project hint stands in for that dir's plain detection, so `.agents` is not also listed as Codex CLI; the detected-only prompts name harnesses, e.g. `replit`) and the user-scope hints under home (`.agent`, `.gemini/antigravity*`→`.agent`, `.claude`, `.codex`→`.agents`, `.cursor`, `.codeium/windsurf`→`.devin`, the Devin config dir (`%APPDATA%\devin` on Windows, else `$XDG_CONFIG_HOME/devin`, else `~/.config/devin`), `.dsh` (resolved `$DSH_HOME`, fallback `~/.dsh`), `.gemini`, `.grok`, `.hermes`, `.kiro`, `.opencode`, opencode config dir, `.pi`, `.qoder`, `.rovodev`, `.vibe`). Targets: explicit list wins (invalid names → `Unknown provider(s): ...`); `-y` → detected project providers, else detected user providers, else DEFAULT_TARGETS; interactive → prints "Detected harnesses:" table then radio/checkbox prompts (raw-mode TTY) or line prompts (`Install target: [1] Detected only (...)  [2] Customize [1]: `, `Select harnesses (comma-separated: ...)`). Scope: explicit; `-y` → project; interactive prompt `Install location` (Project/Global). Hooks: `decideHookInstall` reads `hook.consent` in config(.local).json; declined→false, accepted→true; all targets already have hook markers→true; `-y` or non-TTY→true; else prints HOOK_EXPLAINER and asks `Install the design hook? (Y/n) `, storing consent in `.impeccable/config.local.json`.
  Bundle: `IMPECCABLE_BUNDLE_PATH` (dir or zip) else download `https://impeccable.style/api/download/bundle/universal` (https only, up to 5 redirects, non-200 → `HTTP ${status}`; 30 s connect timeout and a 60 s idle timeout on every read, with no cap on the whole transfer, so a slow but steady connection finishes (#1115); a stall → `no data received for 60 s downloading <url>; check the network and retry`) to `${tmpdir}/impeccable-update-${Date.now()}.zip`, extracted with `fflate.unzipSync` into `${tmpdir}/impeccable-update-${Date.now()}` (zip-slip guarded: `Refusing to extract entry outside target dir: ${entry}`). Bundle layout `<bundle>/<provider>/skills/<skill>/...`, `<bundle>/<provider>/agents/*.md`, hook manifests `<bundle>/.claude/settings.json`, `.cursor/hooks.json`, `.codex/hooks.json`, `.github/hooks/impeccable.json`, `.grok/hooks/impeccable.json`. A failed remote bundle prints either `Could not download skill bundle: ${msg}` (the release lookup, signature, or ZIP request failed: connect error, HTTP status, stall, size cap), `Could not verify skill bundle: ${msg}` (keyring, release redirect or version check, signature envelope, key, signature, digest or size mismatch), or `Could not unpack skill bundle: ${msg}` (staging, saving, reading back or extracting the bundle on this machine), each followed by `. Nothing was installed; retry or update the CLI. If this persists, report it at https://github.com/pbakaus/impeccable/issues/new`.
  Fresh install: `stdout> \nDownloading impeccable skills...`; migrate `*-impeccable` prefixed dirs → `impeccable`; copy each skill dir (rm existing dest first; drop an in-project cross-provider symlinked skills dir); copy agents (`.github`→`.github/agents` or `~/.copilot/agents`; `.cursor`→`.cursor/agents` or `~/.cursor/agents`); write hooks (Claude project scope: `.claude/settings.local.json`, skipped when project `.claude/settings.json` already carries the marker; Claude user scope: `~/.claude/settings.json`, with obsolete Impeccable entries pruned from `~/.claude/settings.local.json`; other destinations: `.cursor/hooks.json`, `.codex/hooks.json` for `.agents`, `.github/hooks/impeccable.json`, `.grok/hooks/impeccable.json`); merged with existing JSON (`mergeHookManifests`: strips existing impeccable entries by markers `skills/impeccable/scripts/hook-probe.mjs|hook.mjs|hook-before-edit.mjs|hook-after-edit.mjs|hook-stop.mjs`, then appends fresh); invalid existing JSON → error `Existing hook manifest is not valid JSON: ${dest}. Re-run with --force to replace it.` (with `--force`, `.bak` written). Claude and Gemini user settings are never replaced wholesale when invalid. Hook command rewriting: `[ ! -f '<abs>' ] || node '<abs>'` (POSIX, single-quote-escaped) when absolute (user scope or global skill), else `[ ! -f "<rel>" ] || node "<rel>"` where rel = `${CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR}/.claude/skills/impeccable/scripts/hook.mjs` (Claude), `.agents/skills/impeccable/scripts/hook.mjs` (Codex; plus `commandWindows: if exist "<p>" (node "<p>" & exit /b)`), `.cursor/skills/impeccable/scripts/hook-before-edit.mjs`; on win32 for non-Codex: `node -e "<WIN32_HOOK_GUARD_SCRIPT>" "<path>"`. Output: `Installed impeccable into: ${targets.join(', ')} (${'global'|'project'})`, optional `Installed <Provider> agents into: <path>` (+ shadow warning), `Installed hooks into: ...`, then `\nDone! Now type /impeccable init in your AI coding agent's chat (not in this terminal) to set up design context.\n`. Errors: `Download failed: ...` / `Install failed: ...` / `Nothing was installed: the bundle had no variants for ...` → exit 1.
  A Claude user-scope hook write also prunes obsolete Impeccable entries from the current project's `.claude/settings.local.json`, preventing a stale project hook from firing alongside the user hook.
  GitHub Copilot hook (`.github/hooks/impeccable.json` in the project, `bash` key): a project install keeps the bundle's portable `$(git rev-parse --show-toplevel)/.github/skills/impeccable/scripts/impeccable` command; a user-scope install rewrites it to the home launcher (`$HOME/.github/skills/impeccable/scripts/impeccable` on POSIX, the resolved profile path with forward slashes on Windows), unless the project ships its own `.github/skills/impeccable/scripts/impeccable`, whose committed portable manifest is left as is. In user scope a project manifest that names the portable path while the project has no Copilot skill does not count as installed, so a repeat install rewrites it.
  Every shipped `impeccable.cmd` (provider dirs, `plugin/`, `cursor-plugin/`, the dist zips) has CRLF line endings: `.gitattributes` sets `*.cmd text eol=crlf`, the build writes batch files with CRLF, and `bun run build` fails on any emitted `.cmd` with a bare LF (#1098).
  Already installed (and not `--force`): `Impeccable skills are already installed (found in ${provider}/).`; compares tree hashes (`sha256` of file content with `\.(claude|cursor|...)\/skills\/` normalized to `.PROVIDER/skills/`); if differs → refresh + `Updated ${n} skill(s) to v${v}.`; missing hooks repaired; else `Skills are up to date (v${v}).` + `Run with --force to reinstall.`; a bundle that cannot be fetched or unpacked (offline, connect error, HTTP status, stall, size cap, a failed save) → `Could not check for skill updates: Could not download skill bundle: ${msg}` (or `Could not unpack skill bundle: ${msg}`, without the retry advice) + `Existing skills were left unchanged.`, skills untouched, exit 0, unless a selected provider or a hook manifest is missing and needs the bundle, which stays fatal; a bundle that fails verification stays fatal over an existing install too: `Install check failed: Could not verify skill bundle: ${msg}...` → exit 1 (#1138); ends `Done!` or the above; `exit 0`. Version read from `^version:\s*(.+)$` in installed `impeccable/SKILL.md`.
- **update flags**: `-y|--yes`, `--force`, `--no-hooks`, scope flags as above (unknown → `Unknown update scope: ${v}. Use --project or --user.`). Resolves project vs user installs holding an `impeccable`/`*-impeccable`/`teach-impeccable` skill; none → `No impeccable skill folders found in this project or at the user level.` + `Run \`npx impeccable install\` to install first.`, exit 1; both → prompt `Update which? [project]/user: ` (non-TTY defaults project). Prints `Updating the ${label} install: ${root} (${providers})`, linked providers note, `Checking for updates...`; up to date → `Skills are up to date (vX).` [+hooks] + `Nothing else to do.`, exit 0; else `Found skills in: ...`, prompt `Update skills in N provider folder(s)? (Y/n) ` (n/no → `Aborted.` exit 0), refresh, `Updated N skill(s) to vX.`, `Done!`. Hook manifests follow install's placement: in user scope the Claude hook is `~/.claude/settings.json` and every other provider's manifest (`.cursor/hooks.json`, `.codex/hooks.json`, `.grok/hooks/impeccable.json`, ...) is the current project's, rewritten against the home skill. Hook consent is read from (and a prompt's answer written to) that same project root, so a project that declined hooks gets none from `update --user -y`. The stale-manifest repair (`repair_stale_hook_manifests`, including the retired Cursor gate) runs on those project manifests regardless of hook consent. An older `update --user` wrote non-Claude manifests under home instead; those are repaired in place too, never pruned, because a home manifest is global. When a home and a project hook (or a plugin and a project hook) both fire for one edit, the `hook` verb's event claim lets only one of them scan (see `hook`).
- **link**: `--source=<path>` (default `.impeccable`), `--providers`, `--force`, `-y`. Source must contain `dist/universal/` or provider `*/skills` dirs, else `Could not find compiled skills in ${src}. Expected dist/universal/ or provider skill folders.` Prompts `Link impeccable skills into N folder(s)? (Y/n) `; creates relative dir symlinks; existing non-link skipped with warning unless `--force`; output `Linked impeccable into: ... (N linked, N already linked, N skipped).` + submodule hint.
- **check**: not installed → `Impeccable is not installed in this project.` + `Run \`npx impeccable install\` to install.` exit 0; else `Checking for updates...\n` then `Skills are up to date (vX).` or `Updates available.` + `Run \`npx impeccable update\` to update.`; failure → `Could not check for updates: ${msg}` exit 1.
- Prompts: non-TTY `ask()` reads answers line-by-line from stdin (fd 0) after echoing the question; TTY SIGINT → `PromptAbortError` (`code IMPECCABLE_PROMPT_ABORT`) → cli.js prints `\nAborted.` exit 130. ANSI (`\x1b[36m` accent, `\x1b[1m` bold, `\x1b[2m` dim, `\x1b[32m` good) only when stdout is TTY, `NO_COLOR` unset, `TERM !== 'dumb'`.
- Tests: `tests/skills-cli.test.js`, `tests/cli-remote-e2e` (opt-in).

### PART 2 — skill/scripts verbs

#### `concept-seed.mjs` -> `node {{scripts_path}}/concept-seed.mjs`

- **Invoked from**: `reference/new-work.md:37` "`node {{scripts_path}}/concept-seed.mjs --scope surface --mode <mode>`" and `:46` "4. Run `node {{scripts_path}}/concept-seed.mjs --scope direction --mode <mode>` and follow what it prints. No substitute, no skip". Header usage lines: `--scope direction --mode persuade`, `--scope surface --mode operate --from <key>`, `--grain flow`, `--candidate-count 6`, `--from <key> --reroll 1 [--register bolder]`, `--chosen <challenger-id> --kind challenger --from <key> --scope direction`, `--kind assigned --from <key> --scope direction`.
- **Args** (each read as `args[idx+1]` after `indexOf`): `--scope` (`surface` default | `direction`), `--from <key>` (default `IMPECCABLE_CONCEPT_SEED` env or `crypto.randomBytes(4).toString('hex')`), `--reroll <n>` (Number, default 0), `--register safer|bolder`, `--mode persuade|operate|read|experience`, `--grain product|flow|view|region`, `--platform web|ios|android`, `--candidate-count 5..7` (default 7), `--chosen <id>`, `--kind assigned|pick|challenger|canon`.
- **Env**: `IMPECCABLE_CONCEPT_SEED`, `IMPECCABLE_CATALOG_DIR` (default = script dir; local catalog files `concept-ingredients.json`, `concept-reviews.json`, `composition-ingredients.json`, `composition-reviews.json`; invalid/missing → treated as absent), `IMPECCABLE_API_URL` (default `https://impeccable.style/api`, trailing `/` stripped), `IMPECCABLE_API_TIMEOUT` (ms, default 4000, one shared deadline for all API calls in the run), `IMPECCABLE_NO_TELEMETRY`, `DO_NOT_TRACK`, `IMPECCABLE_CARD_BASE` (default `https://impeccable.style/worlds/cards`), `IMPECCABLE_COMPOSITIONS=1` (renders composition block; otherwise compositions are dealt but not printed).
- **Validation errors** (stderr, `exitCode 1`): `concept-seed: --scope must be direction or surface`; `--reroll must be a non-negative integer`; `--register must be safer or bolder`; `--register steers a re-roll round; pass --reroll <n> with it`; `--register applies to direction rounds only`; `--mode must be persuade, operate, read, or experience`; `--grain must be one of product, flow, view, region`; `--platform must be one of web, ios, android`; `--candidate-count must be an integer from 5 to 7`; `concept-seed: every challenger tier needs at least one approved concept` (local catalog).
- **Init gate** (render path only): imports `./context.mjs` `loadContext(cwd)`; if `!hasProduct` → `stdout> NO_PRODUCT_MD: the dice stay in the cup until product truth exists. Complete the init ask round and write PRODUCT.md first (reference/init.md), then re-run this exact command. Challengers fuse their form with facts from PRODUCT.md; without it every direction is ungrounded.\n`, `exit 1`.
- **Assigned index math**: `unit(salt) = sha256(`${scope}:${salt}:${key}`).readUInt32BE(0)/0xffffffff`; `indexSalt = reroll===0 ? 'index' : `index:reroll-${reroll}``; `buildIndex = 3 + floor(unit(indexSalt)*(candidateCount-2))` (3..candidateCount). Surface scope deals `dealtIndices=[buildIndex]` plus up to 2 more distinct in 1..candidateCount via `unit(`${indexSalt}:deal-${draw}`)` (fills sequentially after 64 draws).
- **Data resolution**: local catalog → `source:'local'` (challengers via `selectApprovedChallengers`, compositions via `selectApprovedCompositions`, both in `lib/roll-selection.mjs`, hash = Node sha256 hex; `poolRevision` = first 12 hex of sha256 over sorted approved `familyId:id:strength:form:spark:JSON(system):webLeverage` lines); else **roll API** `GET ${API_BASE}/roll?scope=&key=&reroll=[&mode=][&grain=][&platform=]` (`URLSearchParams` order: scope, key, reroll, mode, grain, platform), raced against the shared budget; non-ok, timeout, or `challengers` empty → null; response fields used: `poolRevision, approvedCount, catalogCount, challengers, compositions|stagings|staging`; else **degraded**.
- **Selection algorithm** (roll-selection): challengers: approved concepts; per `wellTier` in `['graphic','interaction','atmosphere']` (all three must be non-empty), optional `minRating` gate, `mode` gate (`review.allowedModes` absent = all; a tier the gate would empty keeps its full pool, except under a mode with tier quotas, where that tier's quota moves to `graphic` and the tier is not refilled; `graphic` itself always falls back), strength filter (`direction` wants `world|dual`, `surface` wants `composition|dual`; per-tier fallback to full pool). Per-tier quota: `TIER_QUOTAS = { operate: { graphic: 5, interaction: 1, atmosphere: 0 } }`, every other mode (and no mode) 2 per tier. Tier order ranked by digests of `${scope}:${key}:tiers${salt}:${tierId}` desc; a zero-quota tier is skipped without shifting `index`; per tier tickets (`breadth==='niche'` excluded; tickets by rating `{1:1,2:2,3:2}`, default 2) ranked by `${scope}:${key}:challenger-${index}${salt}:${id}#${ticket}`; take `quota` picks in ranked order, each the first concept of a `familyId` not yet picked in that tier, else the next distinct id (at quota 2: first pick + first of a different family) → 6 picks when every tier can fill its quota, fewer on a thin catalog; re-roll round n excludes all earlier picks (fallback to the full tier pool if exhausted; under a quota, when fewer than `quota` unseen concepts remain, deal those first, then top up from the rest of the tier ranked with the same salt); `salt = round===0 ? '' : `:reroll-${round}``. Compositions: approved, non-niche (fallback all), `surface===mode` (empty → none), platform hard filter (`platforms` absent = all), tickets ranked with salt `${scope}:${key}:staging[:reroll-${round}]`, grain-preferred stable partition, distinct `familyId` first then top-up, count 3; `match = {grain, atGrain, grainAvailable, platform, platformExcluded}`.
- **stdout (full roll)**: header line `${SCOPE} CONCEPT SEED (key: ${key}; mode: ${mode ?? 'unscoped'}; source: ${local|api}; approved pool: ${poolRevision}; ${approvedCount}/${catalogCount} human-approved; rerun with --scope ${scope}[ --mode m] --from ${key}[ --reroll n][ --register r] --candidate-count ${n} to reproduce this roll against this catalog revision)`, then `[RE-ROLL ROUND n ...]`, the assigned block (`ASSIGNED INDEX: N` / `DEALT INDICES: a, b, c (index N leads)` + instruction, or SAFER/BOLDER REGISTER block), `CHALLENGERS:` with `renderChallenger` per concept:
  ```
    ${i+1}. ${form}
       SOURCE ID: ${id}
       CREATIVE SPARK: ${spark}
       SYSTEM GRAMMAR:
         - rule...
       WEB LEVERAGE: ${webLeverage}
       QUALITY BAR: board ${cardBoard || CARD_BASE/id.webp} · hero ${cardHero || CARD_BASE/id-hero.webp}
  ```
  optional composition block, challenger instruction, quality-bar sentence, authority + richness instructions, `TELEMETRY:` block (api source only), `A user- or brief-pinned decision beats the roll, always.`, and the restated line (`ASSIGNED INDEX (restated for truncated readers): N. ...; seed key ${key}.`). Degraded: header `... source: degraded; rerun with ...)` then `ASSIGNED INDEX`/`DEALT INDICES`, the "No challengers this run: the roll service was unreachable and no local catalog exists..." paragraph naming the single GET to `https://impeccable.style/api/roll`, authority, pinned-decision line, restated line. Safer register while degraded prints the SAFER block without assignment. Full text templates are in the source (lines 405-690) and should be copied verbatim.
- **Mode rules** (binary only; `--mode` given, every round including re-rolls and both registers): the mode's rules file is `${skillDir}/reference/mode-persuade.md` for `persuade` and `experience`, `mode-operate.md` for `operate`, `mode-read.md` for `read`, resolved like every other reference path (`IMPECCABLE_SKILL_DIR`, else the skill dir found beside the binary; with neither, the bare `reference/mode-<file>.md`). The engine takes the bodies of its `## Directions` and `## Comps` sections verbatim (every line after the heading up to the next `## ` heading or EOF, CRLF read as LF, leading and trailing blank lines trimmed; anything before `## Directions` is ignored) and prints, as the line after the richness instruction on a full roll and after the authority instruction on a degraded one:
  ```
  MODE RULES (${mode}, from ${path}). They govern this surface's directions and every comp you write or judge for it, the decision comps included; where shared guidance conflicts, these win.
  DIRECTIONS
  ${Directions body}
  COMPS
  ${Comps body}
  ```
  `${mode}` is the mode as given (`experience` stays `experience`). When the file cannot be read, or either section is missing or empty, the block is the single line `MODE RULES unavailable: read ${path} before writing directions or comps.` and the roll still succeeds. Without `--mode` nothing is printed. The richness instruction is mode-neutral; the per-mode guidance on committing to an interface language lives in the mode files.
- **Presentation** (binary only; every successful roll, direction or surface, full or degraded, every re-roll and both registers; never on validation errors, the PRODUCT.md gate, or telemetry pings): after the last line of the roll, a `PRESENTATION (the decision round, condensed from new-work.md; follow it even when your read of that file came back cut):` header and five `- ` lines, in order: how to serve the hand (round 0: `<self> serve-question --schema`, `--start --payload <file>`, open the URL; re-roll: `--update --key <same key> --payload <file>` while a page is open, never a second `--start`, and `--start` when no page opened yet); the comp line (direction: every card declares a comp under `.impeccable/mocks/decision/`, canon included, declined excepted, generated after serving in reading order with its `a.png.json` sidecar; degraded direction other than the safer register: one text-only card, no comp; surface: comps or wireframes, no pick or canon card; a recorded `code` default, or `--mode operate` / `--mode read` whatever is recorded, turns either scope's line into the code-led flip-reserve variant); `<self> serve-question --wait --key <key>`, led by `After the last comp lands (at once when this round generates none),` or, on a code-led round or a single degraded card, `Right after serving, with no comp to generate first,`, with the session-polling rule (rerun only after exit 3); the build-path line (`recorded default <comp|code> (from .impeccable/config.json|.impeccable/config.local.json)`, resolved exactly like `context`'s `BUILD_PATH_DEFAULT`, or `none recorded ...` with the comp default and the one-time offer after a flip; with `--mode operate` or `--mode read` it is instead `code-led. A surface in <mode> mode builds in code whatever the project records`, naming a recorded non-code default in parentheses as the one that governs Persuade and Experience surfaces, with `"buildPath": {"value": "code", "toggle": true}` and no offer to record a flip); and the structured-tool fallback (`--start` exit 2, `--wait` exit 4, or a harness that cannot hold a blocking `--wait`). Text in `crates/context/src/seed_text.rs`; `reference/new-work.md` is its source and names the block.
- **Roll record** (binary only; every successful roll, the same set that prints the presentation block): writes `.impeccable/questions/roll.json` atomically (temp file, then rename), creating the directory, as `{"scope":"surface"|"direction","seed":<seed reference>,"reroll":<n>}`, replacing the previous record. It holds neither the key nor a time: its age for the roll window is its mtime (`read_roll` fills `at` from it; a legacy record's own `at` still wins). When git already tracks `roll.json` (one `git ls-files`, only inside a work tree), the record goes to `.impeccable/questions/roll.local.json` instead, which `read_roll` prefers and `take_roll` removes the same way; once `roll.json` is untracked again, the next roll writes it and removes the aside file. When the roll printed challengers, the record also carries `"worlds":[{"id":<SOURCE ID>,"board":<url>,"hero":<url>}, ...]` after `at`, one entry per printed challenger in print order, with the same board and hero URLs as its `QUALITY BAR` line; a degraded roll and the safer register (whose hand is spent unseen) print none and write no `worlds` key. A roll the API dealt, while telemetry is on, also carries `"source":"api"`, the response's `poolRevision` (only when it is 12 lowercase hex), its `handId` (only when it is 32 lowercase hex), and the seed's `mode` and `register` when given; a decision page that takes such a roll copies them, with the dealt worlds, into its hand as `seed` (the picker's ping, below). **Seed reference** (binary only): every successful roll prints, after the roll text and before PRESENTATION, `SEED REFERENCE: seed:<16 hex>. Write this, never the key, wherever the seed is recorded (the surface brief's FORM line, build-phase start --direction); on this machine --from accepts it in place of the key.`, where the hex is the first 8 bytes of SHA-256(`impeccable-seed:` + salt + `:` + key). The salt is 128 bits from the OS CSPRNG kept per user in `~/.impeccable/seed-salt`, or, when the home cannot be written, in the project's ignored `.impeccable/questions/seed-salt` (created with create-new on first use; `IMPECCABLE_SEED_SALT` overrides both, for reproducible tests). An existing valid salt always wins over making one: an existing home salt, else an existing project salt, else a new home salt, else a new project salt, so a reference made in a sandbox keeps resolving after the home becomes writable; resolution (`--from`, `--check-seed`) accepts a reference made under any valid salt the machine keeps. A key's salt is chosen once, when its ledger is first written, and everything derives from that file (`impeccable_common::seed::locate`): the printed SEED REFERENCE, the roll record's `seed`, every ledger operation, `--from`, `--check-seed` and build-phase's `--direction`. The locator returns the key's existing ledger wherever it is (its names under the primary and every kept salt first, then a scan of the folder, for a ledger made under a salt now out of reach), whose reference is its own file name; only a key with no ledger takes the primary salt. A reference resolves by opening the file it names and reading the key recorded there. A ledger made with no salt is marked `"unsalted": true`, and one named by the no-salt tag is treated the same (ledgers written before the marker); neither yields a reference. A key the engine just generated (no `--from`, no `IMPECCABLE_CONCEPT_SEED`) skips the search: 128 random bits have no ledger, so it takes the primary salt directly. The lock checks its 3 s deadline on every pass, the stale branch included, so a lock that cannot be removed gives up rather than spin. A salt file that exists but is not 32 lowercase hex is never rewritten, and a corrupt home salt is never replaced by a new project one. With no valid salt there is no reference at all, never an unsalted hash: the roll prints `SEED REFERENCE: unavailable on this machine (...). Record no seed: write \`seed: none (unavailable)\` on the surface brief's FORM line and pass --direction none to build-phase start; never write the key.`, the roll record carries no `seed` (so the page sends no ping), and `build-phase start --direction <raw key>` stores `none`. `concept-seed --check-seed <reference>` prints `SEED KNOWN: ...` (exit 0) when this machine's ledger resolves it, else `SEED UNKNOWN: ...` (exit 1); `--check-seed none` exits 0 with `SEED NONE ACCEPTED: ...` only when this machine has no salt. The salt means a reference cannot be computed from a key by anyone without that file: holding keys (the telemetry dataset does) is not enough to find a committed brief, and a key cannot be enumerated back from its reference. New roll keys (no `--from`, no `IMPECCABLE_CONCEPT_SEED`) are 32 lowercase hex from the OS CSPRNG; an explicit `--from` key is used as given, and a short one is guessable by whoever has the salt, so its reference is no privacy boundary. The seed ledger `.impeccable/questions/seeds/<those 16 hex>.json` records `{"key"}` for every roll (telemetry plays no part), so `--from seed:<hex>` (and `IMPECCABLE_CONCEPT_SEED`) resolves to the key on the machine that rolled it and reproduces the same deal; an unknown reference exits 1 with `concept-seed: seed:<hex> is a seed reference this machine has no record of. ...` naming the way out (pass the original key, or roll anew), and on a ping path prints `choice ping skipped`.
