While telemetry is on, an API-dealt round of either scope also adds `"rounds": {"<reroll>": {scope, mode?, register?, poolRevision?, handId?, dealt: [catalog ids], recorded?}}` (no timestamps; `handId` is the roll response's opaque name for the exact hand, kept only as 32 lowercase hex and absent from older servers). Rounds of ledger files untouched for 30 days (by mtime) are pruned on write, the age and the rounds re-checked under the file's lock and a file with a pending round left alone; the `key` entry stays, so a reference never expires. Every ledger update is a read-modify-write under a per-file lock (`<file>.lock`, create-new, waits up to 3 s, a lock older than 10 s is taken over) with a unique temp name (pid, counter, clock). `build-phase start --direction` stores a reference as given and a raw key as its reference, so the build state never holds the key. **Tracked runtime files** (binary only): a `.gitignore` does not untrack files already committed, so on a roll (and on `serve-question --start`) the engine runs one `git ls-files` over `.impeccable/questions`, `.impeccable/mocks/worlds` and `.impeccable/build/state.json` when inside a work tree; when any are tracked it prints, once per distinct tracked set (remembered in the ignored `.impeccable/questions/.tracked-notice`), `TRACKED RUNTIME FILES: git already tracks <n> file(s) under .impeccable/ ... suggest they run \`git rm -r --cached <the tracked roots>\` and commit ...` before the roll text. Never fatal. **Runtime state is not added to git**: every write under `.impeccable/questions/` (roll record, hands, answers, server state, markers, ledger) first makes sure `.impeccable/questions/.gitignore` exists and lists `*` (a self-ignoring file under the header `# Written by Impeccable: local runtime state, never committed.`; missing patterns are appended, user lines kept), so a project that predates it gets it on its next write. The same helper keeps `.impeccable/mocks/worlds/.gitignore` (`*`, the kept world card copies named by dealt ids) and `.impeccable/build/.gitignore` (`state.json`, which records the seed key) in place. `serve-question` hands those card images over when the decision comps are due (**World cards**). Best effort: a write failure is silent and never fails the roll. Validation errors, the PRODUCT.md gate, and telemetry pings write nothing. `serve-question` reads it for the surface-round comp gate and removes it when a decision page takes the roll (**Missing decision comps**).
- **Client header** (binary only): every `GET ${API_BASE}/roll` and `POST ${API_BASE}/chosen` sends `X-Impeccable-Client: impeccable/<version>[ <channel>]`, matching `^impeccable/[0-9A-Za-z.+-]{1,32}( [a-z]{1,16})?$`. The version is the installed skill's SKILL.md frontmatter version, else `<engine version>+engine`; the channel is `IMPECCABLE_TELEMETRY_CHANNEL` when it is 1 to 16 lowercase letters (`test` in this repo's tests and CI). When telemetry is off (`DO_NOT_TRACK` or `IMPECCABLE_NO_TELEMETRY` non-empty and not `0`/`false` in any case, or `"telemetry": false` in `~/.impeccable/config.json` or the project's `.impeccable/config.json` / `config.local.json`, read at the working directory, the resolved project root, and the repo root, the roots `context` reads its config defaults from), the roll request is still made and also sends `X-Impeccable-Telemetry: off`; no choice ping is sent, by the agent or the page, and no telemetry field or sent mark is written.
- **`impeccable telemetry off|on|status`** (binary only, not gated by PRODUCT.md): `off` writes `"telemetry": false` into the user config `~/.impeccable/config.json` (created if missing, other keys kept, pretty JSON plus newline; a file that is not valid JSON or not an object is never overwritten: exit 1 with `telemetry: <path> is not valid JSON (...)` / `is not a JSON object; fix or remove it, then run ... again. Nothing was changed.`), `on` removes the key, and all three print one line: `telemetry: off (<reason>). Rolls still work; the roll API is told not to record them, and no choice is sent.` or `telemetry: on. ...` naming what is sent, the ways to turn it off, and https://impeccable.style/privacy. No argument means `status`; anything else exits 1 with `telemetry: unknown command "<x>"; use off, on, or status`. A project config `"telemetry": false` also turns it off for everyone in that project; nothing turns it back on over an off elsewhere. `telemetry` is a known config key for `doctor`.
- **Telemetry ping** (`--chosen`, `--kind`, and/or `--option` present; never gated by PRODUCT.md): `pingChosen({chosenId, key, scope, mode, kind, register, reroll, option})`: skipped when `IMPECCABLE_NO_TELEMETRY` or `DO_NOT_TRACK` set, `kind` not in `{assigned,pick,challenger,canon,reroll,grounded}`, `register` not `safer|bolder`, none of chosenId, kind, and option, or kind challenger without chosenId. `kind reroll` reports a rejected hand and never carries a chosenId. `--reroll <n>` is the round the choice resolved on (an integer 0 to 8) and `--option <n>` the chosen card's zero-based position (0 to 15), for a card with no ping kind; anything else is ignored. When the seed ledger holds the round for `--from <key>` (the `--reroll` round, else the latest recorded), the record fills `reroll`, `poolRevision` and `handId`; a `chosenId` that is not one of that round's dealt catalog ids is dropped while the kind is still sent; and a round already marked sent prints `choice already recorded\n` and sends nothing. With no record, the arguments go as given and `reroll` is the flag's value or 0. Else `POST ${API_BASE}/chosen` with `Content-Type: application/json`, body keys in order `chosenId?, key?, scope?, mode?, kind?, register?, option?, reroll, poolRevision?, handId?, pingId?, kindSource?, source` (`pingId`: the round's 32 random hex from the ledger, the same in the page's and the agent's send) with `source: "agent"` (no `kindSource`), abort at budget; a sent ping marks its ledger round. stdout `choice recorded\n` on success, `choice already recorded\n` as above, else `choice ping skipped\n`; always exit 0. The `TELEMETRY:` line names the flags per scope (direction: `--kind <assigned|pick|challenger|canon>` plus `--chosen <SOURCE ID>`; surface: `--kind assigned`, `--kind challenger --chosen`, or `--option <n>`; both: `--kind reroll [--register]` for a rejected hand) and adds ` --reroll <n>` on a re-roll round.
- **Card identity** (serve-question `--start` / `--update`, binary only): a payload carrying `buildPath` (a direction or surface round) is refused, exit 1, before the comp gate, unless every option declares `kind` in `{assigned, pick, challenger, grounded, dealt}` (`grounded`, any number, is another of the agent's grounded directions, such as a safer lineup card; its ping kind is `grounded`) (at most one `assigned` and one `pick`; `dealt` is a surface round's dealt structure other than the lead), every challenger declares `catalogId`, and, when a roll record younger than the roll window exists, each `catalogId` is one of its dealt world ids; a `canonCard` `kind` other than `canon` is refused too. stderr `serve-question: every card on a direction or surface page declares its identity, and these do not: <problems joined by "; ">. Set "kind" on every option: ...` naming the dealt ids, ending `Fix the payload and rerun --start; the page was not served.` or `... rerun --update on the same key; nothing was delivered.`. Other payloads are not checked.
- **Picker ping** (serve-question, binary only): when a detached page whose hand carries `seed` (an API-dealt roll of either scope, taken within the roll window) receives an answer, and telemetry is not opted out, the server sends the ping itself with `source: "picker"` and `kindSource`. A re-roll answer sends `kind: "reroll"` (`register` the answer's steer, `reroll` the rejected round). `canon` (direction only) sends `kind: "canon"`. An option card sends its declared `kind` (`kindSource: "explicit"`): `assigned`, `pick`, `challenger` with `chosenId` = its `catalogId` only when that id is in the hand's dealt worlds, and `dealt` with no kind. A card with no valid kind (older payloads, or a server started without `--start`) falls back to the conventions (`kindSource: "inferred"`): a `verdict` card is a challenger whose `chosenId` is the one dealt world its `hero`/`board` matches (URL or `.impeccable/mocks/worlds/<id>-<hero|board>.<ext>`); on a plain direction round kicker `IMPECCABLE’S PICK` is `pick` and kicker `THE ROLL` or the lead card is `assigned`; on a surface round the lead is `assigned`; anything else sends no kind. Every option-card ping carries `option`, the card's zero-based position. Every picker ping, the re-roll rejection included, carries the round's `handId` from the hand's `seed` when the roll response had one. Before the answer file is written the page claims the ledger round (`"recorded": "pending"` plus `pendingAt`, only when the round has no state, under the ledger lock); the POST runs after it, within `IMPECCABLE_API_TIMEOUT`, carrying the round's `pingId`; success sets `"recorded": "picker"`, failure clears the claim; both completions name the report by its `pingId` and change nothing when the round now carries another (a reproduced round), so a late send for the old report cannot record or release the new one. An agent rerun that finds the round pending polls it every 100 ms until `pendingAt + IMPECCABLE_API_TIMEOUT + 1 s`: confirmed means `choice already recorded`; a failed or still-pending send is sent by the agent with the same `pingId` (a double send the server can drop by `pingId`, rather than a lost report) and then marked `agent`.
- **Exit**: destroys undici global dispatcher then `process.exit(exitCode ?? 0)`.
- Tests: `tests/concept-seed.test.mjs` (scopes reproducibility, degraded seed, register, ratings/breadth/mode/grain/platform, init gate, `pingChosen` validation & opt-out, API roll path + dispatcher destroy).

#### `serve-question.mjs` -> `node {{scripts_path}}/serve-question.mjs`

- **Invoked from**: `reference/new-work.md` "Run `node {{scripts_path}}/serve-question.mjs --start --payload <file>` (`--schema` first for the payload shape). It daemonizes, prints the page URL and a key, and exits; open that URL for the user, in-app browser first, then the system opener, then showing the URL. Collect the choice with `--wait --key <key>`, repeating while it exits 3; the ANSWER prints as JSON. An ANSWER of `{"optionId":"reroll"}` keeps the server alive..."; `visualize.md:24` "Show the three together on the decision page (`serve-question.mjs`, one option per comp with the comp as its hero)"; `new-work.md` "each card carries a `wireframe` schematic (`serve-question.mjs --schema`)". Degraded concept-seed also names it.
- **Arg helper**: `arg(name)` = value after `--name` unless it starts with `--`; `hasFlag`.
- **Env gates** (evaluated at top): `IMPECCABLE_QUESTION_DISABLED` → `stdout> serve-question: disabled in this session (no browser); use the structured question tool instead.`, exit 2. Headless detection only when `wantsBrowser = !--no-open && !--wait && !--stop && !--schema && !--update` and `IMPECCABLE_QUESTION_FORCE` unset: `CI || (SSH_CONNECTION && !DISPLAY) || (linux && !DISPLAY && !WAYLAND_DISPLAY)` → `stdout> serve-question: no browser detected in this environment (CI/headless/remote); use the structured question tool instead. Set IMPECCABLE_QUESTION_FORCE=1 to serve anyway.`, exit 2.
- **Common params**: `--payload <file>` (else stdin), `--timeout <sec>` (default 900; `0` = wait forever for a page; negative/NaN → 900), `--idle-grace <sec>` (default 600; ≤0/NaN → 600), `--port <n>` (default 0 = ephemeral), `--key`, `--no-open`, `--open`, `--poll <sec>` (wait, default 60). State dir `QUESTION_DIR = <root>/.impeccable/questions/`, where `<root>` is cwd, except for `--wait`, `--update` and `--stop` with `--key K` when cwd holds no `<key>.state.json`: those read `~/.impeccable/questions/<key>.root.json` (`{"root": <cwd of --start>}`, written by a successful `--start`, removed by `--stop` and by a `--wait` that collects a closing answer, each only while it still names that root) and use that root when its `<key>.state.json` exists, so an agent whose shell cwd was reset still reaches the round (`--update` still resolves `--payload` against cwd). Files `<key>.state.json` (`{pid, port, url, comps?, lastBeat?, claimedAt?}`; `comps` is the live round's local decision comp paths, see **Decision comps** below), `<key>.hand.json` and `<key>.generated/` (the hand's provenance, **Decision comps**), `<key>.answer.json`, `<key>.flip.json`, `<key>.next.json`, `<key>.log`. `NEXT_CLAIM_GRACE_MS = 10000`.
- **Modes**:
  - `--schema`: prints canonical payload JSON (2-space) then a long explanatory paragraph; exit 0. (Copy verbatim from source lines 210-232.)
  - `--wait --key K [--poll N]`: loops up to N s (1 s ticks): answer file → break; flip file exists → delete it, `stdout> BUILD PATH FLIPPED: comp (for this session only; never write it to settings). The table is still open and the page shows shimmer where the images will land: generate each open card’s comp into its declared path now, lead first, then collect the answer with --wait again. A card whose comp already exists needs nothing.` followed by the `NEXT` visualize line (**Decision comps**), exit 0; no state file for the key in the resolved `QUESTION_DIR` → `stdout> serve-question: no state file for key ${K} in ${QUESTION_DIR}, and no live round recorded for it elsewhere. If this key's answer was already collected or --stop ran, the round is over; otherwise rerun --wait from the directory where --start ran. The page may still be open: do not --start a second server.` exit 2, checked before the dead-server test; server dead (no fresh `lastBeat` <12 s and `process.kill(pid,0)` fails with non-EPERM) → `stdout> serve-question: the question server is gone with no answer. This is a server failure, not a user decision: restart it with --start --key ${K} and the same payload, reopen the URL for the user, and wait again. Never proceed without their choice while their browser session is open.` exit 2; `lastBeat` older than 90 s (`PAGE_CLOSED_GRACE_MS`, past the once-a-minute timer cadence of a long-hidden Chrome tab) and not mid-delivery (next.json mtime <10 s or `claimedAt` <10 s) → `PAGE CLOSED: the question page went away without an answer; re-present, reopen the URL, or fall back to the structured question tool` exit 4; no answer after poll → `WAITING: no answer yet after ${N}s; run --wait --key ${K} again` exit 3; answer → `printAnswer`, delete answer file, delete state and hand files unless optionId `reroll` or `followup:true`; exit 0. Missing `--key` → `stderr> serve-question: --wait needs --key` exit 1.
  - `--stop --key K`: kill pid from state, remove answer/state/hand, `stdout> stopped`, exit 0.
  - `--update --key K --payload F`: payload must have non-empty `options` (else stderr `serve-question: --update payload needs an options array; nothing was delivered. Fix the payload and rerun --update on the same key.` exit 1); then the comp-led direction and surface gate (**Missing decision comps** below; stderr, exit 1, `nothing was delivered`, before the liveness check, `<key>.next.json`, the hand file, or the roll record are touched); server liveness like wait (no state file for the key → stderr `serve-question: no state file for key ${K} in ${QUESTION_DIR}, and no live round recorded for it elsewhere. If this key's answer was already collected or --stop ran, the round is over; otherwise rerun --update from the directory where --start ran. The page may still be open: do not --start a second server.` exit 2; dead → stderr `serve-question: no live question server for that key; the page it served is gone too. Re-present the round with --start and a fresh key, or fall back to the structured question tool.` exit 2); writes a new hand to `<key>.hand.json`, removes the roll record when the payload carries `buildPath`, copies payload to `<key>.next.json`, touches mtime, `stdout> next round delivered; the page reloads itself`, then the `NEXT` visualize line when the new round's comps are due (**Decision comps**), exit 0.
  - `--start --payload F [--key K] [--open] [--timeout] [--idle-grace]`: validates JSON, refuses a comp-led direction or surface round that leaves a card without a decision comp (**Missing decision comps** below; stderr, exit 1, before the hand is recorded, the roll record removed, the old state removed, or anything spawned), decides the hand and writes `<key>.hand.json` when it is new (**Decision comps**), removes the roll record when the payload carries `buildPath`, removes the key's previous state file without signalling its pid, spawns detached child `node serve-question.mjs --payload F --detached-serve --key K --timeout T [--idle-grace G] [--no-open unless --open]` with stdio to `<key>.log`; waits ≤8 s for state file; failure → stderr `serve-question: server failed to start` + last 4 log lines + `serve-question: log at <rel>. A sandboxed exec that cannot listen on localhost causes exactly this; rerun this command once through the harness's network-enabled or unsandboxed command tool before falling back.` exit 1; success stdout: `QUESTION URL: ${url}`, `QUESTION KEY: ${key}` (after writing the key's `<key>.root.json` pointer, see the state dir above; when that write fails, first `stdout> serve-question: could not record this round's directory in ${pointer} (${error}); run --wait, --update and --stop for this key from this directory.`), `Open the URL for the user now: in-app browser when the harness has one, otherwise the system opener (macOS \`open\`, Linux \`xdg-open\`), otherwise show the URL.`, the `NEXT` visualize line when the payload's comps are due (**Decision comps**), `Then collect the answer with: node <abs script> --wait --key ${key}`; exit 0. Key default `Math.random().toString(16).slice(2,10)`.
  - default (blocking): loads payload (error → stderr `serve-question: payload needs an options array` exit 1), listens on `127.0.0.1:${port}`, `stdout> QUESTION URL: http://127.0.0.1:${port}/` and `Waiting for the user to choose in the browser (Ctrl-C aborts)...`, opens browser unless `--no-open`; on answer prints via `printAnswer` and exits 0 after 150 ms. Detached mode writes state instead of printing.
- **Missing decision comps** (engine addition, not in the JS): `--start` and `--update` refuse a payload that is a comp-led direction round (or, per **Surface rounds** below, surface round) with image generation while some card that owes a decision comp declares none. The round owes them when all of these hold: `buildPath.value` is `"comp"` and `buildPath.toggle` is `true` (the toggle is only offered where image generation exists; a code-led round's slots are a flip reserve); the payload carries the canon exit, a truthy `canon` or an object `canonCard` (direction rounds only: surface rounds and the comp round never carry it); and `options` has at least two entries (a degraded roll's single text-only card declares no comp). The cards that owe one are every option whose `verdict` is not `"declined"`, plus `canonCard` when present; a card declares one with a truthy `comp` or legacy `sketch` (a URL counts). Missing cards are named by `id` (`options[<n>]` when the option has none, `canonCard` for the canon card), in card order. Message on stderr, exit 1 (exit 2 keeps its fallback meaning): `serve-question: this direction round is comp-led with image generation (buildPath value "comp", toggle true), so every card declares a decision comp, canon included, declined challengers excepted; ${outcome}. Missing a "comp": ${ids joined by ", "}. Give each of those cards "comp": ".impeccable/mocks/decision/<card id>.png" (the canon card's goes inside canonCard), then ${rerun}; the comps are generated after the page serves, as its NEXT line says.` with `${outcome}`/`${rerun}` = `the page was not served`/`rerun --start` for `--start` and `nothing was delivered`/`rerun --update on the same key` for `--update`. **Surface rounds**: the payload alone cannot tell a surface round (three dealt structures as full cards, no canon, no pick card) from the comp round or any other canon-less page, so the engine links it to concept-seed's roll record (`.impeccable/questions/roll.json`, **Roll record** under concept-seed). When the payload owes no direction comps and the record exists, parses, has `scope` `"surface"`, and its `at` is less than one hour old, a payload with no canon exit that is comp-led with image generation and carries at least two options owes a comp on every option whose `verdict` is not `"declined"`, named as above. Message: `serve-question: this surface round (concept-seed's latest roll here was --scope surface) is comp-led with image generation (buildPath value "comp", toggle true), so every dealt card declares a decision comp; ${outcome}. Missing a "comp": ${ids joined by ", "}. Give each of those cards "comp": ".impeccable/mocks/decision/<card id>.png", then ${rerun}; the comps are generated after the page serves, as its NEXT line says.` with the same `${outcome}`/`${rerun}` pairs. The record binds a roll to the one page that presents it: a refusal leaves it in place, and a `--start` that records its hand, or an `--update` that records its hand, removes it when the payload carries a non-null `buildPath` (only direction and surface rounds do), whatever the round's build path. The record is read once, before the gate, and removed only while the file still holds that same text: a newer roll another session recorded in between stays for its own page. So a later page in the same project (the comp round, a page after a direction roll, anything after the surface round was served) is not gated by it, and a page without `buildPath` (an unrelated question) neither takes nor is gated by it. Checked after the JSON parse (and, for `--update`, the options check) and before anything else; the env and headless gates run first, so a session that falls back to the structured tool never sees it. The blocking (non-`--start`) server does not check.
- **Decision comps** (engine addition, not in the JS): a round's declared comps are the local paths in each option's `comp` (or legacy `sketch`) and `canonCard.comp`, in card order, deduplicated; URLs are left out. The detached server writes them to the state file as `comps` (omitted when empty) and rewrites them when it claims a `--update` round.
  - **Hands and provenance.** Each served hand's provenance lives in `<key>.hand.json`, `{"id": "<per-hand id>", "digest": "<first 16 hex of sha256 over the compact JSON of the whole payload>", "comps": [<declared comps>], "pre": {"<slot>": "<sha256 hex of the file bytes>"}}`, plus `"worlds": [...]` last when the hand was dealt catalog worlds (**World cards**). Only `--start` and `--update` write it, atomically (a temp file beside it, then a rename), and a failed write fails the command: `--start` prints `serve-question: could not record the hand (<file>: <error>); the page was not served. Make .impeccable/questions/ writable and rerun --start.` on stderr and exits 1 before spawning the server or touching the old state; `--update` prints `serve-question: could not record the hand (<file>: <error>); nothing was delivered. Make .impeccable/questions/ writable and rerun --update on the same key.` and exits 1 before copying `<key>.next.json`, so the page keeps the previous round with its hand file intact; the server never does, so its heartbeat and claim rewrites of the state file cannot drop a hand. `--update` always starts a new hand. `--start` keeps the recorded hand when the key's hand file carries the same digest (a restart) and otherwise starts a new one. A new hand fingerprints every declared slot that already holds a file into `pre`. The digest covers the whole payload, not only the slot paths, because a re-roll often reuses the previous hand's paths. `impeccable generate-image`, after writing `--out`, scans `.impeccable/questions/*.hand.json` for a hand that declares that path (resolved against the cwd; compared case-insensitively on Windows) and marks it generated during that hand with a marker file `<key>.generated/<16 hex of sha256 over the JSON string of the slot>-<hand tag>.json` = `{"slot", "hand"}` (the hand's `id`), written atomically. The hand tag is the hand's `id` (a hand file without an `id` uses its `digest`) when that is 1 to 64 characters of `[a-z0-9_]`, else the first 16 hex of sha256 over its JSON string (lowercase only, so ids that differ in case never share a path on a case-insensitive file system). Markers are one file per slot rather than a list inside the hand file because parallel generators (one subagent per card) would race a shared read-modify-write, and the tag in the name keeps one hand's markers off every path another hand writes. A marker counts only while its name carries the hand file's tag, so a re-roll that repeats a payload never inherits the previous hand's markers. Writing a new hand writes the hand file first and only then prunes, deciding from the file name alone: it deletes only markers (and their temp files) tagged with another hand, so a failed write leaves the live hand and its markers intact and a parallel generation's marker for the new hand is never touched, even when it regenerates a reused slot while the prune runs (#886). A legacy untagged marker `<16 hex>.json` (written before names carried the hand) counts, and survives a prune, only while its content's `hand` (else `digest`) matches the hand's `id` (else `digest`); the engine never writes that name again. `--stop` and a closing answer remove `<key>.generated/` with the hand file. A failed marker write prints `generate-image: could not mark <out> as generated for its decision page (<file>: <error>); a --wait may report it stale` on stderr and leaves the exit at 0, since the image itself was written. Readers merge the valid markers into the hand as `generated: [<slot>, ...]`. A declared comp is **this hand's** when its file exists and its slot is in `generated`, has no `pre` entry, or its current bytes hash differently from `pre`; timestamps play no part, so an old file rewritten unchanged in the same second is still not this hand's, while a deterministic generator's identical regeneration still counts through `generated`. Rewriting a comp's prompt sidecar never counts on its own: only new bytes or an engine generation do. Two exceptions: a comp directly in `.impeccable/mocks/` is the comp round's, generated before that round serves, and counts whenever it exists; with no hand file, existence decides. `--wait` judges the hand file's `comps`, else the state's. A declared comp that exists but is not this hand's is **stale**. The page itself still shows whatever file sits at a slot; hiding stale files there is out of scope.
  - Four directives follow:
  - `NEXT read ${skillDir}/reference/visualize.md now, before writing any decision comp prompt; it and the MODE RULES block concept-seed printed for this surface govern every card's image. Then generate each declared comp into its slot, lead first, and record the exact prompt in its sidecar, the image's full file name plus .json (a.png gets a.png.json).` The path is the provider's skill dir (`IMPECCABLE_SKILL_DIR`, else the one found beside the binary); with neither it reads `reference/visualize.md`. Printed by `--start` between the `Open the URL` line and the `Then collect` line, and by `--update` after `next round delivered`, when `buildPath.value` is not `code` (a code-led round's slots are a flip reserve) and some declared comp is not this hand's yet; and by `--wait` right after `BUILD PATH FLIPPED`, when the state has no `comps` or one of them is not this hand's. The engine cannot see a harness-native image tool, so a slot without this hand's comp is the signal that comps are about to be written. So a fresh decision round (serve first, generate after) gets the line even when an earlier round left files at its slots; a restart of the same hand after its comps landed does not; a re-roll through `--update` that reuses slot paths does, and so does a restart of that re-roll before its comps land; the comp round, whose comps sit directly in `mocks/`, does not.
  - **World cards** (engine addition): when the hand was dealt catalog worlds, that same `NEXT` line continues, before its newline, with ` The catalog worlds dealt in this roll have card images, listed at the end of this line: when a card's direction builds on one of these worlds, attach that world's board and hero as reference images to that card's comp (the harness image tool's input images, or \`${impeccable} generate-image --ref <board> --ref <hero>\`${unfetched}), as style inspiration only, never to copy layout or content; a card for a direction you invented yourself has no card images and gets none. WORLD CARDS: ${worlds joined by " | "}` where each world is `${id} board ${board} hero ${hero}` and `${unfetched}` is `; any image listed as a URL could not be fetched, so download it into the workspace first when the tool takes files` when some image is named by URL, else empty. The list closes the line so no path or URL touches sentence punctuation. Models skip the prose rule that says to open the card images and follow an engine line, and a harness image tool takes reference images only as workspace files, so the engine fetches them. **Which worlds**: a new hand copies the roll record's `worlds` (**Roll record** under concept-seed; the record text read once before the gate, since the page then removes it) when the payload carries a non-null `buildPath` (the page that takes the roll), the record parses, its `scope` is `"direction"`, and its `at` is less than one hour old. A surface roll hands over none: its challengers change topology under an identity already committed. An entry is kept only when its `id` is 1 to 120 characters of `[A-Za-z0-9._-]` not starting with a dot (it becomes a file name) and `board` and `hero` are `http://` or `https://` URLs of at most 500 characters with no whitespace, control character, `|` or backtick. The payload is never consulted for which card builds on which world (its `hero` / `board` fields are model-authored and optional), so every dealt world is listed once. The worlds live in the hand file, so a `--start` restart of the same hand and the `--wait` flip line name them too. **Fetching**: right before the line prints, each listed world's board and hero go to `.impeccable/mocks/worlds/<id>-board.<ext>` and `<id>-hero.<ext>` (`<ext>` is `webp`, `png` or `jpg`, from the fetched bytes' signature, so a later `--ref` of the path sends the right type). A file already there under `webp`, `png`, `jpg` or `jpeg` (looked for in that order) is kept and not requested. The rest download in parallel, each one GET under an 8 s timeout through the shared agent (OS trust store, env proxies), following no redirect, accepted like a `generate-image --ref` URL (2xx, non-empty, at most 30 MB, a PNG, WebP or JPEG by signature, so an HTML page served at a card URL is never kept as a card), written to a temp file and renamed. **Which URLs the engine requests unasked**: the record is a project file, so a crafted one must not turn `--start` into a probe of loopback, link-local or private services. A URL is requested only when it starts with the configured card base plus `/` (`IMPECCABLE_CARD_BASE`, else `https://impeccable.style/worlds/cards`), or is `https` to a named host with a dot in it whose last label is letters only or punycode (so no IP address in any spelling: `127.0.0.1`, `127.1`, `0x7f.1`, a bracketed IPv6), carries no userinfo, and is not `localhost` or under `.localhost`, `.local`, `.internal`, `.lan` or `.home.arpa`. Any other URL is never requested and is listed by URL. (`generate-image --ref <url>` is an explicit request and stays unrestricted.) Best effort: a failed, blocked or refused download changes no exit code and prints nothing; that image is named by its URL instead of its path. With `IMPECCABLE_IMAGE_GEN_FAKE` set no request is made: files already there are named by path, the rest by URL. With no world to list (no roll record, an unreadable or expired one, a surface or degraded roll, a page without `buildPath`) the line is exactly the one above and nothing is fetched or created.
  - `COMP SIDECAR MISSING: ${paths joined by ", "} landed with no prompt sidecar. Every decision comp records the exact prompt that produced it in its sidecar, the image's full file name plus .json (a.png gets a.png.json), as {"prompt": "..."} (generate-image writes it itself, a harness image tool does not), and that prompt is written under the comp rules in ${visualize path}. Write each missing sidecar now, and read that file before the next comp prompt if you have not.` Printed by `--wait` as the last line of a WAITING (exit 3) or answered (exit 0) return when a state `comps` path is this hand's comp and has no `<path>.json`. Comps that have not landed, and stale files, are skipped. It is a warning: exit codes and the answer are unchanged, because the user is mid-decision in the browser and blocking `--wait` would strand their answer.
  - `SKETCH STALE: these sketch files predate this round, so the page is still waiting on them: ${paths joined by ", "}. Rewrite each now; the page shows a sketch only once it is written after the round was served.` Printed by a WAITING return for every path in the hand's `sketches` that exists and is not this hand's (written after the hand's `startedAt`, or differing from its `pre` fingerprint).
  - `COMP STALE: files left from an earlier round sit at this hand's slots, and the page is showing them: ${paths joined by ", "}. Regenerate each into its slot now under the comp rules in ${visualize path}, with its prompt sidecar, before the user decides.` Printed by `--wait` as the last line of a WAITING return (after `COMP SIDECAR MISSING` when both apply) when a state `comps` path is stale. Never on an answered return: once the user picks, the image they saw is the one they chose.
  - `NEXT open each decision comp once and run ${visualize path}'s render checks (shipped screen, one dominant move); regenerate any that fail before the user answers.` Printed by `--wait` as the last line of a WAITING return (after `COMP SIDECAR MISSING` and `COMP STALE` when they apply) when at least one state `comps` path outside `.impeccable/mocks/` itself (a decision comp, not a comp-round comp) is this hand's comp and has its `<path>.json` sidecar. Once per hand: the first such return writes the hand's `id` (else its `digest`, else the empty string with no hand file) to `<key>.render-check`, and a later return finds that id there and stays quiet; a new hand carries a new id, so its round prints the line again. A code-led round's slots stay empty or stale until a flip to comp, so it gets no line. Never on an answered return. `--stop` and a closing answer remove `<key>.render-check` with the hand file.
- **Payload processing** (`loadRound`): options decorated with `heroSrc`/`boardSrc` (http(s) URL passthrough; local path must exist → `/img/<i>`), `htmlSrc` (`html`, a local path only, registered even if missing → `/sketch/<i>`; URLs ignored), `compSrc` (`comp` or legacy `sketch`; local path registered even if missing); `verdict==='declined'` cards moved to the end; `canonCard` appended as `{...card, id:'canon', isCanon:true}` before declined; `buildPath` `{value:'comp'|'code', toggle:bool}`.
- **HTTP routes**: `GET /` → HTML page (claims `<key>.next.json` if present, stamps `claimedAt`); `POST /heartbeat` → 204, updates `lastBeat` in state at most every 4 s; `GET /next-status` → `{"ready":bool}`; `GET /sketch/<n>` → the declared HTML sketch as `text/html` with `cache-control: no-store`, `nosniff`, `referrer-policy: no-referrer` and a CSP that sandboxes it and allows only inline styles, Google Fonts and data/blob images (`script-src 'unsafe-inline'` with `sandbox allow-scripts` for the skeleton view, `script-src 'none'` with `sandbox` for raw) and the skeleton script injected before `</body>` (`?raw=1` serves the file as written), 404 until the file exists and is this hand's (written after the hand began, the comps' fingerprint rule; with no hand, existence decides); `GET /img/<n>` → local file stream (content-type by ext: webp/png/svg+xml/gif, else jpeg; 404 if missing); `POST /build-path` `{value}` → `{"ok":true}`, writes `<key>.flip.json` `{"buildPath":"comp"}` on a flip to comp; `POST /answer` `{optionId, steer, register?}` → `{"ok":true}` and answer JSON `{optionId, steer, [register], [followup:true], [hero, board], [html], [comp], [buildPath, buildPathFlipped]}` (`html` only when the picked card's sketch is this hand's file and the live build path is not `comp`) ` written to answer file (detached) or printed; process exits 150 ms later unless reroll/followup in detached mode; else 404.
- **Page** (server-rendered HTML; `<title>${payload.title || 'impeccable · decision'}</title>`, `<h1>` payload.title or `Choose a direction`): cards with kicker, media (comp with shimmer + polling until `/img/<n>` returns 200; hero/board inspiration; HTML sketch (a sandboxed `allow-scripts` iframe at the card's `htmlFrame`, 1440 × 900 by default, scaled to the slot, whose aspect ratio follows the frame and turns portrait like a portrait comp, shimmer and polling until `/sketch/<n>` returns 200, revealed when the skeleton pass posts `{impeccableSketch:'ready'}` or 4 s after load; the expand chip and a click on the slot open `/sketch/<n>?raw=1` in a new tab; a comp face wins over it); wireframe grid), thesis, palette swatches (≤6), material tags (≤4), raise lines, facts, `Build this` / `Adopt anyway` (declined) / `Play it straight` (canon) buttons; optional `#steer` input (placeholder `Optional steer: what should be different or kept?`), `Re-roll` button with optional safer/bolder register buttons, `#canon` footer button when `canon && !canonCard`, build-path toggle. Client heartbeats every 5000 ms via `navigator.sendBeacon('/heartbeat')`; posts `/answer`; after reroll/followup polls `/next-status` and reloads.
- **printAnswer**: `ANSWER: ${raw}` then conditional directive lines `CHOSEN CARD: ...`, `CHOSEN SKETCH: the HTML sketch at that path is the composition the user picked. ...` (when the answer carries `html`), `CHOSEN COMP: ...` (or, engine addition, `APPROVED COMP: the user picked this composition in the comp round, so it is the approved comp. Set "approved": true in its prompt sidecar, the image's full file name plus .json (a.png gets a.png.json), record its path in the surface brief, then close the comps phase with build-phase advance. Build from it as it stands; never regenerate it.` in its place when the answer's `comp` sits directly in `.impeccable/mocks/`, where the comp round writes, or is the `decisionComp` that `.impeccable/build/state.json` records while its phase is `comps` and the page also serves a comp directly in `.impeccable/mocks/` (the comp round; under the same condition that comp counts as the served hand's own, so serving it prints no `NEXT read` and no `COMP STALE` for it, while a direction round reusing its slot still flags it stale); other decision comps under `.impeccable/mocks/decision/` and any other path keep `CHOSEN COMP`, whose comp-led clause names the flag: `On a comp-led build pass it to build-phase start as --decision-comp <that path>, and the comp round adds two variations beside it where it stands;`), `CANON CHOSEN: ...`, `REGISTER: ...`, `FOLLOWUP OPEN: ...`, `BUILD PATH: ${value} (${origin}). ...` (verbatim in source lines 152-190).
- **Lifetime**: no page ever beat → exit 2 `serve-question: timed out with no answer` after `--timeout` (unless 0); after beats, silence > idle-grace (with 10 s claim window for a delivered next hand) → exit 2 `serve-question: the page stopped beating and never came back; exiting`.
- **Browser opener** (`lib/open-system-browser.mjs`): darwin `open <url>`; win32 `${ComSpec||COMSPEC||'cmd.exe'} /c start "" <url>`; else `xdg-open <url>`; spawned `detached, stdio:'ignore'`, `unref`, errors swallowed; returns true/false.
- Tests: `tests/serve-question.test.mjs` (opener commands, blocking answer, reroll, start/wait cycle, headless gating, empty payload, heartbeat/timeouts, refresh semantics, --update, comps streaming), `tests/new-work-e2e.test.mjs`.

#### `generate-image.mjs` -> `node {{scripts_path}}/generate-image.mjs`

- **Invoked from**: `context.mjs appendImageGenDirective` (only when `OPENAI_API_KEY` set): "IMAGE_GEN_AVAILABLE: ... `node ${scriptsPath}/generate-image.mjs --prompt "..." --out <file>` (gpt-image-2, billed to the user's key; say so before the first render, and never reach for it when a native tool exists)."; `new-work.md` ("the harness image tool's input image, or `generate-image.mjs --ref`"); `visualize.md:30` ("every comp generated through `generate-image.mjs` has one [.json sidecar]").
- **Args**: `--prompt <text>` or `--prompt-file <path>` (file wins), `--out <path>` (required), `--size <WxH>` (default `1536x1024`), `--quality` (default `medium`), `--ref <img>` repeatable; `<img>` is a file path or, engine addition, an `http://` / `https://` URL.
- **Fake mode** `IMPECCABLE_IMAGE_GEN_FAKE=1`: no network; `.svg` out → SVG with prompt-hashed 2-3 color gradient (FNV-1a), wrapped prompt text, `SYNTHETIC COMP` label; otherwise a minimal PNG (palette stripes, `tEXt` chunk `Comment\0SYNTHETIC COMP: <prompt>`); size parsed by `/^(\d+)x(\d+)$/` else 1536x1024; `stdout> IMAGE: ${out} (${w}x${h}, fake synthetic comp, $0.00, no API call)`; exit 0. Missing prompt/out → stderr `generate-image: --prompt (or --prompt-file) and --out are required.` exit 1.
- **Real mode**: no `OPENAI_API_KEY` → stderr `generate-image: OPENAI_API_KEY is not set; use the harness-native image tool instead.` exit 1. Without refs: `POST https://api.openai.com/v1/images/generations`, headers `Authorization: Bearer <key>`, `content-type: application/json`, body `{"model":"gpt-image-2","prompt":...,"size":...,"quality":...,"n":1}`. With refs: `POST https://api.openai.com/v1/images/edits` multipart FormData fields `model=gpt-image-2`, `prompt`, `size`, `quality`, `n=1`, `image[]` blobs (type png/webp else jpeg, filename basename). Non-ok → stderr `generate-image: API error ${status}: ${first 300 chars}` exit 1; no `data[0].b64_json` → `generate-image: no image in response` exit 1. Writes decoded bytes to `--out`; best-effort `node embed-prompt.mjs <out> --prompt <prompt>` and sidecar `${out}.json` = `{prompt, createdAt: ISO, tool: 'generate-image.mjs', model: 'gpt-image-2', [refs]}` (2-space). stdout `IMAGE: ${out} (${size}, ${quality}, gpt-image-2, billed to your OpenAI key); prompt embedded + sidecar at ${out}.json`; exit 0.
- **URL refs** (engine addition, real mode): a `--ref` starting with `http://` or `https://` is downloaded (one GET, 30 s timeout, redirects followed, through the shared agent with the OS trust store and env proxies) after the key and argument checks and before the generation request, then attached as an `image[]` part like a file ref, in argument order with the file refs. Its content type is the one the bytes' signature names (PNG, WebP, JPEG), and a signature is required: the response `Content-Type` and the URL's extension decide nothing, so a `200` HTML page behind a `.webp` URL (a soft 404, an auth wall, a proxy interstitial) is refused rather than uploaded as an image. Its file name is the URL path's last segment (`ref` when there is none). The sidecar `refs` records the URL string itself. A download that fails prints `generate-image: could not download --ref ${url}: ${reason}; nothing was generated.` on stderr and exits 1 with no generation request, no image and no sidecar; `${reason}` is `HTTP ${status}`, `the request failed` (no response: refused, unresolvable, timed out), `the response was cut short`, `the response was empty`, `the image is larger than 30 MB`, or `the response is not a png, webp, or jpeg image`. Fake mode never reads `--ref`, so it requests nothing.
- **Decision slots** (engine addition, both modes): after the image is written, `--out` is marked generated in any recorded decision hand that declares it (`serve-question` **Decision comps**); a failed mark is a stderr warning, never an exit change.
- Tests: `tests/new-work-e2e.test.mjs` (fake mode chain, opt-in `test:new-work-e2e`).

#### comp-fidelity verbs: `comp-spec` / `comp-diff` / `font-match` / `build-phase`

Ported from the former `skill/scripts/{comp-spec,comp-diff,font-match,build-phase}.mjs` (+ `lib/{png,raster,image-metrics,font-fingerprint,font-index,hero-checks}.mjs`) into the engine; invoked as `{{scripts_path}}/impeccable <verb>`. All four resolve paths against the process cwd. Printed commands spell the launcher via `IMPECCABLE_SELF` (default `impeccable`), so they name `{{scripts_path}}/impeccable <verb>`, never `node …mjs`. When `IMPECCABLE_SELF` is the Windows `impeccable.cmd` launcher and the printed spelling quotes it, it is printed as a PowerShell call on a single-quoted literal (`& '<path>' <verb>`, with any `'` doubled), since PowerShell reads `"<path>" <verb>` as a string expression and expands `$` and backticks inside double quotes (#744); this rule holds for every verb that prints the launcher. ISO `createdAt`/`startedAt` timestamps in stdout and written JSON are the only run-dependent output.

- **`comp-spec`** — turns an approved comp into a measured build spec. `--schema` prints the region-map JSON schema without reading or writing a project. `--comp <png> --regions <json> --inspect-map` writes reference-only crops, overlay and report in a new directory; exit 2 means diagnosed hard errors, exit 1 means command/input failure. Masked assets also produce paginated, comp-derived `comparison-N.png` sheets (six original/checker pairs per image, matched scale and aspect ratio), listed by `COMPARE` lines and the JSON `comparisonSheets` array. Every affected instance is retained; sheets do not change matcher inputs or approvals. Partial foreground masks are warnings naming the excluding regions and remain in the inspector’s main inspection queue even with zero hard errors. The CLI summary counts them separately; they do not imply an asset failure or visual approval. Code rectangles exclude their full overlap, so separate text elements need separate bounds. Mapping metadata (`parentId`, `reviewGroup`) survives measurement. Review groups must contain peer code regions of one kind and container status; mixed groups and grouped raster assets are refused both at measurement and at component-review preparation. `--comp <png> --grid` writes `.impeccable/build/comp-grid.png` (10x10 labeled grid) and prints PALETTE/BANDS/NEXT; `--comp <png> --regions <json>` measures regions into `.impeccable/build/spec.json` (region box, sampled palette, medium, aspect, detail energy, plate path for raster kinds) and prints the spec; `--comp <png> --auto [--out <draft.json>]` writes approximate bands to a new draft file (default `.impeccable/build/regions.draft.json`), without modifying the measured spec or build state; `--print` prints the compact spec; `--crop <id> [--out f] [--scale n] [--raw]` writes a reference crop; `--plate-prompt <id>` prints the regeneration prompt. `--spec <path>` overrides the spec path (default `.impeccable/build/spec.json`). Validation refusals (stderr, exit 1) are the JS strings verbatim: a region with no id / duplicate id / no note (bands included) / a missing or unknown kind, a `box` that is not numeric, leaves the 0..1 frame, or rounds below one comp pixel, a code-kind region whose note names painted material, a code region over 25% of the comp, a grid span that is not `<colrow>:<colrow>`, uncovered ink cells without `allowUncovered`. Every `text`, `control` and `chrome` region (containers included) gets `surface: {flat, rules}` and, when any 64px window of its comp crop reads as painted (pixels inside raster regions left out), `flags: [{"id":"painted-pixels","message":...}]` plus a `FLAG <id> painted-pixels: <message> <agent advice>` line in the printed spec; docs/PLAN-REVIEW.md defines both readings. The flag is never a refusal, and `codeDrawn` does not suppress it. A raster region whose note names a frame and then the view it opens onto gets `flags: [{"id":"baked-composite","message":...}]` and its own `FLAG` line (docs/PLAN-REVIEW.md). Apart from these additions, spec.json is byte-identical to the JS output.
  Automatic drafts require decomposition into actual visible elements before measurement; a draft flag blocks accidental submission, and existing draft files are never overwritten. Successful `--regions` measurements record the source path and SHA-256. Spec and plate gates reject changed or missing source files, so a failed region edit cannot silently reuse the previous measurements. Legacy specs without source metadata remain readable.
  Region inputs support three coordinate representations: inclusive `grid` cells, normalized `box: {x,y,w,h}`, or `pixelBox: {x,y,w,h}` in whole original-comp pixels. Pixel boxes cannot be combined with the other formats and must be positive-sized and contained in the comp; they avoid snapping an asset boundary to a neighbouring grid cell. Foreground UI excluded from a plate reference is excluded at the same aligned coordinates from the candidate during scoring; unmasked asset bytes still undergo provenance checks.
- **`comp-diff`** — `--comp <png> --build <png> [--spec spec.json] [--out-dir dir] [--align top|stretch|cover] [--label name] [--threshold t] [--json] [--no-files]`. Scores structure / color / detail / bands and per-region verdicts (`match`/`drift`/`missing`/`contradicted`); writes `side-by-side.png`, `heatmap.png`, `regions/<id>.png`, and `report.json` under `--out-dir` (unless `--no-files`); prints the text summary or, with `--json`, the report. Exit 0 measured, 1 usage/unreadable input, 2 the approved comp changed (see the fixed-reference paragraph under `build-phase`), 3 below `--threshold`. The JSON report and text summary are byte-identical to the JS.
- **`font-match`** — `--measure <text-region-id>` fingerprints the comp crop of a text region (cap height, width/weight class, shape vector), records it on the region's `type` block in the spec, and prints the MEASURE line (pure; byte-identical to the JS). `--rank <id> [--candidates "Family:700,…"] [--text "…"] [--transform …] [--category …]` additionally renders candidate faces in a headless browser and ranks them by fingerprint distance, writing a stamped `chosen` face onto the region and a proof sheet under `.impeccable/build/font-match/`. **Browser**: an installed Chrome discovered and driven over CDP (the same browser the URL engine uses; the JS used Playwright/Puppeteer). With no browser resolvable, the catalog's nearest face is recorded (source `catalog`, estimated size) or, with no catalog either, the MEASURE line stands — matching the JS fallbacks; the sha1 `chosen` stamp is byte-identical. Screenshots vary by Chrome version, so the rendered ranking is not byte-stable.
- **`font-index` catalog (paid moat)** — resolved at run time, never committed to the engine repo: `IMPECCABLE_CATALOG_DIR/font-index.json` first, then the skill's shipped `IMPECCABLE_SKILL_DIR/scripts/data/font-index.json`; absent → the built-in per-width shortlist stands in (the JS degraded path).
- **`build-phase`** — the comp-led build state machine at `.impeccable/build/state.json`. `start --comp <png> | --direction <key> [--decision-comp <png>]` (opens the phases; reads comp dimensions for the breakpoint), `status [--json]`, `advance [--force --reason "…"]` (runs the current phase's gate; exit 2 on gate failure, state unchanged), `record hero --build <png>`, `scaffold`, `note "<text>"`, `finish --disposition ship|fix|rebuild|recapture`, `restore-comp`. Phases and gates (`comps`, `spec`, `plates`, `hero`, `sections`, `motion`, `responsive`, `review`) are unchanged from the JS; the hero/responsive gates call comp-diff in-process (the JS spawned it). The organic-clip-path CSS scan is the engine's own rule (`organic-clip-path`), injected into the gate; The spec gate (re-run by hero and responsive) also refuses unknown kinds, noteless bands, sub-pixel regions, and a band-only map. `--force` is refused unless `--reason` quotes the user downgrading the comp (the JS `forceAllowed` logic verbatim). `start --direction --kind` prints `choice ping skipped` and sends nothing; the choice ping is `concept-seed --kind`.

  **The chosen decision comp is option one (engine addition).** `start --direction <key> --decision-comp <png>` records the direction round's chosen comp as `decisionComp` in the state (exit 1 with `build-phase: --decision-comp names option one of a comp round, so it goes with --direction and never with --comp` when combined with `--comp` or given without `--direction`, and `build-phase: decision comp <png> does not exist` when the file is missing). When the recorded file goes missing, the comps gate fails first with `the chosen decision comp <png> is missing: it is option one of this comp round and is never regenerated or replaced. Restore the file the user chose at that path (with its prompt sidecar), then advance again.`, so three other comps cannot close the round without it. While it exists the gate counts it first, wherever it sits (normally `.impeccable/mocks/decision/`), ahead of the comps directly in `.impeccable/mocks/`; no other file under `.impeccable/mocks/decision/` ever counts, approved sidecar or not. Its count reason reads `<n> comp[s] (the chosen decision comp <png> as option one, the others directly under .impeccable/mocks); ...` instead of `<n> comp[s] under .impeccable/mocks; ...`, and the comps NEXT line names it as option one, kept where it is and never regenerated, with two more comps to generate. When its sidecar carries `"approved": true` the gate closes on it like any approved comp: `state.comp` becomes the decision path, and the approved-comp record, kept copy, integrity checks and `restore-comp` below all bind to that path.

  `build-phase check-plate <region-id> --candidate <png> [--json]` runs the normal plate gate on a separate candidate, retaining the full spec for reference exclusions. Exit 0 means pass, 2 means gate failure, 1 means invalid input. It does not write files (apart from the approved-comp copy a build that predates it gets on its first check), select an asset, record an approval, or advance build state. JSON includes `ok`, `region`, `candidate`, `reasons`, `plates`, and `stateChanged: false`.

  **The approved comp is a fixed reference (engine addition).** When a comp is approved (`build-phase start --comp <png>`, the comps gate closing on the approved sidecar, or, for a build that has neither, the first `comp-spec --regions` on the comp `state.json` names), the engine copies its bytes to `.impeccable/build/approved-comp.<ext>` and writes `.impeccable/build/approved-comp.json` (`comp`, `pixelSha256`, `fileSha256`, `copy`, `at`). `pixelSha256` is the identity `comp-spec` writes as `compSha256` (SHA-256 of width, height and decoded RGBA, so a metadata-only rewrite such as `embed-prompt` is not an edit); it is decoded from the file's own bytes, and `comp-spec` measures and hashes that same decode (`png_io::decode_source`: PNG, WebP, JPEG or GIF from the file's own bytes; `load_raster` also decodes non-PNG sources directly on every read, so a stale conversion can never stand in for an edited source). Before anything measures against the comp, every entry point compares the comp's current pixels with that record (or, for a build that predates the record, with the spec's `compSha256`; the build's own spec then keeps the copy when they agree and no record exists, a spec at another path never does): the spec, plates, hero, sections, motion and responsive gates (so `advance`, `record hero`, `check-plate`, and the hero, responsive and plate revalidations), `finish --disposition ship`, `comp-spec --crop`, `comp-spec --comp <build comp> --regions` while a build state names that comp, `font-match --measure` / `--rank`, and `comp-diff` (against the spec's `compSha256` and the record when its `--spec` measures the `--comp` it was given, otherwise against the record kept for that `--comp`, if any). A missing or undecodable comp with a recorded identity is refused the same way, the hash clause reading `is missing or not a decodable image: expected pixel sha256 <approved>`. The build's own spec (`.impeccable/build/spec.json`, which every build-phase check reads, and `comp-spec --crop` / `font-match` / `comp-diff` when they are given that path) must measure the recorded comp: one that measures another file (a composite saved under a new name and re-specced) is refused with `.impeccable/build/spec.json measures <comp>, but the approved comp is <approved>.`, the fixed-reference sentence, and the instruction to re-run `comp-spec --regions` on the approved comp or start a new build on a newly approved file. A spec at another path is checked only against its own comp's record and `compSha256`, so independent crops stay possible, and the first-check keep never replaces a record kept for another comp. For a build that predates the record, `comp-spec --regions` compares the comp with the `compSha256` of the spec it is about to replace and refuses on a mismatch instead of adopting the edited pixels. When the copy or record cannot be written, approval refuses: `start --comp` exits 1 (`build-phase: start refused: cannot keep the approved comp: writing <path> failed (...)`), the comps gate stays open with that reason (also when the approved comp is not a decodable PNG, WebP or JPEG, and also under a quoted `--force`, which keeps the copy before closing; a quoted `--force` never waives this reason, and `advance` adds `--force refused: a quoted downgrade can relax the comp round, but never close it on an approved comp the engine cannot keep as the fixed reference; fix the reason above and advance again`), and `comp-spec --regions` exits 2. A mismatch is refused before any measurement (gate reason, or stderr with exit 2 for the verbs) with: `the approved comp <comp> has changed since approval: expected pixel sha256 <approved>, found <current>. The approved comp is the fixed reference every gate measures against; never edit it, composite into it, or regenerate it. Nothing was measured against the changed file. Restore it with <IMPECCABLE> build-phase restore-comp (the engine kept the approved copy at <copy>), then regenerate the plates or change the page code until they match the unchanged comp.` When no intact copy exists the last two sentences read `No intact engine copy exists for this build: restore the approved file from its original source, then regenerate the plates or change the page code until they match the unchanged comp.` A spec whose `compSha256` differs from the record (re-measured on an edited comp) is refused with `.impeccable/build/spec.json was measured on an image that is not the approved comp (spec compSha256 <spec>, approved <approved>): re-measuring an edited comp does not move the reference.` followed by the fixed-reference sentence and the instruction to re-run `comp-spec --regions` on the approved comp. `build-phase start --reset --comp <png>` naming the recorded comp while its pixels differ is refused (exit 2, `build-phase: start refused: ...`), adding that a newly approved comp is saved under a new file name; `start --direction` forgets the record, since its comp round approves a new comp. `build-phase restore-comp` (no state needed) verifies the copy against `pixelSha256`, moves the edited file to `.impeccable/build/edited-comp-<first 12 hex of its pixel hash>.<ext>` (outside `.impeccable/mocks/`, where the comps gate would count it), writes the copy back, and prints `RESTORED <comp> from <copy> (pixel sha256 <hash>). The edited file is kept at <path> as evidence, not as a comp. Re-run the step that refused; plates and page code built against the edited comp now have to match the approved one.` (exit 0; `comp <comp> already holds the approved pixels (sha256 <hash>); nothing restored` when nothing changed). It exits 1 when no record exists and 2 when the copy no longer matches the record. Gates measure against the editable path once it holds the approved pixels again, rather than silently against the copy, because the crop and font tools, `comp-diff` and the finish reviewer all read that path.

  `build-phase finish --disposition ship` binds the finish to the page it checked. Under native capture (`capturePolicy: "native-html-v1"` or `IMPECCABLE_NATIVE_CAPTURE=1`) `advance` (hero and responsive) and `finish --disposition ship` take `--artifact <entry>` to name the entry that call captures, without rewriting the entry recorded at `start`; ship first re-runs the responsive gate with a fresh native capture of the current files (an input that changes while that capture runs fails it, reopens responsive and records `fix`), then records `finish.artifactSha256` and `finish.captureInputs`, the `{path, sha256}` rows of the manifest that capture bound (`.impeccable/review/native/responsive/inputs.json`: the entry, the static files the capture froze, the spec and the comp). When that manifest is missing or unreadable, ship is refused (exit 2, `finish` recorded as `fix` with reason `final native capture manifest unavailable`) rather than recorded with entry-only binding. Under the screenshot policy ship refuses while the frontend files differ from the responsive screenshots and records `finish.artifactInputsSha256`; no `captureInputs` is recorded. `build-phase completion` reports `status: "changed-after-finish"` when the entry or any recorded capture input differs from what finish bound (a deleted file counts as changed, the entry included, so a deleted entry reads `changed-after-finish` rather than `unverified`), and lists the changed paths, entry first, in `changedSinceFinish`. Printed `NEXT` lines: in the review phase, `Spawn the finish reviewer ... --disposition <word>. Make every fix before ship: <recheck>, and any edit after it, a fix for a hook finding included, needs ship recorded again.`; after a ship that still covers the page, `Finish is recorded for the current entry. Any later edit to a file the final check bound (the page, what it loads, the spec, the comp), a fix for a hook finding included, voids it: run <IMPECCABLE> build-phase finish --disposition ship again before you stop; <recheck>.`; after one that no longer does, `A file the final check bound changed after finish (<up to six changed paths>), so the recorded ship no longer covers the build. Repeat final review if the change goes beyond a fix it asked for, then run <IMPECCABLE> build-phase finish --disposition ship again; <recheck>.` `<recheck>` is `ship re-captures the current files natively and must pass the responsive gate again` under native capture and `ship refuses while the frontend files differ from the responsive screenshots, so recapture desktop and mobile and advance responsive first` otherwise.

---

## 2. Context and utility verbs

Source of truth: `/Users/paulbakaus/code/impeccable-second/skill/scripts/*.mjs` and `skill/scripts/lib/*.mjs` at commit f88b2837 (2026-08-17). All paths below are relative to `skill/scripts/` unless absolute. "cwd" is the process working directory (the user's project; the skill text says "keep cwd at the user's project"). Every script is invoked as `node <scripts_path>/<script>.mjs ...`, where `<scripts_path>` is `<configDir>/skills/impeccable/scripts` per provider (`.claude`, `.cursor`, `.gemini`, `.codex`, `.agents`, `.github`, `.kiro`, `.opencode`, `.pi`, `.qoder`, `.trae`, `.trae-cn`, `.rovodev`, `.vibe`, `.grok`, `.agent` (antigravity), `.hermes`), or the runtime-reported skill base dir + `/scripts`. There is no `impeccable <verb>` mapping in the npm CLI today (`cli/bin/cli.js` only knows `detect`, `ignores`/`ignore`, `skills`, `help`, `install`, `link`, `update`, `check`); the `-> impeccable <verb>` names in headings are the proposed verbs for a reimplementation.

---

### Shared helpers

#### Provider constants (`lib/provider.mjs`)
```js
export const IMPECCABLE_COMMAND_PREFIX = '/'; // @impeccable-provider-command-prefix
export const IMPECCABLE_PROVIDER_ID = 'source'; // @impeccable-provider-id
export const IMPECCABLE_COMMAND = `${IMPECCABLE_COMMAND_PREFIX}impeccable`;
```
The build (`scripts/lib/utils.js` `replaceScriptProviderMarker`) rewrites exactly those two lines per provider: prefix becomes `"$"` for provider `codex`, `"/"` for every other provider; the provider ID becomes the build provider name (`claude-code`, `cursor`, `gemini`, `codex`, `agents`, `github`, `kiro`, `opencode`, `pi`, `qoder`, `trae`, `trae-cn`, `rovo-dev`, `vibe`, `grok`, `antigravity`, `hermes`). In the source checkout it stays `'source'`. `IMPECCABLE_COMMAND` therefore is `/impeccable` or `$impeccable` and appears verbatim in several stdout messages.

#### `--target` parsing (`lib/target-args.mjs`)
`parseTargetPath(args, { strict })` scans left to right; **last occurrence wins**:
- `--target <v>` or `-t <v>`: consumes next arg only if it exists and does not start with `-`. Otherwise: strict -> throw `TargetArgError` (name `'TargetArgError'`, code `'TARGET_VALUE_MISSING'`, message `--target requires a path value.`); non-strict -> ignore.
- `--target=<v>`: value after `=`; empty value: strict -> same throw, non-strict -> ignore.
- Any other arg ignored (so `--help` after `--target` is NOT consumed as a value: `--target --help` throws in strict mode).
`parseTargetOptions` returns `{ targetPath }` when found, else `{}`. `hasTargetOption(o)` = `typeof o.targetPath === 'string' && o.targetPath.trim()`.

#### Slugs (`lib/target-slug.mjs`)
`slugFromTarget(resolved, { cwd })`:
- non-string / empty after trim -> `null`.
- URL (`/^https?:\/\//i`): `new URL(...)`; invalid -> `null`; slug = `kebab(hostname + pathname)` (port, query, hash dropped; hostname is lowercased by URL).
- Else path: abs = absolute or `path.resolve(cwd, trimmed)`; rel = `path.relative(cwd, abs)`; if rel starts with `..` or is absolute -> rel = basename(abs); if rel is `''` or `'.'` -> `null`; slug = `kebab(rel)`.
`kebab(v)`: lowercase; replace runs of `/`, `\`, `.` (`/[/\\.]+/g`) with `-`; replace `/[^a-z0-9-]+/g` with `-`; collapse `/-+/g` -> `-`; strip leading/trailing `-`; empty -> `null`. Values up to 50 characters remain unchanged. Longer normalized values keep their last 41 characters (stripping one leading `-`) and append `-` plus the first 8 lowercase hex characters of the SHA-256 digest of the full normalized value.
Examples: `site/pages/index.astro` -> `site-pages-index-astro`; `http://localhost:3000/pricing` -> `localhost-pricing`; `https://Impeccable.Style/docs/audit/` -> `impeccable-style-docs-audit`.

#### `.impeccable/` path resolution (`lib/impeccable-paths.mjs`)
All helpers take `(cwd, options)` and go through `resolveProjectRoot(cwd, options)` from `context.mjs` (see below): `getImpeccableDir` = `<projectRoot>/.impeccable`; `getCritiqueDir` = `<projectRoot>/.impeccable/critique`; `getDesignSidecarPath` = `.impeccable/design.json`; live paths `.impeccable/live/{config.json,server.json,sessions,annotations}`; legacy `<projectRoot>/.impeccable-live.json`, `<projectRoot>/.impeccable-live/{sessions,annotations}`. `designSidecarCandidatesFor(projectRoot, contextDir)` (in `lib/staleness.mjs`) = `[<projectRoot>/.impeccable/design.json, <projectRoot>/DESIGN.json, <contextDir>/DESIGN.json (only if different from the second)]`, canonical first. `safeSessionId` requires `/^[A-Za-z0-9_-]{1,128}$/`.

#### Project / repo root resolution (`context.mjs`, exported `resolveProjectRoot`, `resolveTargetSelection`, `loadContext`, `resolveContextDir`)
Constants:
```
PRODUCT_NAMES = ['PRODUCT.md','Product.md','product.md']   (checked in this order, first existing wins)
DESIGN_NAMES  = ['DESIGN.md','Design.md','design.md']
FALLBACK_DIRS = ['.agents/context','docs']
MONOREPO_MARKER_FILES = ['pnpm-workspace.yaml','turbo.json','nx.json','lerna.json']
MONOREPO_FALLBACK_PROJECT_DIRS = ['apps','packages']
WORKSPACE_DISCOVERY_IGNORED_DIRS = {node_modules,.git,dist,build,.next,.nuxt,.svelte-kit,.turbo,.cache,coverage,vendor,vendors}; plus any name starting with '.'
```
`resolveTargetDir(cwd, {targetPath})`: no/blank target -> cwd. abs = absolute or resolve(cwd, target). If it stats: dir -> itself, file -> dirname. If stat fails: has extension -> dirname, else itself.

`findMonorepoRoot(startDir)`: walk up from startDir; stop (return null) when dir === `os.homedir()`; return dir if `isMonorepoRoot(dir)`; else if `<dir>/.git` exists -> null; else parent; filesystem root -> null. `isMonorepoRoot(dir)` = any positive (non-`!`) pattern in `readProjectPatterns(dir)` OR (a marker file exists AND `apps/` or `packages/` has a non-ignored subdirectory). Note: `turbo.json` alone with no apps/packages children is not a monorepo.

`readProjectPatternGroups(repoRoot)` = `[impeccablePatterns, packagePatterns]`:
- impeccable: `.impeccable/config.json` then `.impeccable/config.local.json`, `projectRoots` array of non-blank strings (trimmed), concatenated.
- package: `package.json` `workspaces` (array, or `workspaces.packages`), then `pnpm-workspace.yaml` `packages:` (block list `- x` items and flow `packages: [a, b]`; inline `#` comments stripped outside quotes; a new top-level `key:` ends the block), then `lerna.json` `packages`.
Pattern normalization: trim, strip surrounding quotes, strip leading `./`, strip trailing `/`s. `!` prefix = negation. Segment match: `*` matches any; segments without `*` compare equal; otherwise regex from escaped segment with `\*` -> `[^/]*`.

`resolveProject(cwd, options)`: targetDir as above; repoRoot = findMonorepoRoot(targetDir); if none and targetDir !== cwd, try findMonorepoRoot(cwd) and accept if targetDir is inside it. Not monorepo -> `{ projectRoot: nearestTargetContextRoot(cwd, targetDir) || cwd, repoRoot: cwd, isMonorepo: false }`. `nearestTargetContextRoot`: only when targetDir strictly inside cwd; walk from targetDir up to (excluding) cwd; return first dir that is not `<cwd>/.agents/context` or `<cwd>/docs` and has a context file (root or fallback dirs). Monorepo -> `{ projectRoot: resolveWorkspaceProjectRoot(repoRoot, targetDir) || repoRoot, repoRoot, isMonorepo: true }`. `resolveWorkspaceProjectRoot`: rel of targetDir under repoRoot (outside -> repoRoot); for each group in order: negation match -> repoRoot; positive pattern match -> `<repoRoot>/<first N segments>` (N = pattern segment count) or, for `**` patterns, nearest dir with package.json between target and the literal-prefix dir, else prefix+1 segment; then fallback `apps/<x>` or `packages/<x>` if rel starts with those; then nearest dir up to repoRoot with a context file or package.json; else repoRoot.

`resolveLocalContextDir(root)`: root if it has any PRODUCT/DESIGN name; else first of `.agents/context`, `docs` that does. `resolveContext`: projectContextDir = resolveLocalContextDir(projectRoot); rootContextDir = same for repoRoot only when repoRoot !== projectRoot. productPath = first existing product name in projectContextDir, else in rootContextDir (per-file inheritance; design likewise). If neither found: `IMPECCABLE_CONTEXT_DIR` (trimmed; absolute or resolved against cwd) is searched for both. `contextDir` = dirname(productPath) || dirname(designPath) || envContextDir || projectRoot.

`discoverTargetCandidates(repoRoot)`: expand every pattern of both groups (`**` -> walk, collecting dirs that look like project roots: have package.json, a context file, or `src`/`app`/`pages`/`public`; if none, direct children of the literal-prefix base); plus, when a marker file exists, direct children of `apps/` and `packages/`; dedupe by posix rel path; drop `..`; keep only selectable (not negated by impeccable patterns; if an impeccable positive pattern gives a boundary, keep only if boundary === candidate; else not negated by package patterns); sort by `localeCompare`; each -> `{ name: basename, path: rel, targetExample, productStatus, productPath, designStatus, designPath }`. `targetExample` = first existing of `src/App.jsx, src/App.tsx, src/main.jsx, src/main.tsx, src/index.jsx, src/index.ts, app/page.tsx, pages/index.tsx, public/index.html` (repo-relative), else the candidate path. Status values: `'child'` (file directly in candidate root), `'fallback'` (inside candidate but in a subdir, or outside both roots), `'inherited'` (from repo root), `'missing'`. Paths are repo-relative posix or absolute if outside.

`resolveTargetSelection(cwd, options)`: returns null when a target was given, or not monorepo, or projectRoot !== repoRoot, or no candidates; else `{ targetPath: null, projectRoot, repoRoot, targetCandidates, skippedNonUiWorkspaces? }`. Its candidates are `discoverTargetCandidates` narrowed to workspaces with a UI signal (#1014): a candidate found by an impeccable `projectRoots` pattern is always kept; any other (package-workspace glob or `apps/`/`packages/` fallback) is kept only when it shows a UI signal: its `package.json` lists a UI dependency under `dependencies`, `devDependencies` or `peerDependencies` (exact: `react react-dom react-native @react-native/metro-config expo next gatsby @remix-run/react react-router vue nuxt svelte @sveltejs/kit solid-js @solidjs/start preact lit astro @angular/core @builder.io/qwik ember-source alpinejs htmx.org vite storybook @11ty/eleventy @docusaurus/core vitepress electron @tauri-apps/api @ionic/core @stencil/core`; prefixes `@storybook/ @remix-run/ @react-router/`); or it has its own PRODUCT/DESIGN file; or one of `index.html public/index.html src/index.html ios/Podfile ios/Runner.xcodeproj android/build.gradle android/build.gradle.kts app/src/main/AndroidManifest.xml src/main/AndroidManifest.xml angular.json` exists; or its `pubspec.yaml` is a Flutter one (a line `sdk: flutter`, or a bare `flutter:` key; a pure Dart package does not count); or a one-level listing (no recursion) of the candidate root or its `src app pages components public static styles templates` dirs holds a file with a UI or style extension (`.html .htm .jsx .tsx .vue .svelte .astro .css .scss .sass .less .styl`, `*.min.*` excluded), or the root holds a `*.xcodeproj`/`*.xcworkspace` dir. Checked in that order; no check walks a tree, so the boot cost stays at reads, stats and single listings per discovered candidate. `skippedNonUiWorkspaces` = the number dropped, present only when > 0. If the filter would leave no candidate, the unfiltered list is used and nothing is counted as skipped. `doctor` sweeps the unfiltered list, and bare-name `--target` resolution (`findUniqueBareTarget`) still matches every candidate, so a hidden workspace stays reachable by name.

`loadContext(cwd, options)` returns:
```
{ hasProduct, product, productPath (relative to cwd or null), hasDesign, design, designPath, contextDir,
  productContextDir, designContextDir, hasSurfaceBrief, surfaceBrief (text|null), surfaceBriefPath (rel|null),
  surfaceBriefReason, surfaceBriefCandidates: [{slug, path(rel), primaryTarget, relatedTargets}],
  hasVisualImplementation, platform, projectRoot, repoRoot, isMonorepo }
```
Surface brief resolution uses `resolveSurfaceBrief(projectRoot, targetPath-or-null)`.

#### `extractSectionValue(product, heading)` / `extractPlatform`
Heading regex `new RegExp('^##\\s+' + escapeRegExp(heading) + '\\s*$', 'i')` tested against each trimmed line. On match, return the first following non-empty trimmed line; if a heading line (`/^#{1,6}\s/`) comes first, return null. Absent -> null. `extractPlatform`: value lowercased; exact `web|ios|android|adaptive` returned; else split on `/[\s,+&/]+/`, drop empty and `and`; if >=2 tokens, all in {ios, android}, and both present -> `'adaptive'`; else null. Missing section -> null (treated as web downstream).

#### `hasVisualImplementation(projectRoot)`
Scans root-level files, then BFS over `src, app, pages, components, site, public, styles` to depth 4 (skipping dot dirs and WORKSPACE_DISCOVERY_IGNORED_DIRS), at most 250 inspected files. Only `.css .scss .sass .less .styl` and `.html .htm .jsx .tsx .vue .svelte .astro`; skips `*.min.*`. Reads first 64KiB, strips `/* */`, `<!-- -->`, and full-line `//` comments. Style file: name matches `\b(tokens?|theme|design-system)\b` and >80 chars -> true; >=3 `--x:` custom props or >=5 `color|background(-color)|border(-color)|font-family:` declarations -> true. HTML: length > 600 and `<style` or `<link ... stylesheet` -> true. Non-HTML UI file length > 300: (>=3 custom props AND >=3 visual decls) OR >=5 visual decls OR >=12 class tokens in `class=`/`className=` -> true; if it matches `class(Name)?=|style=|styled(|css\`` count as styled component; 3 styled components -> true. Otherwise false.

#### Surface briefs (`lib/surface-briefs.mjs`)
Dir: `<projectRoot>/.impeccable/surfaces/`. `normalizeSurfaceTarget(target, {projectRoot})`:
- URL: strip hash and search, strip one trailing `/` (`https://a.b/x/` -> `https://a.b/x`; origin-only stays e.g. `https://a.b`).
- `route:<r>` (case-insens.) -> `normalizeRouteTarget(r)`: must start with `/` and not contain `..` else null; cut at `?`/`#`, collapse `//`, strip trailing `/` (root stays `/`) -> `route:/docs/intro`.
- `/` alone -> `route:/`.
- Starts with `/` and (not inside projectRoot and does not exist on disk) -> route.
- Else project-relative posix path; outside project or `.` -> null.
Brief file: `<dir>/<slug>.md` where slug = `slugFromTarget(normalized with 'route:' -> 'route', {cwd: projectRoot})` (so `route:/pricing` -> `route-pricing`, `route:/` -> `route`).
`parseSurfaceBrief`: frontmatter `/^---\r?\n([\s\S]*?)\r?\n---(?:\r?\n|$)/`; each `key: value` line; value JSON-parsed if it starts with `[`, `{`, `"` or is `true|false|null|number`, else string with surrounding quotes stripped. Fields: `slug` (meta.slug or file basename), `primaryTarget` (meta.primary_target string), `relatedTargets` (string array), `targets`, `body`, `text`, `path`.
`listSurfaceBriefs`: `*.md` sorted by name. `resolveSurfaceBrief(projectRoot, target)`: no target -> `{brief: only one? it : null, candidates: all, reason: 'only-brief'|'ambiguous'|'none'}`; invalid target -> `reason 'invalid-target'`; exact slug path match whose targets are empty or include normalized -> `'slug'`; else briefs whose targets include normalized: 1 -> `'mapping'`, >1 -> `'ambiguous-target'` (candidates = those), 0 -> `'not-found'` (candidates = all).
`writeSurfaceBrief`: writes
```
---
version: 1
slug: "<slug>"
primary_target: "<normalized>"
related_targets: ["<n1>",...]   (deduped, primary removed, JSON array)
---

<body.trim()>
```
followed by a single `\n`. Overwrites (one brief per slug).

#### Artifact schema (`lib/artifact-schema.mjs`)
`PRODUCT_SCHEMA_VERSION = 1`, `DESIGN_SIDECAR_SCHEMA_VERSION = 2`, `PRODUCT_V4_SECTIONS = ['Positioning','Operating Context','Evidence on Hand','Product Principles']`, `PRODUCT_DEPRECATED_SECTIONS = { Register: 'v4 replaced the brand/product register axis with the four visitor modes (Persuade, Operate, Read, Experience), which are chosen per surface and persisted in that surface\'s brief. Nothing reads `## Register` any more.' }`. Stamp regex `/^[ \t]*<!--[ \t]*impeccable:product-schema[ \t]+(\d+)[ \t]*-->[ \t]*$/im`; stamp line `<!-- impeccable:product-schema N -->`. `stampProductSchema`: existing stamp replaced in place; else inserted as `''` + stamp after the first `/^#\s+\S/` line; no heading -> `stamp + '\n\n' + body with leading newlines stripped`. `readSidecarSchemaVersion` -> integer or null.

#### Template extensions (`lib/template-extensions.mjs`)
