Its page gets the same guarantee: the spec and comp are frozen and hashed with the other inputs (and re-verified, so editing the comp invalidates the capture) but are never served, so the input manifest marks them `"served": false` and `inputs.json` lists what the page may load as `servedToPage`. The page gets the static inventory minus those two files. Before the browser opens, a raster region whose `plate` is the comp's path, or whose plate file is a byte copy of the comp or of the approved review screenshot, is refused (`raster region <id> names the approved comp (<path>) as its plate` or `raster region <id>'s plate <path> is a copy of the approved reference`). After each frame the capture reads every path the page asked the snapshot server for, served or not. A request for the comp or the spec refuses the frame with `<frame> raster capture refused: the page loads the approved comp (<path>). Show the declared plates and draw the rest of the first viewport in code.`, and a loaded file that is a byte copy of the comp or of the approved screenshot, or an HTML, CSS, JS or SVG file that inlines either as a base64 data URI (whitespace ignored), refuses it with `the page loads <path>, which is a copy of the approved reference` (or `embeds the approved reference as a data URI`). As on the text-only path, a copy the page never loads does not block it. Byte checks cannot see a re-encoded copy, so each frame also measures the area images paint with the text-only measurement, leaving out what the spec declares: in `hero` and `desktop`, the grid cells inside every raster region's box (comp pixels scaled to the frame width); in `mobile`, which reflows away from the comp's positions, every image drawn only from a declared plate, wherever it lands and whatever surface paints it (an `img`, a background or a `::before`/`::after`), matched by path with any query string or fragment ignored, as the snapshot server ignores them. The mobile frame is not compared with the comp, so this exclusion cannot help a page pass a comparison. When images paint 15% or more of the frame outside those exclusions, the capture is refused with `<frame> raster capture refused: images outside the declared raster regions cover <n>% of the viewport (limit 15%)`, the largest images, and an instruction to declare the image as a raster region (`comp-spec --regions`) or draw that area in code. The measurement is taken after the raster observations, on the restored page, and is kept only when the viewport shows exactly the graded frame's pixels before and after it (otherwise the frame is refused as unmeasured). It is recorded per frame as `frameProofs.<frame>.rasterCoverage` (with the exclusions under `excluded`), not in the region receipts. The report's `nativeCapture.integrityScope` reads `rendered presence and minimum frame placement; the comp and approved screenshots are never served to the page; existing visual scores unchanged`, and the capture's own `inputs.integrityScope` states both guarantees.

Through the host capture service (`IMPECCABLE_CAPTURE_PORT` and `IMPECCABLE_CAPTURE_CAPABILITY`), the same capture crosses unchanged: the service returns each frame's receipts as `regions`, and the report with every field above plus `captureService`. The client reads the bound spec from the project, checks its SHA-256 against the spec entry in the capture's input manifest (a spec edited since the capture is refused), and lets the spec decide the kind. A spec with no `raster` region requires `"captureMethod": "assembled-page-viewport"`, an empty `regions` list in every frame, a `frameProofs` entry per frame whose `rasterCoverage.share` is under the 15% limit, a `dependencyPolicy` of `hero-review-manifest` or `static-inventory`, and a `servedToPage` list that names neither the spec nor the comp. Either kind needs a `servedToPage` list that names neither the spec nor the comp, an input manifest that does not mark either as served, and a `frameProofs` entry per frame whose `rasterCoverage.share` is under the 15% limit. A spec with raster regions requires any other capture method and, in each frame, exactly one receipt per raster region the in-process capture measures there (every raster region in `hero` and `desktop`, the first one in `mobile`), at most 32. 32 is the native batch bound: the in-process capture refuses a frame with more raster regions, so the service can never return more. Anything else, including a raster capture with an empty `regions` list or a text-only method claimed for a raster spec, fails with `native entry capture unavailable: invalid native capture regions` (or names the mismatched method) and the capture is released. The service's `/audit` endpoint compares the saved `<frame>-observations.json` (`[]` for a text-only frame), frame PNGs, `inputs.json` and `human-approved.png` against its own capture as for raster captures.

For native comp-led runs, a verified assembled-page review can resolve a text-style contradiction against the original comp. The renderer reads the private native review session, binds the approved screenshot (verified receipt, capture digest and blob hash) and the comp bytes, and compares the disputed text region against that human-approved rendering at the current desktop breakpoint. The original comp scores remain in the report; accepted region IDs and the review revision are recorded separately under `humanTextReview`.

The approval is bound visually, not by page bytes: later edits to the entry, its stylesheets or its assets keep it, and every gate compares the approved screenshot with the current capture instead. It never waives missing regions, raster production/placement, or native capture checks. A changed comp, a stale receipt, a partial component capture, or a changed approved image cannot provide this evidence. A host capture service may select its private native review session with `IMPECCABLE_CAPTURE_REVIEW_SESSION` at service startup; this is not a caller-supplied capture parameter. Standalone native builds use the matching local component-review session.


### Responsive gate: displaced regions, crops and escalation

The responsive gate compares the desktop first viewport with the comp: the native desktop frame (1440 wide at the comp's aspect, 1440x960 for a 1536x1024 comp), or the top of `.impeccable/review/desktop.png` at the comp's aspect, scaled to the comp's width. A region that is not at its box is searched for before it is called missing. Every non-texture region whose verdict there is `missing`, and every `text`, `control` or `chrome` region whose verdict is `contradicted`, is looked for within 12% of the comp's height and 4% of its width of its box (blurred luma at a working scale of at most 96 samples per axis, normalized cross-correlation of at least 0.6 over the part of the region that lands in the capture, at least a third of its rows, then the full comp-diff score of that part reading match or drift). A full-page `desktop.png` is searched below the first viewport too. A match that covers at least half of another region of the same kind and similar size (area within half to double), where that region still reads match or drift at its own box, is that region's own pixels and is discarded, so a missing control never borrows an identical neighbour. When the region's own pixels left the frame (a native frame holds the first viewport alone), the content directly above it in its column (three region heights or 8% of the comp, whichever is taller) is searched downward instead, and the region counts as displaced when that offset leaves less than half of it in the frame. A displaced region that still lies wholly inside the first viewport becomes `drift` with the advisory `(advisory, still inside the first viewport) at desktop width, region <id> sits about <N>px lower than in the comp`. One that runs past the frame becomes verdict `displaced` (`rawVerdict` keeps the measurement) and blocks with `at desktop width, region <id> is displaced, not missing: it renders about <N>px lower than in the comp, and <P>% of it stays inside the <W>x<H> first viewport` (or, read from its column, `the content above it in its column sits about <N>px lower than in the comp, which pushes <id> out of the <W>x<H> first viewport`), followed by the remedy (size what sits above it in proportion to the viewport, not with fixed px or a px floor). Offsets are in the capture's own pixels and recorded as the region's `displacement` (`dx`, `dy`, `visible`, `inferred`). A region found nowhere stays `at desktop width, region <id> is missing`. A plate whose current receipt passed and that renders at desktop width (detail at least 30%) is never missing there: at structure 50% or more it is drift as before; below, it is located like any region, displaced past the frame blocks, and otherwise it is drift with an advisory that it renders away from its comp box.

A failed responsive gate sets the same repair crops as the hero: the regions with blocking reasons, lowest score first, at most three, so `advance` prints the `LOOK FIRST` list (`.impeccable/review/diff/desktop/regions/<id>.png` with verdict and scores) and the remedy paragraph, plus one line for a `displaced` crop. Each `advance` from the responsive phase appends `{at, score, worstIds, blockingReasons}` to `phases.responsive.history` (last six kept). After three failed attempts in a row the refusal leads with `The responsive gate has failed 3 attempts in a row.` and the route: with no first viewport accepted, present the first-viewport review (stage hero) with the current page; with one accepted (locally or in a session the host names), show the user the desktop side-by-side with the blocking regions and ask them to choose between restoring them and keeping the desktop first viewport; keeping it downgrades the comp at desktop width and is recorded only by quoting their words saying so in `advance --force --reason` (the refusal gives an example `force` accepts). Both say the phase stays open and that a finish recorded over it is an unfinished build, not a fix round.

The responsive `NEXT` line names what the gate diffs: under native capture, the gate's own desktop first viewport at 1440 wide (the frame size computed from the breakpoint) and a 390-wide mobile frame; otherwise the first viewport of `desktop.png` at that size.

### Visual approval carry-forward

Native component review carries a submitted approval when all displayed image
bytes and review scope are identical, even if shared source files changed.
The comparison includes the approved comp, region, representation, declared
component definition, and native capture geometry. Changed or unverified views
remain unapproved. Source revisions and integrity validation remain independent;
carrying a visual decision never creates a submitted receipt or accepts stale files.

`component-review refresh-approvals --session <id> --store <directory>` applies
this rule to an existing unsubmitted draft. It verifies stored image blobs and
current source integrity, preserves any new reviewer decisions, records the
prior approval revision, and leaves packet revisions and old receipts unchanged.
